- Files: new `src/js/17-wear.js` (helpers, `wearOf`, `wearPic`, `wearBox`, `wearEmb`, `wearName`), new `src/data/d-wear.js` (`WEAR`, `WEAR_EMB`, `WEAR_NAME`, 19 KB, in the data file to keep index.html under its warning), new `src/css/43-shop.css`; edits: `src/js/48-shop.js` (shop rewritten), `46-home.js` (`book()` only), `17-pets-svg.js` (anchors, `petRigHTML`, header comment), `18-reward-art.js` (`RW.pet`, `RW.badge`), `21-art.css` (selector), `30-learn.css` and `40-games.css` (dead rules removed), tools (test, smoke, shots), docs (DESIGN_SYSTEM 6.4, CONTRIBUTING 3.2 row), `tools/build.py` VER.
- Tests: test.js new check "wearables and shop P4-07" (every SHOP hat / toy and BPRIZE item has a drawing and an emblem for prizes; elements g/path/circle/ellipse/rect only, M L C Q Z paths, no ids, defs, gradients, masks, filters, use, text, style, url, raw colour; colours only `--a-*` roles mapped to `--ill-*`; one-decimal coordinates; under 4,000 bytes and 70 shapes; at least one colour area with three tones (not flat); hats sit on (32, 60), toys hold (32, 32); every item on every pet x 5 stages inside the viewBox, in the head / right-arm group, deterministic; an unknown id keeps its emoji; shop (3 tabs and a try-on) and book rendered for all 6 fixtures: no emoji chrome, ids kept, owned / worn items load unchanged and show as Yours / On, the pet on Home / Pet Care / rewards wears the drawn hat and toy, the stage is the pet's place, the book shows the save's stickers; shop / book source has no emoji; 43-shop.css tokens only, no filter, transform/opacity transitions). Shown to fail on an injected raw hex in a hat and an emoji in the shop title. smoke.js block `// P4-07` (380x780 and 1024x600, midway): Home -> Pet Shop -> try on -> Buy -> Yes for 4 items on the 4 pets (cap on the dragon, teddy on the dino, helmet on the bird, ball on the sea pal): coins, owned, on, the coin chip; the drawn item on the stage pet, on Home and in Pet Care; the second pet wears both; not enough coins opens no sheet; the sticker book (4 pages, the save's stickers, die-cut shadow, Puzzle badges opens); 48 px targets, no sideways scroll, no emoji chrome on every screen.
- Test expectations changed: smoke `// P4-05` (two steps) reads the worn hat / toy through the drawing instead of the emoji text, same items expected; test.js "pet rig (P4-05)" expected the midway child's petHTML to hold the emoji `>🎩</text>` and `>🎈</text>`; it now expects the drawn top hat and balloon (`WEAR['🎩']`, `WEAR['🎈']`) and no `<text>` (that is the point of this chunk). The P4-05 / P4-06 checks that call petSVG with emoji wearables directly are unchanged (the emoji fallback still works).
- check.sh: check OK (contrast 92 pairs, test.js 105 of 105, smoke OK with no console errors and no network). The first full run failed three old smoke steps, fixed here: P4-05 "home pet" and "other pets / setup" read the hat and toy as emoji text (now `wornId` compares the drawn `.pw-hat` / `.pw-toy` group with `WEAR[id]` and still expects the top hat and balloon), and P4-03 "shop" found two 0 px crown icons: the Prize tag's class `prize` picked up the old reward `.prize` spin animation (30-learn.css), so it is `ps-prize` now. ### P3-17 Puzzle of the Day and badges (v120, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 9, 7, 2, 3): the Puzzle of the Day gets its small card on Home, badges arrive (store, drawn medals, a badges view from the hub and as a page of the sticker book, a reveal), the "left the same level 3 times, offer the band below" rule is built, and the play top bar shows every type's whole name, so four types get their catalog names back. All framework code is in `src/js/74-teasers.js` and `src/css/48-teasers.css` (index.html); Home in `46-home.js` / `46-home.css`. Inline code calls part code only from handlers, as before.
- Puzzle of the Day: `BT.pickFor(day)` picks one of `BT.offered()` (the types of the child's band; the hub hides the others, so these are the ones open to the child) by a hash of the date alone, at the child's next level in it (`nowLevel`). `dailyInfo()` stores it in `S.bt.daily.pick` and keeps it all day (a type registered later, or progress, does not move it); it is picked again only if the stored pick is damaged or the child's band changed before it was done. `done` now means "a daily was solved today" (one paid daily a day, also across a grade change). Pay as before (P3-01, section 9 table): the level's own stars and game time, plus 5 coins and 60 s, once a day; a replay that day pays a normal replay. Home: `hmDaily()` under the Today row, a white card with a drawn calendar-and-puzzle-piece (`HM_ART.pod`), "Puzzle of the Day", "<type>, level N" and a "+5" coin pill; green with a tick and "Done! Come back tomorrow." after. Hidden until a type registers (and when no type is offered). Tapping plays it (done: opens the hub). The hub's card also shows the +5 pill and turns green when done.
- Badges (the section 9 list exactly): `first.<type>` first solve of each type; `n10`, `n25`, `n50`, `n100` puzzles solved (every solve counts: replays and Endless too); `day7` the Puzzle of the Day 7 days in a row (a missed day starts the count again; the best is kept); `all.<type>.<band>` all levels of a type in a band. Stored in `S.bt.badges = {got: {id: day}, n, dd: {day, n, best}}`, read with defaults (`BT.bdg()`, nothing written on read; no migration, `LATEST` unchanged). `BT.badgeCheck()` stores each new badge once and never removes one; it runs after every solve (`finish()` returns `badges`) and when the hub opens. A save from before badges: `BT.solved()` takes the larger of the counter and the solved levels plus Endless solves in the records, so older work earns its badges; the hub reveals them once in a sheet ("New badge!", See my badges / OK).
- Badge art: `BT.medal(x, size)`, one medal shape for all (64 x 72 grid, light top left, tokens only): two ribbon tails, a soft drop, a rim, a light face with a white ring and gloss, and the badge's number (counts, 7) or its type's icon (`<use>` of the sprite) in the deep tone. Colour by kind (`BT.MEDAL`): counts gold with blue ribbon, daily orange/purple, first solves purple/yellow, all levels blue/red; not earned yet: pale greys (never a lock, never a loss) with "7 of 10" / "Not yet" under it.
- Badges view `BT.badgeView(back)`: top bar with a medal chip (count earned), "You have N of M badges. Tap a badge to hear what it is for.", sections Puzzles solved (counts and the daily streak), First puzzles, Every level (the bands the child can play in each type, plus any earned). Tiles are buttons: a tap says and toasts what the badge is for. Opened from the hub's new "My badges" card (three newest medals and the count) and from a "Puzzle badges" button in the sticker book (section 9: "a new page" of the book).
- Reveal: the win card shows new badges in a tinted row (medals pop in once with `--spring`, then the names, "New badge!" tag) and says them; calm mode and reduced motion show them still (no animation). On a screen under 641 px tall (1024x600) the row is compact (36 px medals, one line), because the first check run caught P3-14's win card at 1024x600 with Again and Levels pushed 22 px off the bottom by the new badge row.
- Section 7 rule: every exit from an unsolved puzzle (Back, another screen, Start again) counts on its level (`S.bt[type][band].quit = {lv, n}`; another level starts its own count; a solve clears it). The third exit on the same level sets `BT.offer` and the level strip (or the hub, or the play screen after Start again) shows a sheet: "Try an easier one? This level is a tricky one. Want to try level N for grades 1-2? Your stars stay safe." with "Yes, try it" (starts that level, capped to the band's level count, in the band below) and "No, keep trying". Nothing is taken away; the count starts again after an offer. No offer in band A or when the type has no band below. Counting every exit (not once per saved attempt) is a choice: a child who keeps leaving the same saved puzzle is the one who needs it.
- Top bar fix: the name is its own element (`.bt-nm`) and may wrap to two lines instead of being cut; on a phone (max-width 480 px) with the timer chip the type icon steps aside and the title is `--fs-3`. Restored catalog names (BRAIN_TEASERS 4): `pyramid` "Number Pyramid", `target` "Make the Number", `logic` "Logic Grid", `memory` "Memory Match" ("MathDoku" was already the name; it now shows whole). At 380x780 with the timer at 59:59 "Make the Number" takes two lines (the bar grows by a few px for that type only); every other name fits on one.
- Save fields: `S.bt.badges` (above), `S.bt[type][band].quit` (sanitized in `BT.rec`). Both read with defaults; damaged values are repaired on read, never throw.
- Tests: named check "Puzzle of the Day and badges P3-17" (the daily pick over 7 grades x 40 dates: only offered types, the child's band and next level, the same for a date, stable when a type is added or progress moves, re-picked on a grade change before it is done, damaged picks; paid once with 5 coins and 60 s, not twice, not for another puzzle, not again after a grade change, again the next day; each badge awarded exactly once at the right solve (first, 10, 25, all levels, 7 days in a row on a moved clock, a gap), an old save earning its badges, a round trip, eight damaged `S.bt.badges` values; every fixture loads with no badges and nothing but `S.bt.daily` written by reading; Home's card; the quit rule incl. caps, no-lower-band cases, damaged `quit`, `BT.stop` counting and a won puzzle not counting; medal art tokens and well-formedness; the catalog names). Smoke block `// P3-17` (above the final network lines): at 380x780 Home's daily card -> played to a win from Home by real taps, 5 coins and game time paid, stars, the badge revealed on the win card, Again pays no coins and shows no badge, Home and hub show done, the badges view from the hub, a tile tap explains the badge, three exits from level 2 offer the band below and "Yes, try it" opens level 2 in band B; at 1024x600 Home, the one-time reveal for older work, the badges view (5 columns); every registered type's play top bar at 380x780 in bands C and D with the timer at 59:59 (22 bars): the name is the type's name, not clipped, left of the timer, at most two lines, inside the bar, 48 px buttons. 48 px targets, no sideways scroll, no emoji chrome on every screen of the block.
- Test expectations changed: none.
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 101 of 101, smoke OK). index.html 1,175,932 bytes (+23 KB), bt.v120.js 336,033 bytes; total 2,144,345 (over the 2 MB warning as before this chunk).
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-daily-home bt-daily-done bt-daily-hub bt-daily-win bt-daily-sheet bt-daily-offer bt-daily-bar bt-badges`): looked at all eight at 380x780 and home, hub, win and badges at 1024x600.
- Merge: built on branch P3-17-daily from df593b6 (v116). Overlaps are appends: test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), smoke.js (one block before the final network lines), shots.js (one `Object.assign(SCREENS, ...)` before `main`), the type names in 77-, 80-, 82-bt files, this log. VER is provisional.

### P3-16 Spatial: cube count, rotations, symmetry (v119, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.11, 2, 3, 7, 9): three new puzzle types, registered on their own as the catalog lists them, 12 levels per band then the framework's Endless: `cubes` ("Cubes", `src/js/84-bt-cubes.js`, object `CUB`, bands BCD, `allow` 1), `rotate` ("Turn It", `85-bt-rotate.js`, object `ROT`, bands ABCD, `allow` 1), `mirror` ("Mirror", `86-bt-mirror.js`, object `MRR`, bands BCD, `allow` 2; `MIR` is taken by 72-shapes.js), their looks in `src/css/49-bt-cubes.css` (with the talk line the three share), `49-bt-rotate.css`, `49-bt-mirror.css` (all moved into `bt.<VER>.js` by the build), and one drawn icon `cubes` (an isometric block of eight cubes, top in the soft tone) in `15-icons.js`; Turn It uses the existing `rotate` icon, Mirror `flip`. No data file; nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- What each band gets (levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12):
  | Band | Cubes (3 stacks a puzzle) | Turn It | Mirror (one grid) |
  |------|---------------------------|---------|-------------------|
  | A (PK-K) | - | 3 shapes, 3 shadows each: "Which shadow matches?" (spoken); 4 squares, 5 from level 9; not turned on levels 1-6, turned from 7; the wrong shadows are other shapes | - |
  | B (1-2) | 2 x 2 x 1 (2-4 cubes) / 2 x 2 x 2 (3-6) / 2 x 2 x 2 with a hidden cube (5-8) | 4 shapes, 4 choices: the shape turned, three with one square moved; 4 squares, 5 from level 5 | 6 x 6, line down the middle; 3-5 / 5-7 / 7-9 squares to fill |
  | C (3-4) | 2 x 3 x 2 (4-9) / 3 x 3 x 2 (6-13, 1+ hidden) / 3 x 3 x 3 (9-20, 2+ hidden) | chiral shapes of 5, then 6 squares: turned, its mirror image (turned), two with a square moved | 8 x 8: line across (1-4), the slanted corner-to-corner line (5-8), either (9-12); 5-8 / 8-11 / 10-14 |
  | D (5-6) | 3 x 3 x 3 (8-17) / 3 x 4 x 3 (12-24) / 4 x 4 x 4 (16-36, 5+ hidden) | 6, then 7 squares: turned, its mirror image, a moved one and that one's mirror image | half-turn about the centre dot: 6 x 6 (6-9), then 8 x 8 (9-13 / 12-17) |
- Cubes: a stack is a height map (columns on a floor tray, nothing floats). `make()` builds it from the front to the back, giving each column a height that keeps its top face fully in view (or, when empty, at least 40% of its floor), so the drawing shows every column's height: every cube is seen or holds up a column whose top is seen, and the answer is the sum of the heights. Hidden cubes (no face in view) grow with the level. Inline SVG in painter's order (x + y + z), faces against another cube left out: top -1, left base, right -2, edges -3 (`vector-effect: non-scaling-stroke`), on a white tray with grey sides and a grid. The number is typed on the question pad (two rows of six; hardware keys too). A wrong number: the box shakes amber, one mistake, "Not 7. Some cubes are hiding. Count again!" (or "Count each cube just once."), in try-ink. Hints per stack: 1 words (count layer by layer from the bottom; cubes under the ones you see are there too), 2 the stack layer by layer (5.11): each layer drawn on its own, bottom first, in star-yellow cards, 3 each layer's count ("Add the layers: 16 + 10 + 5 + 1."). Win note "Stack 1: 7 cubes" for each. State `{i, miss, h}`.
- Turn It: shapes are polyominoes grown at random in a small box; canonical forms over the 4 turns and over all 8 turns and flips decide "the same shape turned", "its mirror image" and "another shape"; C and D shapes are chiral; a moved-square choice is one piece of the same size and another shape; no two choices look the same; the right answer is always visibly turned (except band A levels 1-6). Every piece of an item has one colour (colour never tells the answer) and the same square size. Pieces are drawn like the pattern tiles (ledge, shade, base toward the top-left light, a short gloss); band A's choices are flat grey shadows. A wrong choice: amber badge, out of play, one mistake; the mirror image says "That one is flipped, like in a mirror. Try another!". Hints per shape: 1 words (C and D: a choice is flipped and no turn makes it), 2 the shape in all four turns beside it (A: one wrong shadow taken away), 3 two wrong choices taken away (A: the last wrong shadow). State `{i, miss, h, x, g}`.
- Mirror: the given half is grown as one piece from a square next to the line (half-turn: next to the centre), so the finished picture is one shape; the answer is its exact mirror image. Empty squares of the given half are lilac outlines, the side to fill is white raised squares, the line is dashed purple (half-turn: a centre dot); on the slanted line the squares it runs through are their own twins (given, not tappable). A tap on a twin fills it (pop); any other square shakes and flashes amber, stays empty, one mistake, "Not that square. Find its twin across the line!". Hints: 1 says how the mirror works and the line glows star-yellow, 2 rings a square of the picture whose twin is missing, 3 fills that twin (star-yellow ring), and each press after that fills the next, so a child can always finish. The board is sized in JS to the largest square that fits (beside the talk line on a wide screen). State `{on, hn, miss, h, mk}`.
- Tests: named check `spatial P3-16` (before the report): all three registered (bands, 12 levels, icon); 600 puzzles (every band x 12 levels x 5 days), `make()` deterministic. Cubes: sizes within 2 / 3 / 4 per band; the test's own sum of the heights is the answer and `solve()`; an independent screen-space depth buffer over every face of every cube and the floor (the app casts rays per column) shows every column's whole top and at least a third of every empty floor square; every counted cube is seen or under a seen top; the hidden-cube count agrees; the layers and the hint-3 numbers add up to the answer; no number in hints 1-2; no stack twice in a puzzle. Turn It: squares per band, one piece; exactly one choice is a rotation of the target under the test's own 4 turns (band A 1-6: the very same shape) and it is the answer; the answer is turned; every mirror choice is a mirror image that no turn makes (8-transform check); moved choices are other shapes; choice kinds per band; chiral targets in C and D; D's moved pair are mirror images; hint take-aways never take the answer. Mirror: kind and grid per band and level; the picture never on the side to fill; the solution is the exact mirror under the test's own maps and the finished picture is symmetric; squares to fill in the tier's range; the given half one piece; repeated hint 3 fills exactly the solution. Saved states round-trip for all three; 10, 9 and 10 damaged states are rejected. Difficulty grows by band (cubes 5.0 / 12.1 / 22.5, hidden 0.6 / 3.1 / 7.5, shape squares 4.3 / 4.7 / 5.5 / 6.5, mirror squares 6.0 / 9.2 / 11.0). The built bt file holds the types; `make()` about 5 ms (limit 25; a 4 x 4 x 4 stack with its hidden-cube count is the slow one). The depth buffer first disagreed with the app on hidden cubes: its samples with s = t met cube edges exactly (border of two faces); its samples now avoid the diagonal.
- Smoke block `// P3-16` (end of the main sequence, midway save, tools/lib/bt-p316.js): 380x780: Home -> Brain Teasers -> Cubes (band B): a wrong number (amber box, one mistake, try-ink line, not danger), hints 1-3 (no layers, then the layers, then their counts adding to the answer; three different lines), every stack typed on the pad, win card, 1 star saved and paid; -> Turn It (band A): three shadows, a wrong one shown gently, the rest tapped, 3 stars; -> Mirror (band C): a wrong square (amber, stays empty, one mistake, gentle line), two twins, hint 1 (the line glows), back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes the same squares, the rest tapped, 2 stars. 1024x600 (band D): Turn It: the mirror image tapped ("flipped"), hints 1-2 (four turns, nothing taken), every shape, 1 star; Cubes and Mirror straight through, 3 stars each. Every screen: 48 px targets (mirror squares 44), nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the puzzle does not scroll, the drawing on screen and not tiny, no emoji chrome.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-sp-hub bt-sp-levels bt-cb-b bt-cb-c bt-cb-c-hint bt-cb-d bt-cb-win bt-ro-a bt-ro-a-turn bt-ro-b bt-ro-c bt-ro-c-hint bt-ro-d bt-ro-win bt-mr-b bt-mr-c-h bt-mr-c-d bt-mr-d bt-mr-win`, real taps): looked at (montages) bt-cb-b, bt-cb-c, bt-cb-c-hint, bt-cb-d, bt-ro-a, bt-ro-a-turn, bt-ro-b, bt-ro-c, bt-ro-c-hint, bt-mr-b, bt-mr-c-h, bt-mr-c-d, bt-mr-d, bt-sp-hub, bt-cb-win at 380x780 and bt-cb-d, bt-ro-d, bt-ro-a, bt-ro-c, bt-mr-c-d at 1024x600. Fixed from looking: the name cut to "Cube Cou" (now "Cubes"), the empty squares of the two mirror halves looked alike (the given half is now lilac outlines), the mirror board ran past the bottom at 1024x600 and sat far from its talk line, talk lines were centred in landscape, the piece gloss read as a corner bracket (now a short dash), band A's shape was small, a three-line hint at 380; Turn It band D scrolled by 18 px at 1024x600 (smoke caught it; smaller choices there).
- check.sh: check OK on the first run (test.js 101 of 101, smoke OK, no console errors, no network). Sizes: index.html 1,152,527 -> 1,153,120 bytes (+0.6 KB: the icon), bt.v119.js 336,007 -> 393,523 (+57.5 KB: the three types and their CSS; warn at 400 KB), data unchanged, total 2,179,023 (over the 2 MB warning, as before this chunk).
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now has two or three more types; the Puzzle of the Day can land on them.)
- Not done: the framework's "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule (P3-01). Turn It in band D stays 2D (the spec says "harder shapes"; no 3D views). Mirror has no undo (a wrong square never fills, so there is nothing to undo).

### P3-13 Robot Path (v112, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.8, 7, 8): new puzzle type `robot` ("Robot Path", bands ABCD, 12 levels then the framework's Endless, `allow` 3) in a new `src/js/81-bt-robot.js` (object `RB`: the interpreter, the A/B generator, the bank reader, editing, hints and saved state as pure functions, then the screen and one `BT.reg`), its look in `src/css/49-bt-robot.css` (both moved into `bt.v112.js` by their names, A11b), a drawn `robot` icon in `15-icons.js`, the dev-time generator `tools/puzzles/robot.js` (registered in `build-banks.js`) and its bank `src/data/d-puz-robot.js` (8 KB). Nothing in the framework changed.
- What each band gets:
  | Band | Board | Blocks | Levels | Slots |
  |------|-------|--------|--------|-------|
  | A (PK-K) | 4x4, no walls | up, down, left, right (icons only; a tapped block says its name; the intro is spoken) | route 3 / 4 / 5 (levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12), a gem to pick up from level 7 | par + 2 |
  | B (1-2) | 5x5 with walls | the same arrows, icon and word | route 6, 6, 7, 7, 8, 8, 9, 9, 9, 10, 10, 10; a gem from level 7, two from level 10 | par + 3 |
  | C (3-4) | 6x6 corridor | forward, turn left, turn right, repeat N (2-9), loops two deep | line, line after a first turn, turn, stairs, nested turn and ring, tall stairs, stairs + run, rectangle (par 2 to 7) | par + 2 |
  | D (5-6) | 8x8 corridor | as C, plus "repeat until wall" (levels 1-9) or one function of up to 4 forward and turn blocks (levels 10-12) | rings and spirals (par 4), irregular stairs (6-7), two staircases with the function (8-10) | par + 1 |
- How levels are made: A and B at run time from the seeded rnd: A picks a start and goal (and a gem) at the level's distance; B walks a random path that never touches itself, walls every other cell, then opens walls in random order while a BFS over (cell, gems held) keeps the shortest route as long as the walk (about half the off-path cells stay walls); par = that BFS, `ref` = its route. C and D: corridor worlds (only the path is open) from templates, made and proven at dev time because par is the fewest blocks of ANY program, loops included: `tools/puzzles/robot.js` runs an exact search (effects state -> state/win/crash of every piece of program by cost, each kept once: loop-free sequences, loops of those, sequences with loops, loops of those; then a cheapest path over top-level pieces; the function as one extra command for every plain body of 2-4 blocks, since a function called once can be written out one block cheaper) and keeps a world only if the template's natural program is a shortest one, no plain program (no loop, no function) fits the slots, and on a function level every program without the function is longer. 36 worlds a band, 3 per level in level order; the app picks one of the level's three and one of the 8 turns and mirrors (a mirror swaps the turn blocks). One new block per D level group on purpose: with "repeat until wall" the search follows almost any corridor in 6-8 blocks by spinning (see Ideas and issues), so a function would never be needed next to it. `node tools/puzzles/build-banks.js robot` rebuilds the bank in about 6 s, byte for byte.
- Play: tap a palette block to put it in at the cursor (big drawn blocks: blue moves, purple turns, pink repeat, aqua until wall, green function; 64 px in band A); a loop comes with its end bracket and the cursor goes inside; Repeat asks "How many times?" (2-9). Tap a placed block to take it away (a repeat's head opens the number picker with Take it away; an until head takes the loop away and keeps its blocks); tap a loop's end bracket or the empty slot to put the next block after it; the function strip takes forward and turns only. Undo (40 steps), arrows and Backspace on a keyboard in A and B. A counter shows "3 of 7 blocks". Go works the whole run out first (`RB.run`), then shows it step by step: the running block lifts with a purple ring (its loops and the function call too), the robot glides from cell to cell (170-380 ms a step, faster for long runs; reduced motion: 160 ms steps without gliding), turns show on an orange pointer, orange dots mark the track, gems pop away. Go becomes Stop while it runs (stopping is not a mistake). The robot stops with a win the moment it reaches the flag with every gem.
- Mistakes (5.8: a failed run; the type allows 3): a bump (the robot wobbles, an amber star marks the wall, the block that bumped turns `--try-tint` with a dashed `--try-ink` ring and a warning mark; never red), stopping short of the flag, standing on the flag without every gem, a loop going round more than 40 times ("dizzy"). Each says what happened in the talk line (amber) and is spoken. Stars: the frame's rule; a winning program longer than par reports allow + 1 mistakes, so it earns 2 stars at most ("You made it with 7 blocks! It can be done with 5.").
- Hints (5.8 tiers; `RB.diverge`, `RB.applyHint`): A, B: the first arrow that bumps or does not bring the robot one step nearer the flag on a shortest route (BFS from where it is); C, D: the first block that differs from the bank's natural program (a repeat count the child chose is kept when the program still wins with it; the function strip is checked first once the program calls it). 1) that block, or the empty place, glows star yellow and the line says why ("This arrow bumps into a wall.", "So far so good! The next block goes here.", "The loop should end before this block."); 2) the right block shows faintly in that place ("Try Turn left here."); 3) it is put in (a wrong block replaced, a missing one added, a loop end moved). A program that already works gets "Your program works! Press Go." and no hint is counted.
- State (`api.saveState` after every edit and run; `RB.fit` rejects a state that does not fit the world, which then starts fresh): `{m, f: the program and the function as tokens, cs, ci: the cursor, miss: failed runs, runs, h: undo snapshots}`. Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- Look (DESIGN_SYSTEM, P4-18 notes): one SVG board, tokens only, light from the top left: a purple-tint tray on a ledge, cream floor tiles with a shade lip, stone wall blocks (drop shadow, side, top, highlight, seams), a dashed start pad, the flag on a green pad, aqua gems with facets, and a drawn robot (wheels, blue body with a yellow light, light head with a screen face whose eyes look where it faces, antenna); only transform and opacity move. Portrait: board, talk line, program, palette, Undo / count / Go; landscape: board left, panel right; a long program tightens the panel's rows so it still fits.
- Tests: named check `robot path P3-13` (about 17 s): registered with bands ABCD, 12 levels, allow 3; 1,152 worlds (4 bands x 12 levels x 24 days): `make()` deterministic, grid size and kit by band and level, start, goal and gems on open cells, walls (none in A, at least 4 in B), gems by level, slots; A and B: par equals the test's own BFS over (cell, gems) and the level's steps; C and D: a corridor, the bank index inside the level's three; `solve()` is a program the editor can make, of exactly par blocks, winning in the test's own simulator and in `RB.run`; tier 3 again and again from an empty program builds a shortest win, each step one block, and `diverge()` never changes the state; all 8 turns and mirrors seen. The bank: 36 worlds a band, no repeats, every par proven by the test's own search (written apart from tools/puzzles/robot.js), no plain program fits the slots, the function needed on function levels, all 8 turns and mirrors keep par and win, and the file is what `build-banks.js robot` writes. Interpreter: 18 cases (each arrow, off the board, a wall, the run stopping at the flag, short, empty, the flag without the gem, forward and turns, repeat, repeat inside repeat, repeat into the edge, until wall stopping, until wall with a turning body, dizzy, empty loops, the function), the step list (block, loops around it, the function call) and the bumping block. Editing: solve() built through `RB.add`/`RB.cursor` gives solve() back; no loop in the function, no block past the slots; saved state round trip; 15 damaged states rejected. Hints from wrong programs (4 bands x 3 levels x 6 starts) lead to a win. Difficulty: actions of the winning run grow by band (A 4.0, B 8.3, C 11.4, D 21.3), inside a band the last levels are longer (routes in A, B; program blocks in C, D); over par caps the stars at 2; A/B `make()` about 0.2 ms. Mutation-checked: turn left turning right, and a par one too high, both fail it.
- Smoke block `// P3-13` (end of the main sequence, midway save): band A level 8 at 380x780 (a Pre-K child), band C level 6 and band D level 11 (the function) at 1024x600: Home -> Brain Teasers -> Robot Path -> the level by real taps; the palette by band (icons only in A); a program that bumps, Go: the run fails gently (wobble, amber star, the bumping block exactly `--try-tint` and not `--danger`, dashed ring, warning mark, the line in `--try-ink`; one mistake), Undo empties the program and keeps the mistake; hint tiers 1 and 2 mark the place and show the block faintly with the program unchanged, tier 3 puts in exactly solve()'s first block; back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes exactly; the rest of solve() tapped in (repeat counts, loop ends, the function strip), Go: the blocks light up one after the other and Go reads Stop, the robot reaches the flag, win card, 1 star (three hints) and the next level saved. Every screen: 48 px targets, nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the puzzle fits without scrolling, no emoji chrome. Taps through `tools/lib/bt-rb.js` (token, build, rest, bump, go). Checked by hand (scratch script): reduced motion (no gliding, the run still shown and won), keyboard arrows and Backspace, Stop mid-run (no mistake).
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-rb-hub bt-rb-a bt-rb-b bt-rb-c bt-rb-d bt-rb-f bt-rb-bump bt-rb-hint bt-rb-win`, three sizes each, played by real taps): looked at all 27 (montages per size). Fixed from looking: the landscape panel was taller than the screen with the function strip (a normal-size Go, the counter beside Go, strip names on the box edge, tighter rows for long programs); on phones the board was small (empty slots only in A and B, no strip header without a function); the talk line changed height after the layout was measured; the loop end looked like a letter L (now a bracket); the facing pointer hid behind the antenna (drawn on top, antenna moved aside); band B had too few walls; the 5-block palette cut "Function" at 380 px.
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 90 of 90, smoke OK) on the seventh run. The machine was shared with other sessions (load average up to 21 on 4 cores) and the earlier runs each failed on one or two older timing- or layout-sensitive steps, never on P3-13 (whose test and three smoke steps passed every time): the map-draw timing (52 ms), random Crystal Caves questions measured at 380x780 ("count 12 stars (frames)", "51 prime or composite"), the P4-05 runner's cheer face, P3-10's "target D make() 8.6 ms (limit 8)", and once Chrome closing mid-run in the P2-13 block; one run was cut by the job's time limit, and `SMOKE_SPEED=4` does not work (early blocks wait a fixed 8 s). Sizes (bytes): index.html 1,095,485 -> 1,095,935 (+450, the robot icon), bt.v112.js 290,647 (+57.9 KB over bt.v106.js: 81-bt-robot.js 46 KB, a quarter of it comments and words; 49-bt-robot.css 12 KB), games 187,379 (unchanged), data.v112.js 284,530 (+8.1 KB, the robot bank), total 1,858,491.
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now includes Robot Path in every band; the Puzzle of the Day pick can now land on it.)
- Not done: the "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule is still the framework's open item (P3-01). No dragging of blocks (5.8 and section 8 do not ask for it; tap to add and tap to take away work on a phone).

### P3-11 Tangram engine and 8 puzzles (v111, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.7, 7, 8): new puzzle type `tangram` ("Tangram", bands ABCD, 12 levels then the framework's Endless) in a new `src/js/80-bt-tangram.js` (object `TG`: exact and plain-number geometry, puzzles, snapping, the solved check, hints, the screen `TG.mount`, one `BT.reg`), its look in a new `src/css/49-bt-tangram.css`, the bank `src/data/d-puz-tangram.js` (`PUZ.tangram`, read only inside functions, A11), and a drawn `tangram` icon (soft square, two big triangles, a square, two small triangles) in `15-icons.js`. The file is named `80-bt-*`, so A11b puts it in `bt.<VER>.js`; nothing in the framework changed.
- Puzzle data format (documented at the top of the bank for P3-12): `{id, name, say, pcs}`; `pcs` is the SOLUTION, seven placements `[k, f, x, y]` in the order L L M S S Q P. Unit: the big square of all seven has side 4, so the pieces start as L (0,0)(4,0)(2,2), M (0,0)(2,0)(0,2), S (0,0)(2,0)(1,1), Q (1,0)(2,1)(1,2)(0,1), P (0,0)(2,0)(3,1)(1,1) (y down); flip f (x -> -x, P only), turn k x 45 degrees clockwise about (0,0), move by (x, y). x and y are exact: a number (whole or half) or a pair `[a, b]` = a + b·√2/2, so every corner of every piece is such a pair and the test compares corners exactly. The silhouette is the union of the solution, so every puzzle is solvable by construction. The bank is in order of difficulty: level L uses entry L-1; past the end a level picks one of the harder half by the day's seed; with 12 or more entries each level gets its own slice (P3-12 only adds data). `make()` also mirrors the picture left-right by the seed (an exact mirror: k -> 8-k, f flipped, x -> -x; triangles and the square are put back into an unflipped form, `TG.xnorm`).
- The 8 puzzles (edge-connected, no holes): House (a square under a big roof), Rocket (nose, body, fins, nozzle, a trailing flame), Sailboat (two sails on a hull), Fish (diamond body, fan tail, top and bottom fins), Cat (sitting, two ears, tail up), Rabbit (one long ear, round tail), Turtle (shell, legs, head on a neck, tail), Swan (long neck, head with a beak, wing, tail). Each has a name and a spoken prompt ("Make a house with a big roof.").
- Bands (the section 7 help lever, `TG.PLAN`): A: 2 pieces to place (levels 1-6), then 3, only big ones (big and middle triangles, square), the rest of the picture already built, dashed outlines of the missing pieces, on levels 1-4 the tray pieces already lie the right way; B: 4 pieces (1-6), then 5, any kind, outlines, the parallelogram comes the right way up until level 8; C: all seven, no outlines, one placed for you (a big triangle on levels 1-6, another piece after); D: all seven, no help. A tray piece never starts in the answer's turn when turning is part of the level.
- Input (pointer events, so touch, pen and mouse; one svg with `touch-action: none`): drag a piece from the tray or the board (lifted, it takes the board's size and a drop shadow); a drop on the tray puts it back. Snapping on drop (`TG.snap`): every move of at most 0.45 unit (16-30 px) that puts one of the piece's corners on a corner of the silhouette, of the hidden solution or of another piece is a candidate; one that fits (inside, no overlap) wins, then the one with the most corners landing on corners, then the shortest. Turning: tap a piece to pick it (purple ring), tap the picked piece again to turn it 45 degrees clockwise about its middle (then it snaps again); the Turn button does the same; Flip shows only while the parallelogram is picked. Tap-then-tap (section 8): with a piece picked, a tap on the board moves it there. Undo (40 steps, in memory). Keys R, F, U. A piece that sticks out or lies on another gets an amber dashed ring and the line says so (never red). No mistakes are counted (see Ideas).
- Solved (`TG.solved`, geometric, any arrangement): every piece on the board, no two overlap (shared area at most 0.02 unit², by convex clipping), and every piece lies inside the silhouette (its overlap with the solution pieces adds up to its own area within 0.02). The areas add up, so that is an exact cover; the two big and the two small triangles are interchangeable, the square may turn 90 degrees, the parallelogram 180. Positions within about 0.01 unit count as the same; snapping lands exactly.
- Hints (5.7; tier 1, 2, 3, then 3 again): the slot (a solution piece) not yet filled, big pieces first, preferring one whose piece is still in the tray: 1) its outline glows star yellow on the silhouette and its piece is picked with a dashed purple ring ("See the yellow outline? The red big triangle goes there."); 2) that piece turns (and flips) the slot's way, in the tray or about its middle on the board; 3) it goes in, and a piece lying on that slot goes back to the tray. Tiers 1 and 2 never move a piece; the slot stays shown (and saved) until it is filled.
- State (`api.saveState` after every move): `{pc: per piece null (placed for you), [k, f] (tray) or [k, f, x, y] (board, units), h: the hinted slot or -1}`; `TG.fit` refuses a state that does not belong to the puzzle (which then starts fresh). Puzzle: `{id, name, say, b, n, m, pcs (exact), fix, ol, tray}`. Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's records.
- Look (tokens only, light from the top left): the board a white card, the tray a `--bt-tint` well; the silhouette one path (`TG.outline` joins the solution and drops the inner edges) in `--ill-ink-2` with a lighter copy moved down-right and clipped to it, so a shadow runs along its top-left inside edge, and a deep rim (gold when solved). Pieces in seven `--ill-*` colours (red, blue, yellow, green, pink, orange, aqua), each on a ledge in its shade tone, with a deep outline and a light edge on the sides facing the top left; pieces placed for you lie flat (no ledge). On a win the pieces pop one after another. Layout: portrait talk line, svg (board on top, tray below; the board takes the height the silhouette can use at full width), Turn / Flip / Undo; landscape svg (board, tray) and a side column with the talk line and the buttons. Tray cells are at least 56 px; on the board a piece is caught 12 px around its edge.
- Tests: named check `tangrams P3-11` (about 15 s). With exact numbers of its own (pairs, exact signs and products), every bank entry: format, exact areas (each piece's, 16 in all), no two pieces overlap (separating axis on every edge normal), one shape joined by shared edges (a shared corner alone does not count), the spec's 96x96 raster union within 2%, the app's outline one loop of area 16, `TG.xverts` and `TG.xmirror` agree with the test's own corners. Then 288 puzzles (4 bands x 12 levels x 6 days): `make()` deterministic, the bank index per level, the band plan (pieces to place, kinds in band A, the given piece in C, outlines, tray turns and flips), the puzzle's pieces are the bank's or their exact mirror image, both seen; `solve()` wins, a fresh puzzle does not; 1,456 interchangeable forms accepted (big triangles swapped, small triangles swapped, the square turned 90/180/270, the parallelogram 180, a 2e-5 nudge); 2,939 near misses refused (each piece turned 45 degrees, the parallelogram flipped, moved by 0.05, 0.25 or 0.4, back in the tray); 720 drops 0.18-0.3 unit off land exactly (with the other pieces in, and on an empty board); hints from the start to the end: tier 1 changes nothing, tier 2 turns only the named piece and moves no middle, tier 3 puts exactly that piece in its slot and sends a piece lying there back to the tray, hints alone finish every puzzle; a saved state round trip fits and gives the same next hint; 792 damaged states refused (17 kinds: not an object, six pieces, turn 8 or 1.5, flip 2, a flipped triangle, three numbers, x missing or far away, h 7 or a string, a piece missing, a given piece moved or hinted); `make()` well under 5 ms. Mutation-checked: an overlapping bank entry, and a solved check with a 0.3 tolerance, both fail it.
- Smoke block `// P3-11` (end of the main sequence, midway save), 380x780 grade 1 (band B, level 1, by CDP touch) and 1024x600 grade 5 (band D, level 1, by mouse): Home card -> hub -> Tangram -> level 1. Every move is worked out from the solution and the board (the slot each piece fills): pick, turn with taps (touch) or the Turn button (mouse), flip if needed, then a drag onto the slot's middle with a 6-9 px miss that the snap corrects, or (touch, once) tap-then-tap; each piece must land exactly in its slot. Also a drag back to the tray, a piece dropped turned wrong rings amber with an amber line and Undo takes it back (mouse), hint tiers 1-3 (1 shows the slot and picks its piece, nothing moves; 2 turns only that piece; 3 puts one more piece on the board), back, back, Keep going resumes the same puzzle and state, the rest by drags, the win card with 1 star (3 hints) and the star saved. Every screen: buttons and tray cells at least 48 px, the smallest piece on the board catchable at 48 px, nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the puzzle needs no scrolling, no emoji chrome.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-tg-levels bt-tg-a bt-tg-a-turn bt-tg-b bt-tg-c bt-tg-d bt-tg-hint bt-tg-win bt-tg-sheet`, three sizes; `tangram` is the same sheet as `bt-tg-sheet`, BRAIN_TEASERS 5.7): looked at a, c, d at 380x780 (several rounds), d and b at 380x780 and 600x960, c and hint at 1024x600, a-turn, b, hint, win and levels at 380x780, a, c, win at 600x960, the sheet (every silhouette and its solution) and the hub card with the new icon. Fixed from looking: tray pieces were tiny (cells were sized for every turn of every piece; now only the pieces in the tray, each by its box, growing as the tray empties, and the board takes only the height the silhouette needs); "Tangrams" was cut in the top bar; the hint ring did not show on the yellow piece (now purple, dashed); pieces placed for you now lie flat. First designs of an ice cream (its silhouette was a plain diamond), a tree (it looked like the house) and a swan were replaced; the fish's lower fin moved back so its nose is a point.
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now includes Tangram in every band; the Puzzle of the Day pick can now land on Tangram.)
- Not done: the 24 more puzzles and Shape Fit (P3-12); no keyboard way to move a piece (R, F, U only); undo is not kept across a resume.
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files (new 80-bt-tangram.js, 49-bt-tangram.css, d-puz-tangram.js; the icon in 15-icons.js; tests, smoke, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v111 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-11-tangram from 302c92c (v106). Overlaps are appends: tools/test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), tools/smoke.js (one block at the end of the main sequence), tools/shots.js (one block after the P3-05 shots), 15-icons.js (one icon before `path-math`), BRAIN_TEASERS 5.7 (an as-built note), built files (rebuild and stage everything, deletions included). Renumber VER at merge.

### P3-14 Riddles, Memory Match and Copy Cat (v114, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.9, 2, 3, 7, 9): three new puzzle types, each registered on its own, bands ABCD, 12 levels then the framework's Endless: `riddle` ("Riddles", `src/js/81-bt-riddle.js`, object `RDL`, `allow` 1), `memory` ("Memory", `82-bt-memory.js`, object `MM`, `allow` 1), `copycat` ("Copy Cat", `83-bt-copycat.js`, object `CC`, `allow` 2), their looks in `src/css/49-bt-riddle.css`, `49-bt-memory.css`, `49-bt-copycat.css` (all moved into `bt.<VER>.js` by the build), three drawn icons (`riddle` a speech bubble with a question mark, `memory` two cards, `copycat` four pads with one lit) in `15-icons.js`, and two data files: `src/data/d-riddles.js` (`RIDDLES`: 36 riddles per band, 144 in all, plus `pics` and `names`) and `src/data/d-memory.js` (`MEMW`: picture themes, 20 synonym pairs, 23 word roots). Data is read only inside functions (A11). One framework touch: on a screen under 641 px tall the win card leaves out its big disc and may scroll (`48-teasers.css`), because at 1024x600 a card with note lines pushed Again and Levels off the screen (the new smoke check of the bottom edge caught it; it was true for P3-07's win card too).
- What each band gets:
  | Band | Riddles (3 a level) | Memory Match (levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12) | Copy Cat (target length, levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12) |
  |------|---------------------|-----------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------|
  | A (PK-K) | "Who am I?" / "What am I?", 3 picture choices (`picFor`, no words), the riddle read aloud by itself | 3 pairs (levels 1-6), 4 pairs (7-12): same picture, same picture, big letter + small letter | 4 pads, from 1 to 3 / 4 / 5, light 700 ms + 320 ms gap |
  | B (1-2) | simple riddles, 4 choices with a picture and its word | 6 pairs: number + dice dots (1-6), word + picture, number + dots (4-10, two dice faces from 7) | 4 pads, from 2 to 4 / 5 / 6, faster (520 + 240) |
  | C (3-4) | wordplay, 4 word choices | 8 pairs: fraction + circle picture, Spanish + English (SPN), synonyms | 6 pads, from 2 to 5 / 6 / 7 (480 + 220) |
  | D (5-6) | lateral thinking and number riddles, 4 choices | 10 pairs: equal fractions, fraction / decimal / percent, word root + meaning | 6 pads, from 3 to 7 forward, then 5 / 6 backwards (440 + 200) |
  Each level of a tier lights 15 ms faster. 5.9's "star thresholds 4 / 6 / 8 scaled by band" became these target lengths: the frame's stars count hints and mistakes, and the length grows by band and level (section 7 "steps").
- Riddles: 36 original riddles per band (spec 30), a few retold folk riddles (hands but cannot clap, keys but no lock, a neck but no head, a room with no doors ...), nothing scary or rude, each with a nudge and a strong clue. Level L plays riddles 3(L-1) to 3L-1, so a band's whole set is used before a riddle repeats; the seeded rnd shuffles the choices. Play: a wrong choice turns amber with its badge, stays out and counts one mistake; the line says "Not that one. Think again!" in `--try-ink`. The right one turns green, the line says the answer ("Yes! It is a cow." in A and B, spoken), the next riddle follows. Hints per riddle: 1 nudge, 2 strong clue (yellow hint line, spoken), 3 takes away the wrong choices but one; then that riddle has no more hints. The win card lists the three answers. State `{i, miss, h, x, g}`; `RDL.fit` rejects a state that does not fit.
- Memory Match: the layout comes from the seeded rnd (same for a band, level and day). Every card has exactly one partner (the values, words and roots in a puzzle are all different). A face-down card holds no face in the page and is labelled "Card N, face down"; the saved state names cards by place only. Cards turn on their Y axis (`--t-slow`; instant with reduced motion or calm mode), a pair rings green with a tick and pops, two that do not match ring amber with a small wobble and turn back after 1.1 s (A 1.5 s) or at the next tap, with a soft `SFX.del`. A "slip" is a miss when the first card's partner, or the second card, had already been seen; the win reports the slips over the band's free ones (A 0, B 1, C 2, D 3). Hints: 1 peeks one card (the partner of the card that is up, else the unseen partner of a seen card, else an unseen card), 2 shows one pair for a moment, 3 matches one pair (star yellow). Cards are 4:5, square on a wide short screen; the grid is fitted to the space left (`MM.fitGrid`). State `{m, hm, seen, turns, slips}`; a card left up mid-turn is face down after a resume.
- Copy Cat: pads are red circle, blue square, yellow triangle, green star, then purple heart and orange diamond, each in its own place with its own note (`SFX.key`: C E G C, or C D E G A C), drawn with the patterns' shading (shadow, shade, base toward the top-left light, gloss). The cat (`picArt('cat')`) says "Watch me!", the pads light one by one (the light tone fills the pad and it grows 5%), then "Your turn!". A wrong pad: amber with a dashed ring, "Oops! Watch again.", a soft sound, the round shown again 20% slower (one mistake). Round dots and step pips show the progress; band D backwards levels carry a "Backwards" tag and say "last one first". Hints (while it is the child's turn): 1 the round again 70% slower, 2 the next pad ringed in star yellow (and named aloud), 3 the next pad tapped for the child. State `{r, miss}`; a resume shows the round again. `CC.expect` / `CC.check` are the pure rules the screen, the test and the bot share.
- Tests: named check `riddles, memory, copy cat P3-14` (before the report): all three registered (ABCD, 12 levels, an icon); the riddle bank: at least 30 per band (36), shape, 2 or 3 wrong choices, choices all different (articles and plurals ignored), no wrong choice in an `ok` list, the answer (and a number as its word, 0-20) in neither the riddle nor its hints, hints end a sentence, no repeated riddle, sentence and riddle length per band (A 10/24, B 14/30, C 18/36, D 24/50 words) and words per sentence growing A 4.4 < B 5.4 < C 8.3 < D 9.1, BAD on every word, band A/B pictures named and allow-listed; 240 puzzles of each type (4 bands x 12 levels x 5 days): `make()` deterministic, riddles are the level's bank riddles and levels 1-12 use all 36, hint tiers (words, then all wrong choices but one, also after a wrong tap), `solve()`; memory: pair counts and kinds per band and level, exact pairs (each id twice, one per side), the test's own value of every face shows each value on exactly two cards of one pair, `solve()` matches them all in n turns with no slip, a first miss is free and a repeat flip is a slip, settle, a matched card cannot be picked, hint 1 peeks the partner of the card that is up, hint 2 changes nothing but seen, hint 3 matches that pair and alone finishes the board, grid at least 48 px in three spaces; copy cat: pads and run rule per band, `expect`/`check` exact (every prefix "go", the whole round "ok", any other pad at any step and an extra tap "no"), backwards rounds, `solve()` rounds; saved states round-trip for all three and 11, 11 and 9 damaged states are rejected; difficulty grows by band (pairs 3.5 / 6 / 8 / 10, copy cat length 4 / 5 / 6 / 7 with backwards counted, light 678 / 498 / 458 / 418 ms); the built bt and data files hold the types and banks; `make()` well under 5 ms.
- Smoke block `// P3-14` (end of the main sequence, midway save, tools/lib/bt-p314.js): 380x780: Home -> Brain Teasers -> Riddles (band A): three picture choices, hints 1 and 2 (two different lines, nothing taken), hint 3 takes one wrong picture, the other wrong one tapped (amber badge, one mistake, the line in `--try-ink`, not `--danger`), the three riddles answered by taps, win card with the answers, 1 star and level 2 saved, stars paid; -> Memory (band B): face-down cards hold no face and say nothing of it, a mismatch (both up and amber, gentle line, one turn) turns back, a pair found, hint 1 (one card peeks) and hint 2 (a pair peeks, other words) leave the matched cards, back to the levels and the hub, Keep going brings back the same board, the rest tapped in, 1 star; -> Copy Cat (band C, 6 pads): a wrong pad (amber, one mistake, gentle line) and the same round shown again, hint 1 replays, hint 2 rings exactly the next pad, every round copied, 1 star. 1024x600: Memory band D, 20 cards, a mismatch, every pair tapped, 3 stars. Every screen: 48 px targets (pads 64), nothing outside the screen (now the bottom edge too), no sideways scroll, the puzzle does not scroll, no emoji outside the content pictures.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-p314-hub bt-rd-a bt-rd-b bt-rd-c bt-rd-d bt-rd-win bt-mm-a bt-mm-a-abc bt-mm-b bt-mm-b-word bt-mm-c bt-mm-c-es bt-mm-d bt-mm-d-fdp bt-mm-d-root bt-mm-win bt-cc-a bt-cc-b bt-cc-c bt-cc-d bt-cc-win`, three sizes, real taps): looked at every one at 380x780, most at 1024x600 and seven at 600x960 (montages). Fixed from looking: band A picture choices were small (now up to 104 px pictures on 168 px buttons), the D win note read "1. 6" (now "Riddle 1: 6"), memory words and the card-back star were small, 20 memory cards on 1024x600 were tiny 4:5 cards (now square cards on wide short screens), the talk lines were centred in landscape, the memory win card listed five pairs and hid its buttons at 1024x600 (three pairs now, and the win card fix above).
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 93 of 93, smoke OK, no console errors, no network). The machine was busy (load 16-19): the first full run failed only the existing P3-10 timing step (`target D: make() takes 10.5 ms on average (limit 8 ms)`, not touched here; the test.js run just before passed it); the rerun passed. ### P2-37 Discover science 3-6, geography and civics (v113, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM 2.4 and 6, ROADMAP P2-37): Discover now has three worlds, by world id and in CURRICULUM order: `d_nature` Nature Trail (K) grows two stops after Push, Pull & Stuff (geo_world 1 "Maps & Land", civ 1 "Our Community"), `d_lab` Science Lab (grade 3: sci_life 2, sci_earth 2, sci_phys 2, geo_us 1, geo_world 2) and `d_cosmos` Star Observatory (grade 5: sci_life 3, sci_earth 3, sci_phys 3, geo_us 2, civ 2), each ending in a boss. 18 stops in all. Skills `sci_*` max 1 -> 3, new `geo_world` (max 2), `geo_us` (max 2), `civ` (max 2) in `38-skills.js`; icons map, pin, users. Strand "Geography and civics" already existed.
- Levels: CURRICULUM 6 marks cells "n/a" where a skill has no content for a grade band, so a skill's levels count only the bands it has and match the stop table (geo_us 1 is the 3-4 column, geo_us 2 the 5-6 column, civ 2 the 5-6 column). Each item's `grade` says its band. Note added to CURRICULUM 6.
- Content: `src/data/d-disc2-sci.js` (science levels 2-3) and `src/data/d-disc2-soc.js` (geography and civics), built into data.v113.js. They are named d-disc2-* because "d-disc-x" would sort before d-disc.js, which defines `discRows` and `DISC_HINT`; `discRows` gained an optional grade argument. 915 new items, all with grade and topic tags and a hint that teaches: sci_life 2 63 (lifecycle, foodchain, adapt, classify, plantfn, fossils), sci_life 3 62 (cells, ecosystem, photo, body, traits), sci_earth 2 66 (watercycle, rocks, erosion, resources, moonphase, planets), sci_earth 3 73 (plates, rockcycle, atmosphere, solarscale, rotrev), sci_phys 2 72 (states, forces, machines, energy, circuits), sci_phys 3 73 (mixtures, change, gravity, transfer, waves), geo_world 1 63 (mapwords, landforms, continents, oceans), geo_world 2 73 (grid, key, hemi, climate, features), geo_us 1 166 (capitals, states, regions, borders; generated from a table of 50 states, 50 capitals, five regions and a border table, no random numbers), geo_us 2 60 (landmarks, rivers, mountains, timezones), civ 1 65 (helpers, rules, symbols, needswants, community), civ 2 74 (branches, constitution, rights, economy, supply). Map skills are asked in words: coordinates written as pairs such as (3, 2), a key and a scale described in a sentence; no item needs a map or a picture (4 items have a small picture, all from the allow-list). Civics is neutral: how government works, no living person, no holiday of one faith. Regions skip states that books place differently (DE, MD, WV, KY, OK, AR, VA, CO, LA); neighbour questions never use states that touch only at a point or across water.
- Start world: Discover has worlds only for K, 3 and 5, so the old rule (first world at or above the grade) would start a 1st, 2nd or 4th grader on a locked world. `PATHS.disc.startBelow` makes `startWorld()` use the last world at or below the grade (Nature Trail for K-2, Science Lab for 4th grade, Star Observatory for 5-6). No other path sets it.
- Saves: no format change (LATEST stays 6, `S.maps.disc` keyed by stop id). A child who had finished the old Nature Trail keeps every star, the boss stays passed, the two new stops open as NEW stops (`stopBehind`/`stopNew`), Science Lab opens (previous world finished) and Star Observatory opens at grade 5 or above only.
- Tests (test.js): "Discover 3-6, geography and civics P2-37": bankProblems with 40+ per level, grade and topic tags, one right answer, no wrong answer that holds the words of the right one, hints never name the answer (word or whole), the answer is not in the prompt, no prompt points at a picture or map, 3,000 seeded questions per level with kindProblems (36,000), the state, capital, region and border tables checked against each item, worlds and stops in CURRICULUM order, a finished Nature Trail child at 9 grade values over 4 fixtures, and the content is in the data file not index.html. Expectations changed in the P2-36 check: it now covers level 1 only (`filter(it=>it.lv===1)`), world list d_nature/0,d_lab/3,d_cosmos/5, first stops sci_life1 sci_earth1 sci_phys1 and a boss at index 5, the Home card says "0 of 18 stops", and a 3rd grader and up start in a later world. In the grade check (start world per grade) a `startBelow` path expects the last world at or below the grade.
- Smoke: the P2-36 block expects the Nature Trail map (12 stops shown: Nature Trail and the world after it) and 6 stops in the first world. New `// P2-37` block at the end of the main sequence: a 4th grader opens Discover on Science Lab, plays Life Cycles & Food, States & Capitals and Our Community with real taps at 380x780, stars saved under each stop id, 48 px choices and stop buttons, no sideways scroll, no cut-off words. Shots `disc-map-lab`, `disc-q-geo1`, `disc-q-civ1`, `disc-q-life2`, `disc-q-earth2`, `disc-q-phys2`, `disc-q-us1`, `disc-q-geo2`, `disc-q-life3`, `disc-q-earth3`, `disc-q-phys3`, `disc-q-us2`, `disc-q-civ2`.

### P2-33 Units batch 3: Spanish world La Ciudad (v117, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM S-units and 2.3 row 6, ROADMAP P2-33): four units appended to `ES_UNITS` in `src/data/d-es.js` (no `mix`, no `spell`, so the Big Review and the old stops are untouched): `es_place` Places in Town (11 words: parque, tienda, biblioteca, hospital, playa, restaurante, iglesia, mercado, calle, banco, cine), `es_trans` Transport (10: carro, autobús, tren, avión, bicicleta, barco, camión, moto, helicóptero, cohete), `es_verb` Action Words (12 infinitives: correr, saltar, comer, beber, dormir, leer, escribir, jugar, cantar, bailar, nadar, caminar; glosses "to run" ...), `es_adj` Opposites (13 masculine singular adjectives in pairs: grande/pequeño, alto/bajo, rápido/lento, nuevo/viejo, caliente/frío, bonito, limpio/sucio). Nouns carry el/la, verbs and adjectives have no article.
- Pictures: places, verbs and adjectives use `cap:1` (the English shows under the picture in levels 1 and 2), because one emoji cannot tell "big" from "tall" or "run" from "walk" by itself; so no distractor can also be right. Transport pictures are unambiguous without it. Eleven emoji were added to `tools/emoji-allow.txt` (none risky): 🏪 🏥 ⛪ 🥕 🏦 🎬 🏃 🦘 🛌 🎤 💃.

Saves are keyed by stop id; no format change. La Casa and El Tiempo (P2-31, P2-32) go before it in CURRICULUM; place by id at merge (the world is not "last").
- Tests (test.js): "Spanish La Ciudad P2-33": counts, explicit gender list, infinitives, NFC, allow-list pictures, no duplicate word across all units, no shared picture within a new unit, 3,000 seeded questions per unit and level through `kindProblems` (one right answer, caption in level 2), the P2-30 hash of the old stops, world and stop order by id, save safety on the six fixtures. Smoke (`// P2-33` block): La Ciudad opened by id, es_verb 1 and es_adj 2 rounds with real taps at 380x780, stars saved, 48 px choices, no sideways scroll. Shots `es-ciudad-map`, `es-ciudad-place1/2`, `-trans1`, `-verb1/2`, `-adj1/2`.
- Test expectations changed: (1) in "Spanish framework P2-30" the old-stops hash now runs over the nine original skills by name (it used every `es_*` skill, which now includes the new ones; same hash, same 2,700 questions) and the stop-list check compares the first 21 stops, which are the original ones; (2) the known exceptions of "say present for every question" list the four new level 2 skills, same reason as the old ones (level 2 gives no `say` on purpose).

### P2-32 Spanish world El Tiempo (v116, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM S-units, section 2.3 row 5): five units appended to `ES_UNITS` in `src/data/d-es.js`, and world `e_tiempo` "El Tiempo" (`g:-1`, after Gran Repaso; position checked by id) in `PATHS.es` with stops `es_days 1-2, es_months 1-2, es_weather 1-2, es_big 1-2, es_time 1-2` in that order (titles 22 letters or less; 10 stops and the boss). Saves are keyed by stop id; no format change.
- Units: `days` 10 words (lunes to domingo with `el`, hoy, mañana, ayer; `cap:1` so the English day shows under the calendar picture), `months` 16 (enero to diciembre, primavera, verano, otoño, invierno with their articles; `cap:1`), `weather` 10 (hace sol, hace calor, hace frío, hace viento, llueve, nieva, está nublado, plus hace fresco, hay tormenta, el arco iris), `big` 18 (level 1: treinta to noventa and cien, picture `n:30`...; level 2 adds veintiuno, veintitrés, veintisiete, treinta y cinco, cuarenta y dos, cincuenta y ocho, sesenta y uno, setenta y cuatro, ochenta y nueve, noventa y nueve: hear it -> numeral at level 1, numeral -> word at level 2), `time` 20 (level 1: es la una, son las dos ... doce on the hour; level 2 adds y media and y cuarto, e.g. son las tres y media). No `mix`, no `spell`. `veinte` is not repeated in `big` because it is already in `es_teen` (no word twice across units).
- Two small additions to the framework: a unit field `l1:N` (level 1 draws only from the first N items, level 2 from all; old units do not have it so their questions are unchanged by seed) and a picture form `k:H:MM` (an analogue clock from `VIZ.clock`, class `clkpic`; there is only one clock emoji in the allow-list and clocks need to show half and quarter past). Pictures are emoji that draw clearly in headless Chrome (looked at in the shots): ☀ 🥵 🥶 💨 ☔ ❄ ☁ 🍃 ⚡ 🌈, not 🌧. New allow-list entries (none risky): ☔ 🍂 💨 📅 📆 🥵 🥶.
- Tests: "Spanish El Tiempo P2-32" (unit counts and words, NFC, articles, glosses, every number picture against a small Spanish number-word function, every clock against its words, no duplicate word across all units, 3,000 seeded questions per level through `kindProblems` with answer and choices inside the level pool, big/time level 2 reach the later items, world and stop order by id, fixture save safety for all six fixtures). Smoke block `// P2-32` (380x780): world exists; a round of days (listen) and of big numbers (numeral -> word) with real taps, 3 choices of at least 48 px, no sideways scroll, stars saved under the stop id. Shots `es-tiempo-days1/2, months1, weather1/2, big1/2, time1/2` (looked at).
- Test expectations changed: P2-30 test accepts the `k:` picture form; its old-stops hash now covers only the original Spanish skills (the hash value is unchanged, the new skills are left out of the loop) and its stop list is a prefix check, since a world now follows Gran Repaso; the "say present" known exceptions list the five new level 2 skills for the same reason as the older ones (level 2 is silent on purpose).
- Not done / ideas: the seven weekday pictures are the same calendar leaf (the caption tells them apart); levels 1 and 2 of days and months are the same pool. `menos cuarto` and times to the minute are left for later.
- Merge: P2-31 (La Casa) is built in parallel; this world is appended after `e_repaso` and may need re-placing after `e_casa` by id when merged. Provisional v116, renumber at merge.
