### P2-14 6th grade: world Galaxy Core (v111, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM M-6b and 2.1 row 16, ROADMAP P2-14): new world `m_galaxy` "Galaxy Core" (grade 6, milky way icon, right after `m_sky`, the last math world for now) with the 10 stops in CURRICULUM order: ooo 2 Exponents, alg 1 One-Step Equations, ooo 3 Evaluate Expressions, alg 2 Solve with x and /, geo 5 Triangles & Trapezoids, coord 2 Four Quadrants, fact 3 GCF and LCM, data 5 Mean, Median, Mode, alg 3 Two-Step Equations, story 9 Rate & Percent Stories, then the boss. New skill `alg` (3 levels, "Equations"); extended `ooo` 1 -> 3, `geo` 4 -> 5, `coord` 1 -> 2, `fact` 2 -> 3, `data` 4 -> 5, `story` 8 -> 9 with dispatchers in `38-skills.js` (old levels untouched, checked by seed).
- Generators: one block `// ---- P2-14 Galaxy Core ----` at the end of `35-gen-math-upper.js` (names `gal`; `gx` is the graphics pass's CSS prefix, so it was not used). ooo 2: 14 expression shapes with squares to 12, cubes to 5 and parentheses (also `(a + b)^2`, `P - (b + c)`), every step a whole number, results 2-400; ooo 3: `3x + 4` for a given x (9 shapes, letters x n y k m p, `x^2`, `a(x + b)`, `x / a + b`), two letters x and y in a third of them. alg 1: `x + a = b`, `a + x = b`, `x - a = b`, solutions 1-99, the first hint step is a balance (VIZ.balance, kept small by `.galbal`); alg 2: `a x = b` (x to 15) and `x / a = b`; alg 3: `a x + b = c` / `a x - b = c` (x 1-12) and inequalities `x > k`, `x < k` with k -4..4: pick the number line (four lines, one picture each) or name the inequality of a line. geo 5: triangle (base x height / 2), parallelogram (a slanted side that is not needed in half of them) and trapezoid, labelled base and height with a dashed height and a square corner, answers whole or .5 (decimal pad, `eq:'value'`), units cm, m, in, ft. coord 2: tap a point (grid -2..2), pick the ordered pair of a point (-6..6), name the quadrant (from the pair or the picture), reflect across an axis (pick the pair, or tap on the small grid). fact 3: GCF and LCM of 2-36 (LCM at most 144) as bare numbers and as word problems (bracelets, buses), hints list the factors or count by the bigger number. data 5: mean, median, mode, range of 5-7 whole numbers (a median only with 5 or 7; the mean is whole, the mode is unique, the mean is not the median) as a list or as a line plot (VIZ.linePlot). story 9: ratio (part or total), rate, unit price (decimal answer), percent (part, sale price, tip) and one-step equation stories (add, take away, multiply, divide, money with `stepsSub`).
- Step hints: every question has one (steps, the last ends in "?", none states the answer; ooo uses `stepsStory`, the rest `skyHint`; the money story uses `stepsSub`).
- Shared engine edits: `VIZ.rayLine(o)` in `23-viz.js` (an inequality on a number line: the numLine with every value labelled, a thick ray and a hollow end dot; header documented) and the CSS class `.vray` in the new `src/css/45-galaxy.css` (with `.galbal`, the small balance). `VIZ.coordGrid` and the `neg` pad needed no change (four quadrants and negative numbers already existed). No change to `49-kinds.js` (a typed (x, y) answer with the +/- key was built first, then dropped: with the 5th pad row the 13 x 13 grid shrank to about 70 px on a phone, so reading a point and reflecting are choices of ordered pairs).
- Tests (test.js 90 of 90): "Galaxy Core P2-14" with 3,000 seeded questions per new level (10 levels), maths recomputed from the label with an own evaluator (+ - x / ^ parentheses, letters, a number next to a letter means times) and from the picture (tap targets and their pixel position on the grid, the point drawn, the ray read back from the svg, dots of a line plot, the numbers written on a drawn triangle), every sum a hint writes is true, hints never give the answer, kindProblems zero, say/label/choices, reproducible, old levels unchanged by seed, stops read from CURRICULUM row 16, block has no Math.random / raw hex / name without the `gal` prefix, `VIZ.rayLine` deterministic and tokens only, four-quadrant tap grid at most 310 units wide; and a save check (fixtures x 9 grades + 3 synthetic saves) that ends the path at Galaxy Core while it runs: no star moves, nothing re-locks, the map opens where it did except for the new world.
- Test expectations changed: the `SK` maxima in the P2-07 (geo 5, data 5, story 9), P2-09 (fact 3), P2-10 (geo 5, story 9) and P2-11 (ooo 3) stop checks, because those levels are this world's (the skills grew; their old levels are checked unchanged). The Volcano Lab P2-11 fixture check removes the later worlds `m_desert`, `m_caves`, `m_sky` to compare with and without its world; it now removes `m_galaxy` too. Nothing weakened.
- Smoke (`// P2-14` block, last in the main sequence; `answer()` learned the `match` kind): a 6th grader whose earlier worlds are finished (Sky City too) opens the map on Galaxy Core (position by id), plays Exponents with a step hint, then Exponents, One-Step Equations, Four Quadrants and Two-Step Equations as full rounds of real taps and three measured questions of each other stop, plus 26 forced pictures and kinds (a point tapped on the grid, a reflected point tapped, ordered pairs and number lines picked, the balance hint, area pictures, a line plot, GCF and LCM stories, percent and ratio stories). Every question measured at 380x780: 48 px keys, choices and tap targets, nothing wider than the screen, no word cut off, and with a step hint open the question stays in view; stars saved. A review question from an earlier stop, or a question one level up or down, is not measured (another chunk's content; `lay.qsk`). Shots `gal-*` (23 of them; looked at them at 380x780).
- Seen in the shots and fixed: the first stop title "Exponents & Brackets" wrapped and the lock after it covered its second line (now "Exponents"); the balance picture in the hint pushed the keypad down (now 42% size); the four-quadrant read and reflect grids shrank to 70 px with the typed answer and 5-row pad (now choices); the area pictures shrank to 49 px with a decimal pad and a step hint open (the prompt line went, the picture is 116 high); the 7 x 7 tap grid (-3..3) was 340 units wide and its 48 px targets came out at 44.6 px on a 380 px phone (now -2..2).
- Found and not changed: because of `mastQ` (51-mastery.js), a child who does very well at a Crystal Caves coord 1, Volcano Lab ooo 1, Factor Factory fact 2, Crystal Caves geo 4, data 4 and story 8 stop is now sometimes asked the new level one up as a challenge question; that is the engine's rule for any skill that has a next level. Tap grids stop at -2..2 on a phone (a -3..3 grid does not fit 48 px targets in 316 units); reading, naming the quadrant and reflecting use -6..6. With a step hint open, the area pictures and the 13 x 13 grids shrink to 49 px and the labels are small (the hint repeats the numbers).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files [x] VER v111 (provisional) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on a parallel branch from A11b (v105); `m_galaxy` goes after Sky City; renumber VER at merge (rebuild, `git add -A`: bt, games and data files are renamed). Conflicts to expect: `38-skills.js` (`ooo`, `fact`, `geo`, `coord`, `data`, `story` lines and the new `alg`: keep every level of every chunk), `42-paths.js`, `23-viz.js` (the `rayLine` header lines and the return list), `35-gen-math-upper.js` (blocks appended), `tools/test.js` and `tools/smoke.js` (blocks appended before `// ---- report ----` and at the end of the main sequence), the `mx` tables and the world list of the Volcano Lab check.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v110 -> v111 at merge.

### P2-30 Spanish framework: unit data format, level 3 (spell), voice fallback (v108, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM S-frame, ROADMAP P2-30): Spanish vocabulary now lives in `src/data/d-es.js` as `ES_UNITS`, a list of units `{id, t, e, theme, items, cap?, spell?, mix?}`; an item is `[spanish, english, picture, article, art?, say?]` (format documented at the top of the file). `spanish` is the bare NFC lowercase word, `article` is `el`/`la`/`los`/`las`/`''` and shows in levels 1 and 2 ("la casa") and as a clue in level 3, `picture` is an emoji from the allow-list (or `c:#hex`, `n:12` as before), `art` is an optional art name that goes through `picFor(art, picture)`, `say` is an optional spoken override. `SPN` (unit id -> items) is still built from it, so balloons, Speed Race and the learn screen are unchanged.
- Old units: the 8 existing SPN units were migrated into the new format with their words, order, pictures and article `''` (the spec says existing units keep their words); `34-gen-es.js` `genEs` was rewritten around the format (`esFull`, `esReg`) and produces byte-identical questions: a test hashes 2,700 seeded questions (es_* levels 1-2, seeds 1-150) and the hash is the one taken from v104. `es_mix` draws from units flagged `mix:1` (the 8 old ones), so later units do not change its seeds unless a chunk flags them. The skills for the old units stay in `38-skills.js`; `esReg` (called from `d-es.js`) creates `SK.es_<id>` for any unit that has none, with `max` 3 when `spell:1`.
- Level 3 (spell): `genEs(set, 3)` makes a `spell` question for units with `spell:1`: picture, "Spell it in Spanish: el ... boy" (article and English gloss as the clue, so it works with no voice), blanks, the word spoken by Hear it (auto, Spanish voice). The answer is the bare word (articles are not spelled); only single words of a-z/ñáéíóúü, 12 letters or fewer, are asked (`esSpellable`). The accent row of the spell keyboard is now `ñ á é í ó ú ü ¿ ¡`, and the validator accepts ¿ and ¡. 9 keys fit 380 px. No shipped unit has `spell:1` yet.
- Stops added: none. CURRICULUM 2.3 lists level 3 only for es_house, es_clothes and es_school in `e_casa` (P2-31), so those chunks set `spell:1` and add the stops by world id. Saves stay keyed by stop id (`es_house3`); a test adds a fixture world with a level 3 stop to every save fixture and checks that no older stop changes stars or locks and the new one starts empty.
- Voice fallback: `esVoiceMode(voices, canSpeak, ms)` (12-speech.js, pure) gives `yes` (any voice whose lang starts `es` or `spa`), `no` (no speech, or voices exist and none is Spanish, or the list is still empty after 3 s) or `wait` (list empty in the first 3 s; some browsers fill it late, so it is asked again at every call, not cached). With `no`: `say(..., 'es')` stays silent (it used to hand Spanish text to the default voice) and shows the grown-up note once per session ("No Spanish voice on this tablet. Grown-ups: add one in the tablet's text-to-speech settings. The words are shown to read."); `esQFix(q)` runs after a Spanish question is drawn (rounds, placement, Speed Race): the speaker button becomes the word shown big (`.esbig`) and Hear it goes away. Listen questions already print the word, so they turn into read questions; level 3 has the picture and English clue. The learn screen shows the note too. English speech is untouched. The 90-app voice kick-start is unchanged.
- Tests (test.js 89 of 89): "Spanish framework P2-30": unit format (ids, fields, NFC, article set, picture in emoji-allow.txt or c:/n:, no duplicate word or gloss, skill title/emoji match, spell units spellable), builder output through `kindProblems` for levels 1-3 (200 seeds each, shipped units plus a fixture spell unit with articles and accents), accent keys typed give the exact word (niño, pingüino, canción, ¿cómo, ¡hola ...), old stops unchanged by seed (hash), `esVoiceMode` cases, late voices, no speech call without a Spanish voice, note once, stops list and fixture save safety. Smoke block `// P2-30` (fake unit from `tools/lib/es-fx.js`, 380x780): level 3 typed with the ñ key, no-voice path, 48 px keys and choices, no sideways scroll. Shots `es-spell`, `es-novoice`.
- Test expectations changed: none.
- Not done / ideas: the keyboard row is always shown for Spanish spelling including ¿ ¡, which only phrase spelling would need (no level 3 phrases are planned); spelling items with a space (hace sol) are skipped at level 3, the slot row has no space slot. The note text is English only like the rest of the chrome.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v109 -> v110 at merge.

### P3-07 Logic grids (v107, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.3, 7, 8): new puzzle type `logic` ("Logic", bands B, C, D; 12 levels then the framework's Endless; `allow` 2) in a new `src/js/80-bt-logic.js` (object `LG`: the pure rules, the hint engine, the screen, one `BT.reg`), its look in a new `src/css/49-bt-logic.css`, a drawn `logic` icon (a grid on a soft square with a tick and two crosses) in `15-icons.js`. The bank `PUZ.logic` (P3-06) is read only inside functions (A11); no puzzle-type code is referenced from start-up code. One framework addition: `api.win({..., note})` takes up to 6 lines of plain text that the win card shows (`.bt-sum`, 74-teasers.js and 48-teasers.css), used for the answer in words.
- What each band gets:
  | Band | Grid | Clues | Extra |
  |------|------|-------|-------|
  | B (1-2) | 3 children x 1 picture category: one 3x3 block, squares up to 92 px, picture heads (`picFor`), names as row heads | each clue card draws child + picture + a tick or cross disc, the sentence under it, a speak button; the intro is spoken | 2-3 clues; hints use only "clue mark" and "only place left" |
  | C (3-4) | 3 x 2: the classic grid (children x A, children x B, and B x A below, drawn turned so the block sits under A), squares 44-60 px | sentences with a speak button | link clues; hints add "link" |
  | D (5-6) | 4 x 2, the second ordered (age, house, race), squares 44-56 px | as C | or / comparison / one-apart clues; an Auto (auto-cross) toggle; hints add "two-way clue" and "chain" |
- Level and variety: level L uses bank entries 3(L-1)..3L-1; the seeded rnd (type|band|level|day) picks one of the three and a new set of names from the generator's 34 (`NAMES`, different first letters, band B at most 4 letters), so a level is one puzzle a day. Every name in the stored sentences is replaced at once as a whole word (a name appears in a sentence only as itself, 5.3 "Runtime"), so the clues stay true and the solution stays the bank's. Over 10 days 35, 36 and 35 of the 36 entries of bands B, C and D come up (the test asks for 30).
- Play: a tap cycles blank -> cross -> tick -> blank (5.3). A tick crosses the open squares of its row and column in its block (auto-cross, as the grader assumes; band D can switch it off); taking the tick away takes those crosses with it unless another tick still needs them. Undo (60 steps). Tapping a clue card strikes it through (and back); its speak button reads it. The row and column heads of the last tapped square are tinted, so a child sees which child and which item a square is. Win: every tick right (crosses are implied and filled in); the talk line and the win card show the answer, one line a child ("Raj eats the cupcake and lives at number 3.").
- Mistakes (5.3 "Check counts wrong marks without showing them unless hints are used"): a mark is never judged right or wrong as it goes in. A mark that breaks a rule the child can see is shown at once, gently: two ticks in a line, a line all crossed, a mark against a clue (is / not / or, a comparison or one-apart clue whose two order items are ticked, or its two children ticked together), or a mark against the marks through a tick. The square turns amber (`--try-tint`, a dashed `--try-ink` ring, a warning mark; never red), the clue card turns amber, and the talk line says it in words in `--try-ink` ("That does not fit clue 2: Nia does not read.", "Two ticks in the row for Ben. Ben has just one pet."). A cross on its way to a tick (the second tap) is judged only after a 0.9 s pause, so a child heading for a tick is not warned on the way. These are not mistakes. Check counts every mark that is not right (each one once, `seen`) as a mistake and says how many; after a hint it also rings them amber.
- Hints (5.3; tiers 1, 2, 3, then 3 again for the next mark), the grader's techniques from tools/puzzles/logic.js ported to the page (single marks from the child's grid): first a mark that is not right ("One mark in the yellow line is not right yet." / "This cross is not right: Ben paints." / puts it right), then with auto-cross off the rest of a tick's lines, then 1 clue mark ("Look at clue 2: Ben paints." / "Clue 2 says: Ben paints. So put a tick for Ben and paint." / "A tick for Ben and paint."), 2 only place left ("Every other hobby is crossed out for Ben, so Ben paints."), 3 link ("Jun plays basketball. The child who plays basketball does not live at number 2, so Jun does not live at number 2 either."), 4 two-way clue ("Clue 1 says: Ravi is younger than Luca. Ravi can only be 6, 7 or 8, so Luca is not 6.", "So Ravi cannot be the oldest: Ravi is not 9.", "Clue 3 is about two different children, so ..."), 5 chain ("What if Mia is 6? Then Leo is not 7 (clue 3), and Leo would have no age left. That cannot be, so Mia is not 6."). Tier 1 shades the clue card (scrolled into view) or the line in star yellow, tier 2 rings the square (and the tick and source mark of a link), tier 3 places that one mark, locked (star-yellow; a tick brings its auto-crosses). Tiers 1 and 2 never change the state. A last-resort "Try this square" exists but never happens on the bank (tested). The hint words come from a small copy of the generator's verbs and subjects; the test checks that they rebuild every is / is-not sentence of the bank word for word.
- Layout (section 8; the grid is sized to the space left, the clue list takes the rest and scrolls): portrait: talk line, grid, tools (Undo, Check, D: Auto), clue list; landscape (1024x600): talk line, tools and clue list left, grid right. When all three blocks would need squares under 44 px, the grid shows the children's two blocks side by side with the third block on a tab ("Children" | "Colour + race"), and when that is still under 44 px, one block per tab ("Colour" | "Race" | "Colour + race"). A tab whose block has an amber square gets an amber dot; a hint opens the tab of its square. What each screen gets (measured): 380x780: B 70 px squares; C the children's two blocks at 44 px with the third on a tab (the clue list keeps about 200 px); D one block per tab at 49 px. 600x960: B 92 px, C 60 px and D 51 px, all three blocks. 1024x600: B 92 px, C all three blocks at 56 px, D the children's two blocks at 56 px plus a tab. The stage never scrolls; the clue list does.
- State (`api.saveState` after every move; `LG.fit` rejects a state that does not belong to the puzzle, which then starts fresh): `{v: '0' blank, '1' tick, '2' cross per square of blocks (0,1), (0,2), (1,2) (n x n each, row = item of the first category), a: '1' where a tick put the cross, h: undo steps [[square, old v, old a], ...], miss, u: struck clues, hn: hint squares (locked), seen: squares Check counted, ac: auto-cross, hu: a hint was used, tab}`. Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- Tests: named check `logic UI P3-07`: registered with bands BCD and 12 levels; `LG.NAMES` is the generator's list; 360 made puzzles (3 bands x 12 levels x 10 days): `make()` deterministic, the bank entry is one of the level's three, the puzzle is that entry (sizes, items, solution), new names from the list with different first letters (B at most 4 letters), each sentence is the bank sentence with the names replaced (the test's own word-by-word reading), the clue structure unchanged, each child named in a clue is there and no old name is left, and (a share of them) the test's own brute force over every assignment finds exactly one solution, the stored one; the summary names every child; the hint words rebuild every is / is-not sentence of the bank. Every bank puzzle (108, and the 36 of band D again with auto-cross off): exactly one solution by the brute force; `solve()` tapped in wins; the hint chain from a fresh grid finishes it: tiers 1 and 2 leave the state byte for byte, all three tiers point at the same square, only tier 3 places, the mark is the solution's, and nothing else changes except a tick's auto-crosses; no "guess" or "wrong" step; hint words without undefined, ending with a full stop, tier 1 naming its clue; every technique seen per band, none harder than the band allows. Moves: first tap a cross, second a tick that crosses exactly its row and column, undo, the third tap takes the tick and its crosses away, undo all the way gives the fresh grid; a mark against a clue is a conflict with words; Check counts it once; the hint finds it first and tier 3 puts it right and locks it; two ticks in a line (auto-cross off) are shown; a saved state round-trips (same next hint) and 13 damaged states do not fit. `make()` well under 5 ms. Mutation-checked: replacing only the first name of a sentence fails it.
- Smoke block `// P3-07` (end of the main sequence, midway save): 380x780 grade 1 (band B) and 1024x600 grade 5 (band D), Home -> Brain Teasers -> Logic -> level 1 by real taps: band B draws every clue as child + picture + tick or cross; the speak button of clue 1 speaks the clue; tapping a clue strikes it through; a mark against a clue is shown gently (amber tint exactly `--try-tint`, not `--danger`, dashed ring, warning mark, the line in `--try-ink` saying "does not fit clue", the clue card amber, no mistake yet); Check counts it; Undo takes it away; hint tiers 1 and 2 (something highlighted, then a ringed square and different words; the grid unchanged); one tick; back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes the same puzzle and state; the rest tapped in (tabs tapped when needed); win card with one summary line per child; 1 star (two hints) and level 2 saved. Every screen: 48 px targets (grid squares 44 px, section 8), nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the page does not scroll, no emoji outside the pictures (`.lg-pic`).
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-lg-b bt-lg-c bt-lg-d bt-lg-d-hint bt-lg-win`, three sizes each, played by real taps through `tools/lib/bt-lg.js`): looked at bt-lg-b at 380x780 and 1024x600, bt-lg-c at 380x780 and 1024x600, bt-lg-d at 380x780 (twice) and 1024x600 (twice), bt-lg-d-hint at 600x960 and 380x780, bt-lg-win at 380x780. Fixed from looking: "Logic Grid" was cut off in the top bar (now "Logic"); band D on a phone squeezed 34 px squares and "1st2nd3rd4th" ran together (now one block per tab at 48 px, heads sized to the square); on 1024x600 band D first showed one block per tab (now both children's blocks side by side); the tools wrapped (now 48 px buttons, "Auto"); the clue list was crushed on a phone (the grid now leaves room for two clues); band B at 1024x600 was small (squares up to 92 px); a child ticking a square saw a warning on the way through the cross (now judged after a pause).
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 87 of 87, smoke OK, no console errors, no network). The machine was busy (load 13), so a run took about 40 minutes. Runs: the first passed; the second (after renaming the tabs to the grid's category names) failed only the existing random Crystal Caves layout step (`fop3` "count 11 balloons", question area 1 px too tall at 380x780; P2-12, not touched here); the third passed. Sizes: index.html 1,439,580 -> 1,489,949 bytes (+50.4 KB: 80-bt-logic.js 41 KB, 49-bt-logic.css 8 KB, the icon and the win-card note), data.v107.js 276,381 (unchanged apart from the version line), total 1,766,330.
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now includes Logic for bands B-D; the Puzzle of the Day pick can now land on Logic.)
- Deviations: band B uses the grid, not "cards: tap a pet, tap a child, a line connects them" (5.3 UI): the band B clue pictures (child + picture + tick or cross) are the grid's marks, the hints and Check work on marks, and the grid teaches the C/D way of working; recorded as "As built" in BRAIN_TEASERS 5.3. The type name is "Logic" (the catalog's "Logic Grid" does not fit the phone top bar).
- Not done: the framework's "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule (P3-01). No keyboard play (sudoku has digits and arrows; the grid squares are buttons, so Tab and Enter work).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files (new 80-bt-logic.js, 49-bt-logic.css, tools/lib/bt-lg.js; the icon in 15-icons.js; the win-card note in 74-teasers.js and 48-teasers.css; tests, smoke, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v107 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] size (see Ideas and issues: close to the 1.5 MB budget) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-07-logic-ui from 0f31196 (v103). Overlaps are appends: tools/test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), tools/smoke.js (one block after the P3-03 block), tools/shots.js (one `Object.assign(SCREENS, ...)` block before `main`), 15-icons.js (one icon before `path-math`), 74-teasers.js (`win` / `winCard` take a note), 48-teasers.css (two lines at the end), built files (rebuild, `git add -A`). A11b moves `7x-bt-*` / `8x-bt-*` files: `80-bt-logic.js` is named for that. Renumber VER at merge.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v108 -> v109 at merge.

### P2-36 Discover path shell and Science K-2 (v109, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM 2.4 and 6, ROADMAP P2-36): new path `disc` ("Discover", graded, `noPlace`) in `42-paths.js` with world `d_nature` "Nature Trail" (grade K) and its three stops in CURRICULUM order: sci_life 1 Living Things, sci_earth 1 Sky & Earth, sci_phys 1 Push, Pull & Stuff, then the boss. Skills `sci_life`, `sci_earth`, `sci_phys` (area `disc`, max 1, bank-backed) in `38-skills.js`; skill icons in `ICO_SK`. geo_world 1 and civ 1 (P2-37) join Nature Trail later; worlds `d_lab` and `d_cosmos` are P2-37.
- Content: new `src/data/d-disc.js` (data file, built into data.v109.js). 208 level 1 items, each with `grade:'K-2'`, a `topic` tag, a hint that teaches (per-topic default, or its own) and say text. sci_life 95 (living 14, babies 16, covers 13, habitat 16, plants 14, needs 10, senses 12), sci_earth 62 (weather 16, seasons 12, daynight 11, sunmoon 10, landwater 13), sci_phys 51 (push 12, stuff 15, soundlight 12, solid 12). Pictures (`em`, through picFor, emoji fallback) only show the subject of a question, never the answer, and no item needs a picture or a map.
- Home: card from the existing generic path loop plus a drawn `HM_ART.disc` (magnifier over a leaf and a star), subject colour `--disc` already in the tokens. Placement: `placePaths()` skips paths with `noPlace`. Grown-ups report, strands (Science) and mastery were already generic.
- Saves: no migration (LATEST stays 6). `migrate()` now also gives every path in PATHS an empty `S.maps[p]` before its version check, so a save written before Discover gets `S.maps.disc={}` (the load in 42-paths.js already did this at start-up; migrate() is what tests call after loadSave).
- Tests (test.js): "Discover science K-2 P2-36": bankProblems, grade and topic tags, one right answer, hint never names the answer, 3,000 seeded questions per skill with kindProblems, path/world/stop structure, 7 fixtures x 7 grades with Discover added (no star moves, nothing re-locks, S.maps.disc empty, a Discover star saves under sci_life1 and moves nothing else), Home card, content is in the data file not index.html. ### P2-17 Tracing letters A-Z and a-z (v104, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: `src/data/d-trace.js` gains the sets `upper` (A-Z, "big letter") and `lower` (a-z, "little letter"), 52 letters in neat school print (manuscript, ball and stick) drawn to sit on Andika bold; `TR.LV[2] = 'upper'`, `TR.LV[3] = 'lower'`; skill `trace` max 1 -> 3 (title 'Tracing'); ABC Beach gets `trace 2` "Trace Big Letters" after abc 2 and `trace 3` "Trace Little Letters" after syl 1 (CURRICULUM 2.2: abc 1, abc 2, trace 2, abc 3, syl 1, trace 3, abc 4). Letters in abc 1 order (`ABCO`), rounds of 5 as for digits; tolerance by grade as before.
- Handwriting model (in the d-trace.js header, one line per letter): 1-4 strokes, top to bottom, left to right, lift between strokes; capitals E F H I T built from downs and slides (I and T slide across the top first), B D P R down then the bumps, C G O Q S curve back from the top, M N V W one stroke with corners after the first down; lowercase a d g q = c-curve then the line, b h m n p r = down then (from the line) round/over, e slides across then curves back, i j end with a dot (a one-point stroke), g j p q y reach the descender line, f and t start with their stem and end with the crossbar. Lines: capitals top 10, mid 50, base 90; lowercase top 10, mid 34, base 75, desc 94 (Andika's proportions). Points 7.1 to 13.6 units apart along curves. Every letter checked over the font on a debug overlay at 3.4x (ink depth along each stroke, uncovered ink) and on the new sheets at 2x.
- Reading colour: a skill may set `lvArea` {level: area}; `skArea(sk, lv)` (38-skills.js) is what the map stop (`nInfo`), the round's subject colour (`qSubj`), the bonus game and the activity log (`ACT.qStart(sk, now, hidden, lv)`) read. `SK.trace.area` stays 'math' (digits, Tiny Town) with `lvArea: {2: 'read', 3: 'read'}`. The prompt shows "Trace the little letter j." and speaks "the little letter J" (abc 1's way of naming letters).
- Save safety: stars keyed by stop id, no format change. The new stops sit between older ones, and a save that had abc 3 (or abc 4) open must keep it open, so a stop of a skill marked `side` (tracing) never holds back the stop after it (`sideOpen` in 42-paths.js `unlocked`): it opens with the tracing stop. The map still leads through the tracing stop (it is the first open unplayed stop). This also stops Tiny Town's boss waiting for trace 1 (P2-16's test had excluded that boss from its re-lock check).
- Tests: new named check "tracing letters P2-17": sets, order, names, lines; every letter's strokes valid (box, dense points, length, stroke count and first direction per the model, loops start at their top and close smoothly, no spikes but at corners, curve points 7-15 apart, ascender/x-height/descender heights, Q's tail, dots last over the stem between top and mid); 8,404 synthetic traces at tolerance 14 and 10: ideal, noisy and 8 px jitter pass; reversed, noisy reversed, off the path, zigzag, random scribble, far start fail; taps ignored; half kept and finished; every other stroke traced in place of each stroke fails (wrong order); dot taps pass, taps away and lines from the dot fail; 3,000 questions per level (kindProblems, prompt, labels one per letter, 4 or 5 board lines), rounds in order and reproducible; stops in CURRICULUM order, trace areas per level (map, round, activity log), placement never asks them; 27 saves (ABC Beach states x grades -1/0/1 and fixtures) with and without the new stops: stars unchanged, none on the new stops, nothing re-locks, a finished ABC Beach shows both NEW and open and the map opens where it did, otherwise where it did or on a new tracing stop before it. Shown to fail on a reversed c, a moved i dot, a bad k point, and (save check) without `sideOpen`.
- Test expectations changed: "ABC Beach (P2-15)" stop list `abc1 abc2 abc3 syl1 abc4` -> `abc1 abc2 trace2 abc3 syl1 trace3 abc4` (and trace2/trace3 added to its no-stars-on-new-stops list); "tracing P2-16" `SK.trace.max` 1 -> 3 (its area is still 'math').
- Smoke (`// P2-17` block): touch at 380x780, Pre-K: Trace Little Letters opened from the ABC Beach map, both tracing stops and the round chrome in the reading colour (`.s-read`, `--c` = `--read`), round fixed to a i g k y (a dot, two tails) x 3 passes traced with real touch moves, 5/5, 3 stars in the save.
- Shots: `trace-upper-sheet`, `trace-lower-sheet` (every stroke over the Andika letter at 30%, letters scaled by H / h, desc line; full page), `trace-letter-E` (level 1, two strokes done), `trace-letter-j` (level 2, the dot next), `trace-letter-y` (level 3, half of the tail stroke traced); `trace-sheet` is digits only. `SHOTS_SCALE=2` shoots at 2x (`openApp` takes `scale`). Looked at: both sheets at 600x960 x2, trace-letter screens at 380x780.
- Sizes: index.html 1,377,790, data.v104.js 247,397, total 1,625,187.

### P3-05 MathDoku (v106, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.2, 7, 8): new puzzle type `mdoku` ("MathDoku", bands BCD, 12 levels then the framework's Endless, `allow` 2) in a new `src/js/79-bt-mdoku.js` (object `MD`: the pure rules, the screen, one `BT.reg`), its look in a new `src/css/49-bt-mdoku.css`, and a drawn `mdoku` icon (soft square, a thick cage corner, a plus and a times sign) in `15-icons.js`. Nothing in the framework or in Sudoku changed. The bank `PUZ.mathdoku` (P3-04) is read only inside functions (A11). The file is named `79-bt-*` so A11b can move it with the other puzzle types; nothing at startup refers to `MD`.
- Shared code with Sudoku: considered and not done. The two screens share the approach (padHTML pad with dots for "left", the amber clash look, notes, undo, hint marks, saveState, the layout function), but the MathDoku grid has no boxes, a label layer and svg cage lines, so a shared helper would have meant reworking 78-bt-sudoku.js; it stays untouched and MathDoku has its own code modelled on it.
- What each band gets:
  | Band | Grid | Operations | Keys | Bank (P3-04) |
  |------|------|------------|------|--------------|
  | B (1-2) | 3x3 | + | 1 2 3, Erase, Undo (no notes: three numbers need none); the intro, wrong lines and the win are spoken | grades 1-2 |
  | C (3-4) | 4x4 | + − × | 1-4, Erase, Notes, Undo | grades 2-4 |
  | D (5-6) | 5x5 on levels 1-6, 6x6 on levels 7-12 | + − × ÷ | 1-5 or 1-6, Erase, Notes, Undo | grades 2-5 |
- Levels and variety: level L uses bank entries 3(L-1)..3L-1 (sorted by score, so difficulty rises); the seeded rnd (type|band|level|day) picks one of the three and one of the 8 rotations and mirrors (the same table as tools/puzzles/mathdoku.js `SYMS`). Numbers are never relabelled (the arithmetic would break); a turned cage keeps its op and target (− and ÷ do not care about order) and its label moves to its new top-left square. The 12 levels of a day are 12 different bank puzzles, so no grid repeats across levels on one day. Next-day repeats of a level are chance (3 puzzles x 8 forms: about 1 in 24; 15 of 504 in the test). The puzzle stores the result (`cg` cages `{c, o, t}`, `s` solution), the bank index `k`, the transform `x`, the grade. `make()` well under 1 ms.
- Play: tap a square, then a number. Cage lines are an svg over the grid of buttons: thin `--line` inside a cage, thick `--bt-shade` between cages, the grid's edge a ring. The cage label (target and sign, e.g. `12×`, `3−`, `2÷`) sits in the top-left square in ink, 14-22 px (15 px on a 6x6 at 380x780, 18 px at 1024x600). Single-square cages are givens printed in ink. Never judged right or wrong while playing: a number already in its row or column is a clash (both squares `--try-tint`, a dashed `--try-ink` ring, a warning mark, "Two 3s in this row. Change one of them."; one mistake); a full cage that does not make its number turns its label amber with a warning mark and a dashed ring, and the line says why in words ("2 + 3 = 5, but this cage needs 6. Change a number."; one mistake; a clash and a miss from one tap count once). A cage that is filled and works pops and says so ("1 + 3 = 4. That cage works!"). Notes (C, D) as in Sudoku (a number placed clears it from the notes of its row and column, undoable); Undo goes back 60 steps; hint-filled squares lock (star-yellow). Hardware digits, Backspace, arrows, N for notes. Speech turns signs into words ("12 times", "2 plus 3 equals 5").
- Hints (5.2; tier 1, 2, 3, then 3 again): the steps follow the bank grader (`grade()` in tools/puzzles/mathdoku.js), so every bank puzzle finishes without guessing (tested): a number that is not right first (a clashing one, then one in a missed cage: "One number in this cage is not right yet." / "This 3 does not belong here." / fill); a cage with one gap its arithmetic fixes ("This 60× cage needs just one more number." / "The numbers in this cage multiply to 60. It has 3 and 5, so the last number is 4: 3 × 5 × 4 = 60."); a row or column with one gap; a cage single, the cage with the fewest ways first ("Look at the 3− cage. Which numbers can make it?" / "Only 1 and 4 make 3− in two squares. 4 is already in this square's row or column, so it is 1."; up to four number sets are listed, e.g. "12× in three squares can be 1, 3 and 4, or 2, 2 and 3."); a hidden single in a line; then a cage in a line or a naked pair until a single shows ("Every way to make 3+ puts a 1 in the marked squares of this column, so no other square of the column can be 1. Now ..."). Tier 1 shades the cage (or the line) yellow, tier 2 rings the square in star yellow and selects it, tier 3 fills it.
- Layout (section 8): the board is a square sized to the space left; portrait: talk line, board, pad; landscape: board left, talk line and pad right. The pad is a 60-column grid so digits and tools share rows evenly. 6x6 on a phone: at 380x780 the squares are 56 px (all six digits in one row of 50 px keys, Erase Notes Undo below), so no zoom box is needed; at 1024x600 68 px; 5x5 at 380x780 67 px; 3x3 up to 96 px.
- State (`api.saveState` after every move; `MD.fit` rejects a state that does not belong to the puzzle, which then starts fresh): `{v: entries as a digit string ('0' empty or given), m: note bit masks ([] when none), sel, miss, hn: hint-filled squares, h: undo steps [[square, old value, old notes], ...], nt: notes on}`. Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- Tests: named check `mathdoku UI P3-05` (540 puzzles: 3 bands x 12 levels x 15 days): registered with bands BCD and 12 levels; `make()` deterministic; size by band and level, the bank index inside 3(L-1)..3L-1, 12 different bank puzzles and 12 different grids a day; the stored puzzle equals the bank puzzle under its symmetry (the test's own decode and the dev-time `SYMS` table), grade kept; independently of the app: the solution is a Latin square, cages cover every square once, ascending, connected, ops allowed for the band, − and ÷ on two squares, `=` exactly on singles, every target recomputed from the solution, exactly one solution by verify-banks.js `countCages`, and the dev-time grader solves the turned puzzle to that solution (with the same grade in all 540); all 8 transforms seen; `solve()` fills every empty square once with the solution; hints from the start to the end: tiers 1 and 2 leave the state byte for byte and point at the same square, tier 1 shades the whole cage for cage steps, tier 3 fills exactly that square with its number, no "guess" step, last / cage-last / cage / hidden steps and band D eliminations seen; moves (first 3 days): a clash shows in `conflicts`, counts one, the hint finds it, undo restores the state byte for byte; a cage miss (an empty cage filled without a clash but missing its target) counts once, shows in `misses`, the hint points into it, undo takes it back; a right number goes in, a given takes nothing; notes in C and D go in, are cleared by a number in their line and come back with undo, none in B; hinted squares cannot be erased; saved state: puzzle and state through JSON fit and give the same next hint, eleven damaged states do not fit; `spoken()` words; `make()` under 5 ms. Runs in about 4 s.
- Smoke block `// P3-05` (end of the main sequence, midway save): 380x780 grade 1 (3x3, band B, level 1), 1024x600 grade 5 (6x6, band D, level 7 opened in the record) and 380x780 grade 5 (the same 6x6 on a phone): Home -> Brain Teasers -> MathDoku -> the level by real taps; a clash (two or more amber squares, background exactly `--try-tint` and not `--danger`, dashed ring, warning mark, the line in `--try-ink`; one mistake), Undo takes it away and keeps the mistake; a cage miss (amber label with a warning mark and a dashed ring, "..., but this cage needs N. Change a number."; two mistakes now), Undo takes it back; 6x6: Notes put a 1 and a 3 in an empty square; hint tiers 1 and 2 (a cage or line shaded, then a ringed square and a different line, board unchanged); a right move, back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes the same puzzle and state, the rest tapped in, win card, 1 star (two hints) and the next level saved. Every screen: 48 px targets including board squares, cage labels at least 14 px, nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the puzzle fits without scrolling, no emoji chrome. Taps through `tools/lib/bt-md.js` (move, rest, clash, miss).
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-md-hub bt-md-b bt-md-c bt-md-d5 bt-md-d6 bt-md-hint bt-md-win`, three sizes each, played by real taps): looked at bt-md-d6 at 380x780 and 1024x600 (notes, a missed 8+ cage), bt-md-b at 380x780 and 1024x600 (clash and miss on a 3x3), bt-md-c at 600x960, bt-md-hint at 380x780, bt-md-win at 600x960, bt-md-hub (levels) and bt-md-d5 at 380x780. Fixed from looking: band B first had all five keys in one row with icon-only Erase and Undo; now 1 2 3 above Erase and Undo with their words.
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 86 of 86, smoke OK) on the second run; the first run failed only the existing Crystal Caves `fop3` layout step (P2-12: a random "count 13 flowers (frames)" question measured taller than the screen on a busy machine), with every P3-05 step passing. index.html 1,439,580 -> 1,487,116 bytes (+47.5 KB: 79-bt-mdoku.js 39.9 KB, a third of it comments and hint words; 49-bt-mdoku.css 7.3 KB; see Ideas and issues), data.v106.js 233,759 (unchanged), total 1,720,875.
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now includes MathDoku in bands B-D; the Puzzle of the Day pick can now land on MathDoku.)
- Not done: the "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule is still the framework's open item (P3-01).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files (new 79-bt-mdoku.js, 49-bt-mdoku.css, tools/lib/bt-md.js; the icon in 15-icons.js; tests, smoke, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v106 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).

Overlaps are appends: tools/test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), tools/smoke.js (one block at the end of the main sequence), tools/shots.js (one `Object.assign(SCREENS, ...)` block before `main`), 15-icons.js (one icon before `path-math`), BRAIN_TEASERS 5.2 (as-built notes), built files (rebuild, `git add -A`). If A11b lands first, 79-bt-mdoku.js joins its puzzle-type file by its name pattern. Renumber VER at merge.

### A11b Puzzle code and games in separate precached files (v105, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- Why now (ROADMAP A11, risk R1): index.html was back at 1,394,701 bytes (warning 1.2 MB, limit 1.5 MB) after the 5th grade worlds, three Brain Teaser puzzle types and tracing, with 6th grade worlds, sudoku/MathDoku/logic-grid UIs, tangrams and more games still to come. About 1.14 MB of it was `src/js`.
- What moved (sizes of the sources): the Brain Teaser puzzle types `75-bt-maze.js`, `76-bt-pattern.js`, `77-bt-number.js` (122 KB) and their CSS `49-bt-maze.css`, `49-bt-number.css`, `49-bt-pattern.css` (18 KB) into `bt.v105.js`; the Game Room `60-blocks.js`, `62-hill.js`, `64-gtimer.js`, `66-colornum.js`, `68-lowpoly.js`, `70-art.js`, `72-shapes.js` (187 KB) into `games.v105.js`. The puzzle types alone would have left index.html at about 1.25 MB, still over the warning, so the games went too (A11's own note named them as the next lever). Nothing moved needs anything at start-up: the analysis listed every top-level name these files define and where else it is used; the only uses outside them are inside functions that run when a puzzle or game opens (`gameRoom()` calls `garageS`, `colorMenu`, `bbMenu`; `view()` already calls `stopHC`/`gtStop` behind `typeof`; Home only calls `BT.list()`, which is in the framework). `rng()` lives in `62-hill.js` and is used only by game files.
- What stayed: the framework `74-teasers.js` and `48-teasers.css` (Home's card needs `BT.list()`, `view()` calls `BT.stop()`), `58-playtime.js` (game time, `gameRoom()`), `40-games.css` (a mix of Home, map, shop, boss and game styles, and in the middle of the cascade, so moving it would change rule order), `47-trace.js` (rounds use it).
- Build rule (`tools/build.py`, `PARTS`): a list of (file stem, regular expression on whole file names in `src/js` and `src/css`). `bt`: `\d+-bt-[\w-]+\.(js|css)`, so every `NN-bt-*.js` and `NN-bt-*.css` goes to `bt.<VER>.js` with no build change. `games`: the seven file names above or `\d+-game-[\w-]+\.js`. A part file is a header comment, then (if the part has CSS) one line that appends `<style id="css-bt">` with its CSS to `<head>`, then its JS in filename order, then the last line `var BT_VER='v105';` (`GAMES_VER` for games). The build writes one `<script src>` per part into the shell's new `<!--@@PARTS@@-->` marker, adds the files to the service worker's precache list, and deletes every other `name.vNN.js` at the repo root.
- Load order: index.html's code, `bt.v105.js`, `games.v105.js`, `data.v105.js`, boot; all plain classic scripts, so it works over file:// and they share one global scope. Parts may use anything in index.html's code while loading (`BT.reg`, `ICONS`, `UI`, `padHTML` ...); the data file still loads after all code, so data files keep calling code helpers at load time. A `function` in a part is no longer hoisted into index.html's script, so index.html's code may call part code only once the app has started, never while loading (the test checks this). The part CSS lands after index.html's CSS, where `49-bt-*.css` already sorted, so the cascade is unchanged; nothing is on screen before boot, so the first paint has it.
- Guard: `appFiles()` in `90-app.js` lists `bt`, `games`, `data` with their stamps; `dataReady()` is true only if all three equal the page's VER. `dataMissing()` is unchanged on screen ("Star School needs to finish updating ... Try again") and logs one line naming every bad file, for example `bt file missing (app v105)` or `games file v1 does not match (app v105)`. No save change.
- Sizes (bytes): index.html 1,394,701 -> 1,068,449; bt.v105.js 140,773; games.v105.js 187,379; data.v105.js 233,759 (unchanged); total 1,630,360 (was 1,628,460 in two files). Head room to the 1.2 MB warning: about 130 KB.
- Budgets (`tools/test.js`): index.html unchanged (warn 1.2 MB, fail 1.5 MB); each part file warn 400 KB, fail 800 KB; data warn 1 MB, fail 2 MB; all files together warn 2 MB, fail 3 MB. `tools/check.sh` prints every file the page loads and the total.
- Tests: the check `data file split A11` became `code split A11b (and data file split A11)`: index.html's scripts are code, bt, games, data, boot in that order with no async/defer; index.html's code and CSS are exactly src minus the part files; the longest line of every moved file is nowhere in index.html; `74-teasers.js`, `58-playtime.js`, `90-app.js` are not in a part; every `*-bt-*.js` is in bt; each part file holds its JS byte for byte, its CSS as the `<style>` before the code, and its stamp once as the last line; `appFiles()` lists the same files as PARTS + data; then, in a fresh Node `vm` context with each file run as its own script (like the page, so nothing leaks from the other checks and nothing is hoisted across files): the code alone loads (`dataReady()` false), code + data without the parts loads, everything loads with all five puzzle types registered, ten game functions defined, the data globals as before and the parts' CSS added; each file missing or stamped `v1` makes `dataReady()` false and names that file; sw.js precaches exactly the three files; no stale `name.vNN.js` at the root; the budgets. Shown to fail on a stale `bt.v1.js`, on index.html code that calls game code while loading, on a puzzle file left inline, on data loaded before the parts, and on a precache list without the games file. `tools/test.js` reads PARTS from build.py and evaluates sources in the built order (`js` = index.html's src/js, each part's src/js, src/data).
- Smoke (block `// A11`): over file:// the page loads exactly `bt`, `games`, `data.v105.js` in that order; the data checks as before; the five puzzle types are registered, `<style id="css-bt">` is in the head, and a maze, a pattern and a number pyramid open from the Brain Teasers hub (grade 3) with their part CSS applied (`.mz` flex, `.pt-chs` grid, `.bn` flex); the Game Room opens with its three games. Guard, in a temp copy: data file missing (Try again with it back starts the app), data file of another release, `bt.v105.js` missing (Try again with it back starts the app and a maze opens from the hub), `games.v105.js` of another release: each shows Try again, progress unchanged (stars 85, coins 64), the App log line names the file, and the only console errors are the missing file's.
- Offline check by hand (Playwright, midway save, `python3 -m http.server` at the repo root; "offline" = context offline and the server stopped, since the context's offline switch does not cover the worker's own fetches; script not committed): online, the worker installs and takes control, and cache `starschool-v105` holds `./`, index.html, bt, games and data v105, manifest and icons. Offline, reload starts v105 (`dataReady()` true, the five types registered, `<style id="css-bt">` in) with no request reaching the server; a maze, a pattern and a pyramid open from the Brain Teasers hub, and the Game Room's Coloring button opens the Art Studio; no console errors. Then `bt.v105.js` deleted from the cache and the HTTP cache cleared, still offline: reload shows the guard (App log `bt file missing (app v105)`, stars 85 and coins 64 unchanged, one `net::ERR_FAILED` console line for the script); back online, Try again starts the app and the worker has cached `bt.v105.js` again. Not verified: an upgrade from a v101 install over GitHub Pages, a real tablet, Safari/iOS.
- Files: `tools/build.py`, `src/shell.html`, `src/boot.js` (comment), `src/js/90-app.js` (A11 block), `tools/test.js` (loader and constants at the top, the A11 check), `tools/smoke.js` (`// A11` block), `tools/check.sh` (size report), docs (CONTRIBUTING 3.2, 3.3, 4, 6; ROADMAP principle 8, A11, 7.1, R1, R4; BRAIN_TEASERS 3); built `index.html`, `sw.js`, new `bt.v105.js`, `games.v105.js`, `data.v105.js` (renamed from v101).
- For later chunks: a new puzzle type named `src/js/NN-bt-<id>.js` (CSS `src/css/49-bt-<id>.css`) goes into `bt.<VER>.js` by itself; a new game named `src/js/NN-game-<name>.js` goes into `games.<VER>.js` (its CSS stays in index.html's CSS). Neither may be called by index.html's code while loading, only from click handlers and screens. A test that looks for puzzle or game code in the built output must read `PART_FILES` (test.js), not index.html. A new part file needs one line in PARTS, one in `appFiles()` and the expected stems in the A11b check and smoke.
- Merge: built on branch A11b-split from f8adabe (v101) while other chunks are in flight; VER v105 is provisional. Renumbering renames all three files: rebuild and `git add -A`. Conflicts to expect: `tools/build.py` (VER), `tools/test.js` (top constants, the A11 check), `src/js/90-app.js` (A11 block), `tools/smoke.js` (`// A11` block), built files (rebuild). A chunk in flight that adds `src/js/7x-bt-*.js` or `src/css/49-bt-*.css` lands in the bt file with no change; one that edits a game file keeps working (the file name is in PARTS); a test it adds that reads index.html for puzzle or game code must switch to the part file.

### P2-13 6th grade: world Sky City (v102, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM M-6a and 2.1 row 15, ROADMAP P2-13): new world `m_sky` "Sky City" (grade 6, cloud icon, right after `m_caves`; Galaxy Core, P2-14, follows later) with the 8 stops in CURRICULUM order: ratio 1 Ratios and Tables, ratio 2 Unit Rates, int 1 Integers on a Line, ratio 3 Percent of a Number, int 2 Absolute Value, ratio 4 Fractions and Percents, fop 7 Divide Fractions, int 3 Integer Add & Subtract, then the boss. New skills `ratio` (4 levels) and `int` (3 levels); `fop` extended 6 -> 7 with a dispatcher in `38-skills.js` (old levels untouched, checked by seed).
- Generators: one block `// ---- P2-13 Sky City ----` at the end of `35-gen-math-upper.js` (names `sky`). ratio 1: ratio of blue to orange dots (VIZ.tenFrame, coprime pairs, four choices none an equal ratio), a ratio from a sentence ("a : b" or "a to b"), an equal-ratio line with one blank. ratio 2: price of one (whole cents in 5s, decimal pad `eq:'value'`), a rate per hour or minute, which is the better buy (two deals in the prompt, buttons Deal A / Deal B). ratio 3: part of a number (10, 20, 25, 50, 75 half the time, else any multiple of 5; whole parts, numbers 20-400) and the whole from a part and a percent. ratio 4: match four fractions, decimals or percents (`match` kind), fraction to percent, fraction to decimal (bottoms to 100), decimal to percent, percent to decimal, percent to a fraction in simplest form. int 1: tap a number on -10..10 (4-5 targets at least 4 apart), compare (`<` `=` `>`) with the line, which of two temperatures is colder or warmer, order three (`order` kind, up or down). int 2: absolute value, opposite, distance between two integers (line with two dots). int 3: add and subtract integers with operands and answer in -20..20 (all four sign cases), hint of three steps with the number line (start dot, the rule for the sign, the hop). fop 7: fraction / fraction, mixed number / fraction, fraction / mixed number, answer in simplest form (a mixed number in the three-slot pad when it is more than 1, else a fraction; never a whole number; `eq` off so 6/20 is refused).
- Step hints: every question has a `skyHint` (steps of `ST.step`, plain text, a spoken line that says "negative"), the last step ends in a "?" and none states the answer. int 3's hop picture appears only in the last step and shows the arc, not a number.
- Shared engine edits: none. Negative entry already existed (num `pad:'neg'`, the +/- key under the pad, P1-03); `VIZ.numLine` already draws negative ranges with the minus sign. Both were tested (the pad key only on `neg` questions, typing -5 with real taps in smoke, a -10..10 line is deterministic, tokens only, tap targets at least 48 units).
- Tests (test.js 83 of 83): "Sky City P2-13" with 3,000 seeded questions per new level (8 levels), maths recomputed independently from the label and the picture (dots counted in the svg, equal-ratio multipliers, unit prices in cents, percents, every match pair, tap targets read back from the svg, marks on the line, hop start and end read from the arc, mixed-number division by fractions, simplest form enforced by `same()`), hints (steps, "?" last, never the answer), say, labels, kindProblems zero, reproducible, old levels unchanged, stops read from CURRICULUM row 15, block has no Math.random / raw hex / name without the `sky` prefix; and a save check (fixtures x 9 grades + 3 synthetic saves) that ends the path at Sky City while it runs: no star moves, nothing re-locks, the map opens where it did except for the new world (a 6th grader with nothing further on now starts in Sky City, startWorld).
- Test expectations changed: the Decimal Desert P2-10 stop check expected `SK.fop.max` 6; it is 7 (level 7 is this world's). The Volcano Lab P2-11 fixture check removes the later worlds `m_desert` and `m_caves` to compare with and without its world; it now removes `m_sky` too. Nothing weakened.
- Smoke (`// P2-13` block, last in the main sequence): a 6th grader whose earlier worlds are finished opens the map on Sky City (position by id, after `m_caves`), plays Ratios and Tables with a step hint, then Ratios and Tables, Integers on a Line, Absolute Value and Integer Add & Subtract as full rounds of real taps, measures every question at 380x780 (48 px keys, choices, tiles and tap targets, no cut-off words, no sideways scroll; with a step hint open the question stays in view), 24 forced pictures and kinds (a match of each kind, a tapped number, three numbers ordered, a negative answer typed with the +/- key and read from the box before the check key), stars saved. Shots `sky-*` (looked at them at 380x780).
- Seen in the shots and fixed: the better-buy choices with the whole deal as button text ("9 stickers for $26.10") wrapped to four lines; the deals are now in the prompt and the buttons say Deal A and Deal B. int 3's third step first pushed the equation out of the card; its text is two lines now.
- Found and not changed: the tap line shows -10..10 in 5s, so the child counts ticks for an answer such as -8 (labels on every tick do not fit 290 units); the map icon for every math world is the same math icon.

### P3-06 Logic grid generator and banks (v103, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.3 and 6): new dev-time generator `tools/puzzles/logic.js`, registered as `logic` in the `build-banks.js` TYPES registry (seed `star-school-logic-1`); `verify-banks.js` gains `verifyLogic` and its own assignment counter `countLogic`; generated bank `src/data/d-puz-logic.js` (108 puzzles) that builds into the data file. No UI: nothing in the app reads `PUZ.logic` yet (P3-07 does).
- Vocabulary: 34 short first names (different first letters in a puzzle, band B at most 4 letters, none ending in s, none a common word); unordered categories pet, snack, sport, hobby, music, ride, colour with per-item verb phrases ("has the cat", "swims", "does puzzles"); ordered categories age 6-9, house 1-4, race first-fourth. Every picture is an emoji already in `tools/emoji-allow.txt` (no new emoji) and every item word is a `picFor` name (some, such as cat, dog, apple, drum and bike, already have art). Band B uses only the six picture categories; band C never pairs sport with music (both "plays"). Planets from the spec's theme list were left out (no distinct pictures).
- Generator (5.3 steps 1-4): random solution; pool of every true clue the band allows whose sentence fits the word limit (B 6, C 11, D 14 words): B is / is not; C plus links between the two categories (is / is not); D plus or, comparison (`gt` / `lt`) and one apart (`nx`) between two different children named by name or by their unordered item. Clues are drawn by tier weights (the easy, medium and hard thirds of the slots lean more on negatives, links and comparisons) until one assignment of all 6 / 36 / 576 is left, then minimized (each clue is tried out; it stays out while the puzzle stays unique).
- Grader (5.3 step 5, `grade(p)`): the child's grid rules with auto-cross on a tick. Techniques, easiest first, grade = hardest used: 1 clue mark, 2 only place left, 3 link (copy marks through a tick to the third block), 4 two-way clue (or / comparison / one-apart clue crosses what it rules out with the current marks, and its two children are different), 5 chain (tick an open cell, apply 1-4 for at most 2 steps, contradiction, so cross it). Accept: B grade <= 2, 2-3 clues; C grade <= 3 (no chain), 4-5 clues, at least one link clue; D grade <= 5 with at most one chain, 5-8 clues, at least one comparison or one-apart clue, and the hard third must need its chain. Score = grade x 100 + effort (sum of the technique numbers of all steps); each band sorted by score, level L = entries 3(L-1) to 3L-1.
- Bank format: `PUZ.logic = {cats, B, C, D}`, 36 puzzles per band, each `[head, clue, ...]`; head `'Ella,Ben,Tom|hobby=paint,draw,puzzle|201|2|204'` = names | category=items (several joined by `;`) | solution (each child's item index per category) | grade | score; clue `[kind, ...indexes, sentence]` with entities as (category, item), category 0 = the children: `['is'|'not', c1, i1, c2, i2, text]`, `['or', c, i, d, j1, j2, text]`, `['gt'|'lt'|'nx', c1, i1, c2, i2, text]`. `cats[id] = {label, ordered, pics}`. Structured clues plus the finished sentence (the spec's step 6 stored indexes only and left words to runtime theme packs; the sentence is stored so the UI needs no grammar, and band B can draw child + picture + tick or cross from the structure).
- Counts and grades: 36 per band (spec), 108 in all, 478 clues, none redundant. B: 22 with 2 clues and 14 with 3, all grade 2, scores 204-209, only 3 distinct shapes (see Ideas and issues). C: 21 with 4 clues and 15 with 5, all grade 3, scores 316-326, 32 shapes. D: 5-8 clues (4/16/11/5), 24 grade 4 and 12 grade 5 (one chain each), scores 432-575; clue kinds 71 or, 69 comparison, 32 one-apart, 29 is, 32 not. Generation took 48 / 56 / 496 attempts, about 1 s.
- Determinism: each slot (band, index) has its own rng `seed|band|index` and avoids the shapes before it; then each band is sorted. `node tools/puzzles/build-banks.js logic` writes the file in about 1 s; `--check` reports `same` for all three banks.
- Tests (test.js, named `logic grid banks P3-06`, before the report): the bank loads alone and with all of src/js and src/data (same object; sudoku and mathdoku still there) and is in the built data file; every puzzle through `verifyLogic`: head format, children count and names (known, different initials), categories allowed for the band, items known and in order, solution a permutation, clue kinds and indexes for the band, each clue true of the stored solution, sentence = `render()` and within the word limit, clue count, link / comparison clue present, exactly one solution by `countLogic` and it is the stored one, no clue redundant, grader finishes on the stored solution with the stored grade and score, band grade and chain limits, sorted, no duplicates (`bankKey`). Then the test's own brute force over every assignment (no code shared with logic.js or verify-banks.js; checked: 6 / 36 / 576 assignments, contradictory clues give none): one solution, the stored one, every clue true, every clue needed (0 redundant); sentences: plain sentence, band word limit, every word a template word, a name or a word of the categories' phrases, nothing in `BAD`, each entity of the structure named in the sentence, "not" exactly in the `not` clues; names short and clean with different initials, item words not names, band B items all have pictures and picture names; its own duplicate key (clues with unordered items replaced by their child, over every renaming); logic.js agrees on the first puzzle per band; the whole bank regenerated from the seed equals the file byte for byte. Shown to fail on a wrong grade, a dropped clue (two solutions), an added redundant clue, a false clue, a changed sentence, a duplicate, an unsorted band and an unknown name.
- Size: data file 228,577 -> 271,203 bytes (+42,626: the bank file is 42,622 bytes); index.html unchanged apart from the version.
- check.sh: build, contrast, hex guard, test.js 83 of 83, smoke OK (no console errors, no network); check OK on the first run.
- Docs: BRAIN_TEASERS 5.3 generator steps (vocabulary, pool, grader techniques and band limits, score, format, runtime note) and the section 6 file table (`logic.js`, `verify-banks.js`); CONTRIBUTING section 4 (`build-banks.js [sudoku|mathdoku|logic]`).
- Files: new `tools/puzzles/logic.js`, `src/data/d-puz-logic.js`; edited `tools/puzzles/build-banks.js`, `tools/puzzles/verify-banks.js`, `tools/test.js`, `tools/build.py` (VER v103, provisional), `docs/BRAIN_TEASERS.md`, `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`; built `index.html`, `sw.js`, `data.v103.js` (replaces `data.v99.js`).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files [x] VER v103 (provisional) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js (nothing user-visible changed, no smoke block) [x] no screenshots (no UI) [x] fixtures unchanged [x] size [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-06-logic from afb8325 (v99); VER v103 is provisional. Conflicts to expect: `tools/build.py` (VER), `tools/test.js` (the new check before the report), `build-banks.js` / `verify-banks.js` if another bank type lands at the same time, `Chunk status` and this log, built files (rebuild, `git add -A`).
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v102 -> v103 at merge.

### P3-03 Sudoku (v98, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1, 7, 8): new puzzle type `sudoku` ("Sudoku", bands ABCD, 12 levels then the framework's Endless, `allow` 2) in a new `src/js/78-bt-sudoku.js` (object `SD`: the pure rules, the screen, one `BT.reg`), its look in a new `src/css/49-bt-sudoku.css`, and a drawn `sudoku` icon (grid on a soft square, three filled squares on the diagonal) in `15-icons.js`. Nothing in the framework changed. The bank `PUZ.sudoku` (P3-02) is read only inside functions (A11).
- What each band gets:
  | Band | Grid | Symbols | Givens, levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12 | Entry |
  |------|------|---------|----------------------------------|-------|
  | A (PK-K) | 4x4, 2x2 boxes | 4 drawn animals (duck, frog, pig, fish: shapes differ, not only colours) | 10 / 9 / 8 (bank easy / medium / hard; every hint is "the one gap") | a wrong animal wiggles and hops out (a mistake); the line says where it already is and that one glows amber; the intro, wrong lines, hints and the win are spoken |
  | B (1-2) | 4x4 | 1-4 | 8 / 6 / 5 | as A, with numbers |
  | C (3-4) | 6x6, boxes 3 across x 2 down | 1-6 | 22 / 18 / 14 | never judged right or wrong; a clash is tinted (one mistake); Erase, Notes, Undo |
  | D (5-6) | 9x9, 3x3 | 1-9 | 42 / 36 / 31 (hard needs locked candidates or a naked pair) | as C; the zoom box on small screens |
- Levels and variety: levels 1-4 use the easy list, 5-8 medium, 9-12 hard (section 7, most givens to fewest). Each level owns a quarter of its tier's 20 puzzles, so the 12 levels of a day are 12 different bank puzzles; the seeded rnd (type|band|level|day) picks one of that level's five and a transform (5.1: symbols relabelled, rows swapped inside a band, bands swapped, the same for columns and stacks, a transpose for 4x4 and 9x9). The puzzle stores the result (`g`, `s`), the tier, the bank index `k`, the grade and the transform `x`. Over 15 days no level gave the same grid two days running (672 pairs). `make()` well under 1 ms.
- Play: tap a square, then a symbol on the pad. The pad is the question screen's `padHTML` (0 and the check key taken out; Erase kept in C and D, Notes and Undo added as keys); under each symbol a row of dots shows how many are left (dots, not a small number, which would read as a power: "2" with a "4" beside it), and a green tick when none are left (in A and B that key greys out). The selected square is purple-ringed, its row, column and box are shaded, the same number elsewhere is tinted. Clash (C, D): both squares amber (`--try-tint`), a dashed `--try-ink` ring, a warning mark on the new entry, the line "Two 5s in this row. Change one of them." (amber text); never red. Notes: a toggle (aria-pressed) that turns the pad's digits small and purple; marks show in a small 3-across grid; a number placed clears that number from the notes of its row, column and box (undoable). Undo goes back 60 steps; squares filled by a hint lock (star-yellow) and no undo or erase takes them. Hardware digits, Backspace, arrows, N for notes. Win: the board pops box by box, then the frame's win card.
- Hints (5.1; tier 1, 2, 3, then 3 again fills the next one): the next step is found in order: a number that is not right (C, D: one that clashes first; "One number in this box is not right yet." / "This 4 does not belong here." / fill), a house with one gap ("This row has just one empty square." / "Only 4 is missing from this row."), a naked single ("Look at this box. One square can be filled now." / "Only 5 fits here: 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 9 are already in its row, column or box."), a hidden single, boxes first ("Look at this box. Where can 3 go?" / "3 can only go here: the other empty squares of this box already have a 3 in their row or column."), then for D hard locked candidates and naked pairs until a single shows ("In this row, a 4 can only go in the marked squares, so no other square of that box can be a 4. Now only 1 fits here.", the squares marked). Tier 1 shades the house yellow, tier 2 rings the square in star yellow and selects it, tier 3 fills it. A last-resort "Try this square" exists but never happens on the bank (tested).
- 9x9 on a phone: the board is a square sized to the space left (section 8). At 380x780 a 9x9 square can be 36 px at most (348 px of width; BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1 and 8 allow 36 px on a phone), so when a 9x9 square would be under 46 px the zoom box comes on: the selected square's 3x3 box drawn again at 48 px squares. In portrait it shares a row with the talk line, under the board, and the pad goes to two rows of six (1-5 Erase, 6-9 Notes Undo): board squares 36 px, zoom 48, keys 48 tall, nothing scrolls at 380x780. A tap on the board picks a square and brings its box into the zoom box, where a near miss is put right with one big tap (the smoke bot does exactly that); notes are easy to read there. At 1024x600 the bars leave 428 px of height, so squares are 45 px (section 8's 44 px for dense grids) and the zoom box sits beside the pad in the side column. At 600x960 squares are 60 px (as 5.1 says) and the pad is one row of nine.
- State (`api.saveState` after every move; `SD.fit` rejects a state that does not belong to the puzzle, which then starts fresh): `{v: entries as a digit string ('0' empty or given), m: note bit masks per square ([] when none), sel, miss, hn: hint-filled squares, h: undo steps [[square, old value, old notes], ...], nt: notes on}`. Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- Tests: named check `sudoku UI P3-03` (720 puzzles: 4 bands x 12 levels x 15 days): registered with bands ABCD and 12 levels; `make()` deterministic; tier by level, the bank index in the level's quarter, 12 different bank puzzles a day; the transform is one of the allowed ones (permutations that keep bands and stacks, transpose only with square boxes) and the stored puzzle is the bank puzzle under it (the test's own reading); independently of the app: the solution is a valid grid, the givens agree with it and have the tier's count, exactly one solution by `countSimple` (verify-banks.js), and the dev-time human grader solves it to that grid with the bank's grade; next-day repeats under 2% (0 seen); `solve()` fills every empty square once with the solution; hints from the start to the end: tiers 1 and 2 leave the state byte for byte, point at the same square, tier 3 fills exactly that square with its number and changes exactly one square, no "guess" step, band A only "one gap" steps, locked-candidate steps seen in D; moves: a wrong symbol bounces in A and B (one mistake, board unchanged), a clash in C and D shows in `conflicts` and counts, the hint finds the wrong number first, undo restores the state byte for byte, givens take nothing, notes go in and are cleared by a number in their house and come back with undo, hinted squares cannot be erased; saved state: JSON round trip fits and gives the same next hint, eight damaged states do not fit; `make()` under 5 ms. Mutation-checked: column swaps across stacks, and a tier-2 hint that fills, both fail it.
- Smoke block `// P3-03` (end of the main sequence, midway save): 380x780 grade 1 (4x4, band B) and 1024x600 grade 5 (9x9, band D): Home -> Brain Teasers -> Sudoku -> level 1 by real taps, a wrong entry (B: bounces, the board unchanged, amber line saying where it already is; D: two or more amber squares, dashed ring, warning mark, background exactly `--try-tint` and not `--danger`, the line in `--try-ink`; one mistake each), D: Undo takes the clash away and keeps the mistake, Notes put a 3 and a 7 in an empty square; hint tiers 1 and 2 (a house shaded, then a ringed square and a different line, board unchanged), a right move, back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes the same puzzle and state, the rest tapped in, win card, 1 star (two hints) and level 2 saved. 380x780 grade 5: squares 36 px, zoom squares 48 px, a board tap brings the box into the zoom box, a zoom tap picks the square, the number goes in. 380x780 grade 0: four animal drawings on the pad, a wrong animal bounces, the rest tapped in, 3 stars. Every screen: 48 px targets (board squares of a zoomed 9x9: 36 px on a phone, 44 on a tablet), nothing outside the screen, no sideways scroll, the puzzle fits without scrolling, no emoji chrome.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-sud-hub bt-sud-a bt-sud-b bt-sud-c bt-sud-d bt-sud-d-hint bt-sud-win`, three sizes each, played by real taps through `tools/lib/bt-sud.js`): looked at bt-sud-d at 380x780 and 1024x600, bt-sud-a and bt-sud-c at 380x780, b, c, d-hint at 600x960, hub, win, b and d-hint at 380x780, c and a at 1024x600. Fixed from looking: the first phone layout had a 4-row pad that squeezed 9x9 squares to 30-33 px (now the zoom row and a 2x6 pad: 36 px); the "left" count looked like a power ("2" with a small "4"), now dots; bigger digits on the 4x4 pad.
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 80 of 80, smoke OK). The first run failed only the existing `Sentences: never saw sent5choicewide` smoke step (P2-24: level 5 is played until both question types have shown, at most 9 questions, and a random run can miss one); the second run passed. index.html 1,300,262 -> 1,345,141 bytes (+44.9 KB: 78-bt-sudoku.js 35.6 KB, a third of it comments and hint words; 49-bt-sudoku.css 8.8 KB), data.v98.js 218,632 (unchanged), total 1,563,773.
- Test expectations changed: none. (The P3-01 hub count follows `BT.offered()`, which now includes Sudoku; the Puzzle of the Day pick can now land on Sudoku.)
- Not done: the "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule is still the framework's open item (P3-01). The zoom box shows the selected box only; a row or column focus was not needed.
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files (new 78-bt-sudoku.js, 49-bt-sudoku.css, tools/lib/bt-sud.js; the icon in 15-icons.js; tests, smoke, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v98 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-03-sudoku from 285b8f7 (v97). Overlaps are appends: tools/test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), tools/smoke.js (one block after the P3-08 block), tools/shots.js (one `Object.assign(SCREENS, ...)` block before `main`), 15-icons.js (one icon before `path-math`), built files (rebuild, `git add -A`). Renumber VER at merge.

### P4-06 Dino, bird and sea pal drawn on the rig (v101, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: the three pets that were still emoji (dino, bird, sea pal) are drawn on the P4-05 rig in `src/js/17-pets-svg.js` (`PET_RIG.dino`, `.bird`, `.ocean`) with palettes in `src/css/41-pet.css` (`.pet-k-dino`, `.pet-k-bird`, `.pet-k-ocean`, `--ill-*` tokens only). Same 5 stages (egg, baby, kid, grown, super = grown + glow ring), same 6 faces (idle, happy, oops, cheer, sleepy, hungry) plus the cheer / oops reaction faces, same named groups, hat / toy anchors per stage, same CSS motion (bob, blink, egg rock, tail wag, cheer hop with arms up, oops wobble, sleepy breathing), same shading recipe as the dragon (shade rim, base moved up-left, translucent white highlight, dark contact accent, light from the top left, no outlines, no gradients, ids or filters).
- The pets: **Dino** is blue with a cream belly and snout, rounded orange plates on the head, along the back (the `extra` group, so they flap on cheer like the dragon's wings) and on its thick curled tail, darker blue spots, stubby arms with cream claws and chunky feet; its egg is pale blue with blue spots and a plate poking out. **Bird** is a round yellow chick-bird with a three-feather tuft, wings for arms (they lift on cheer), a fan tail, orange toes and a light scalloped belly; it has its own faces (`face: birdFace`: the shared eyes, the orange beak is the mouth and opens for happy, cheer and hungry with the tongue showing); its speckled egg sits in a straw nest. **Sea pal** is a little aqua whale with a white chin and belly (throat grooves), a blue fin on its head and a fin on each side, blue flippers and flipper feet, light whale-shark spots and a two-lobed tail fluke; its egg is aqua with white waves and two bubbles. At 28-48 px the three read apart by colour and silhouette (plates and tail / tuft and round body / fins and fluke).
- Shared helpers added to 17-pets-svg.js: `petVol` (shade + base path), `petGl` (gloss stroke), `petAt` (placed group), `petShell` / `petShellHi` (egg shell). The dragon's code and output are unchanged.
- Hooks: every pet is on the rig now, so the emoji fallbacks are gone from the drawing paths: `petHTML` (42-paths.js) always returns `petRigHTML`, `petArt` / `petPick` always draw, `petAura` is always `'rig'` (05-pets.js), `RW.pet()` (18-reward-art.js) always draws. Setup, Home, Pet Care, Pet Shop, the map, the round runner, the growth overlay and the reward screens therefore show the drawn pet for every choice. `petE()` stays for the games' canvases (60-blocks, 62-hill; see Ideas and issues).
