### P2-16 Tracing engine and digits 0-9 (v101, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: a tracing engine (new question kind `trace`, `src/js/47-trace.js`, CSS `src/css/47-trace.css`) and the stroke data for digits 0-9 (`src/data/d-trace.js`, global `TRACE`). New skill `trace` (level 1, `area: 'math'`, own round of 5 glyphs, `noPlace`) and the stop `trace 1` "Trace Numbers" at the end of Tiny Town (`m_tiny`), as CURRICULUM 2.1 and E-trace say. The letter stops (ABC Beach `trace 2`, `trace 3`) are left to P2-17.
- Stroke data format (documented in the header of d-trace.js, for P2-17): `TRACE.sets[set] = {n, lines: {top, mid, base[, desc]}, order: [keys], glyphs: {key: {say, s: [stroke, ...]}}}`. A stroke is a list of centre-line points `[x, y]` in a fixed 100 x 100 box (y down) in pen order; the pen runs a Catmull-Rom curve through them (drawn and checked as the same curve); `[x, y, 1]` is a sharp corner; a stroke whose last point equals its first is a smooth closed loop; a one-point stroke is a dot (tap). 1 to 4 strokes per glyph; points 8 to 14 units apart on curves. Digits sit between `top` 10 and `base` 90, middle line 50. P2-17 adds sets `upper` and `lower` (with a `desc` line for tails) and `TR.LV[2]`, `TR.LV[3]`; nothing in the engine is digit-specific except the prompt's dice dots.
- Handwriting model (school manuscript, top to bottom, left to right): 0 from the top going left round to the start; 1 down; 2 from below the top line up and round, slant to bottom left, slide right; 3 round to a middle point, round again; 4 two strokes (down and slide right; then the long line down); 5 two strokes (down to the middle and round the belly; then the top flag); 6 from top right down round and close the loop; 7 slide right, slant down; 8 from the top going left into an S and back up; 9 from the right of the ball up and round, then straight down. Open 4, 1 without a flag and a straight-stemmed 9 (Andika draws a closed 4, a flagged 1 and a curved 9): the prompt card draws the glyph from the strokes (not the font), so the child copies the same model they trace.
- A question is one glyph traced three times (CURRICULUM's guide levels): level 1 the pen demonstrates every stroke, then the child traces over hollow outlined strokes with numbered green start dots and arrows; level 2 a dotted centre line, the start dot and one arrow; level 3 the start dot only. Pass pips under the board. Done strokes snap on in the subject colour (fade in, transform/opacity only); lifting part way keeps the part and the next touch carries on from its end. A miss is never red: the ink fades, the start dot pulses, the arrows run along the stroke, a soft sound, "Start at the green dot." / "Follow the arrow." Hints: Help (or 2 misses on a stroke) = tier 1, the pen draws the stroke; Help again (or 3 misses) = tier 2, a yellow ball runs along the stroke and stays just ahead of the finger, over a dotted guide. The hint panel stays empty (the hint is on the board). Voice: the prompt is spoken on its own (`pk`), "Your turn!", one line per pass.
- Checking (`TR.track` / `TR.check`, box units, tolerance `tol` = 14 for Pre-K, 10 for K and up, or `q.tol`): the stroke is a dense centre line (1 unit apart); finger points are joined and walked in steps of 2 units; the first touch must be within 1.5 tol of the start (or of where a lifted trace stopped); each step finds the nearest stroke point in a window from 1 tol behind to 2.5 tol ahead of the progress so far, within tol it is on and moves progress forward (never back), else off. OK when progress reaches within 0.8 tol of the end, at most 25% of the trace length is off, and the trace is no longer than 2.5 x the stroke + 3 tol (scribbles fail). So coverage and direction count, not pixels: reversed, out-of-order and off-path traces make no progress. Verdicts: ok, part (kept), tap (ignored, no miss), start / off / scribble (a miss).
- Scoring: glyph stars 3 = no misses, 2 = one or two, 1 = more (shown on the board when the glyph ends). The answer `done` is submitted; a glyph under 2 stars sets the round's `tries` so it counts as needing another try (no star point, amber dot). Round of 5, so the stop's usual 3/2/1 stars follow.
- Pointer input: pointer events on the svg (touch and mouse), `touch-action: none`, pointer capture, coalesced events; palms: a touch wider than 60 px is ignored, one finger at a time, and a second touch replaces the first only if the first has not moved and the second is nearer the dot. Board fits 380x780 (348 px square), 600x960, 1024x600 (board on the right, 54%). Animations are rAF (demo, ball) with `TR.stop()` called from `view()`; CSS animates transform and opacity only; calm mode and reduced motion stop the pulses.
- Edits to existing files: `49-kinds.js` (KIND.trace = TRK, header), `39-hints.js` (hint kind `trace`), `40-view.js` (`TR.stop()`), `38-skills.js` (skill), `42-paths.js` (stop), `15-icons.js` (`ICO_SK.trace = 'edit'`), `45-placement.js` (the quiz skips skills marked `noPlace`: a trace question always ends right, so it says nothing about level; Tiny Town's placement items are unchanged), `tools/build.py` VER.
- Tests: new named check "tracing P2-16": every digit's strokes valid (format, inside the box and the lines, length, 1-unit dense points, stroke count, first direction and closed loops per the model, top-to-bottom/left-to-right order, loops smooth at the join); 1,684 synthetic traces: ideal, 40 noisy per stroke and tolerance (jitter 0.3 tol, fast and slow parts, start up to 0.5 tol off, stop up to 0.45 tol short) and 20 with 8 px jitter pass; reversed, noisy reversed, leaving the path, zigzag (scribble), random scribble, far start and wrong stroke order fail; a tap is ignored; half a stroke is kept and finishing from there passes; dot strokes; 3,000 generated questions pass kindProblems, render, labels one per digit, no letters outside `.pktxt`; rounds of 5 distinct digits in order, reproducible by seed; validators catch broken questions; Tiny Town ends with trace 1; placement never asks it; 21 saves (fixtures and Tiny Town states x grades -1, 0, 1) with and without the stop: stars unchanged, no stars on trace1, nothing re-locks, the map opens where it did (a child with all 9 old stops passed and the boss next now opens on trace1, which the boss waits for), a finished Tiny Town shows it NEW; CSS tokens and motion; no Math.random. Shown to fail when the progress window is removed (reversed loops pass). Generator fuzz handles kind `trace`.
- Test expectations changed: "Tiny Town (P2-01)" stop list `... pk_num2 sort2` -> `... pk_num2 sort2 trace1` (the stop this chunk adds). smoke.js P2-01 block: "every Tiny Town stop answered by taps" skips `trace1` (it has no taps; the P2-16 block plays it). smoke `answer()` traces `trace` questions (they can come up in Tricky Mix, boss and test-out pools).
- Smoke (`// P2-16` block): touch at 380x780 (Pre-K, CDP touch emulation) and mouse at 1024x600 (K): the stop opened from the map, prompt spoken on its own, tolerance by grade, a backwards stroke is a gentle miss (pulse, arrows run, no red colour anywhere on the board, nothing filled, a spoken tip), Help twice (pen demo, then ball and guide; empty hint panel), a palm (110 px touch) resting on the board while a stroke is traced is ignored, 5 glyphs x 3 passes traced with pointer moves made from the stroke data, misses per glyph 1,0,0,0,0, 5/5, stop saved with 3 stars; 48 px buttons, board at least 240 px, no sideways scroll.
- Shots: `trace-1` (level 1, 5 with stroke 1 done), `trace-2` (level 2, 8 part traced), `trace-3` (level 3, 4 after a miss), `trace-hint` (tier 1 pen), `trace-lead` (tier 2 ball), `trace-done` (glyph stars), `trace-sheet` (every glyph's strokes, dots and arrows over the Andika glyph at 30% opacity; P2-17's sheet). Looked at: trace-sheet (600x960, 1024x600), trace-1, trace-2, trace-lead (600x960), trace-3 (600x960, 380x780), trace-done (600x960, 1024x600), trace-hint (380x780).
- Sizes: index.html 1,334,666 (warning over 1.2 MB was already on at 1,300,262), data.v101.js 223,814, total 1,558,480.

### P3-04 MathDoku generator and banks (v99, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.2 and 6): new dev-time generator `tools/puzzles/mathdoku.js`, registered as `mathdoku` in the `build-banks.js` TYPES registry (seed `star-school-mathdoku-1`); `verify-banks.js` gains `verifyMathdoku` and its own cage counter `countCages`; generated bank `src/data/d-puz-mathdoku.js` (108 puzzles) that builds into the data file. No UI: nothing in the app reads `PUZ.mathdoku` yet (P3-05 does).
- Generator: a random Latin square (csp.solve with a seeded rng); cells visited in random order each start a cage of a random size (band weights) that grows into random free orthogonal neighbours; extra single cells are merged into a neighbouring cage with room, too few (band B needs 2) means a new partition. Operations per band (B `+`; C `+ - *`; D `+ - * /`), `-` and `/` only on 2 cells, `/` only when one value divides the other, `*` only up to 144 (the times tables). Uniqueness by csp.js (`solutions(p, 2)`, cages as `cons`, single cells as givens); when there are two solutions the largest cage they differ on is repaired: a 2-cell cage first gets another operation, a bigger cage is split into two connected parts (or a cell made a single) while the band's single-cell limit allows, else another operation; up to 30 repairs, then the puzzle is discarded. Repairs keep the band rules (cage size, singles B 2-3, C 1-2, D 0-1), and the finished puzzle is checked against them again.
- Grader (cage-aware, human-style, `grade(N, cages)` in mathdoku.js): candidates are the values not in the row or column, kept only if some way of filling the cage (its target, the other cells' candidates, no repeat in a line) uses them. Techniques, easiest first: 1 last cell (a line with one gap, or a cage with one gap the arithmetic forces), 2 cage single, 3 hidden single, 4 cage in a line (a value every fill of a cage puts in its cells of one row or column leaves the rest of that line), 5 naked pair. Grade = hardest technique used; a puzzle it cannot finish is discarded (no guessing), so every bank puzzle is solvable by these steps.
- Difficulty score (5.2 step 6): csp.js search nodes with propagation only x 1000 + the grader's effort (sum of the technique numbers of all steps), so the many puzzles that propagation alone solves (1 node) still sort by how much work they take. Each band is sorted by score (band D: 5x5 first, then 6x6), so level L is entries 3(L-1) to 3L-1.
- Bank format: `PUZ.mathdoku[band] = [36 strings]`, each `'N|cages|solution|grade|score'`; cages comma-separated, each = cells as one base-36 character per index (row * N + col, ascending; the first is the top-left cell where the label goes) + op (`+ - * /`, `=` for a single given cell) + target, e.g. `'4|01+3,23*8,...|1234...|2|1018'`. The spec's `N;cells-op-target` with one character per cell (36 cells fit 0-9a-z), plus the solution, grade and score like the sudoku bank (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.2 step 7 and the section 6 file table updated).
- Counts and grades: 36 per band (spec), 108 in all. B 3x3: grades 6 x 1 and 30 x 2, scores 1006-2008. C 4x4: 27 x 2, 8 x 3, 1 x 4, scores 1016-4024. D 5x5 (18): 1 x 2, 13 x 3, 4 x 4, scores 1034-6043. D 6x6 (18): 9 x 3, 8 x 4, 1 x 5, scores 1051-19069. Generation took 67 / 59 / 49 / 70 attempts.
- Determinism: each slot (band, grid size, index) has its own rng `seed|band|N|index` and avoids the puzzles before it; then each band is sorted. `node tools/puzzles/build-banks.js mathdoku` writes the file in about 0.3 s; `--check` reports `same` for both banks.
- Tests (test.js, named `mathdoku banks P3-04`, before the report): the bank loads alone and with all of src/js and src/data (same object; PUZ.sudoku still there) and is in the built data file; every puzzle through `verifyMathdoku`: format, size per place (D 5x5 then 6x6), solution is a Latin square, cages cover every cell once with ascending cells in first-cell order, connected, cage size, op allowed for the band, `-`/`/` on 2 cells, `=` exactly on single cells, target = op on the solution, product limit, single-cell count, exactly one solution by `countCages`, grader finishes on the stored solution with the stored grade, score recomputes, sorted, no duplicates under the 8 rotations and mirrors. Then the test's own counter (cage-by-cage over each cage's arithmetic tuples; no code shared with csp.js, mathdoku.js or verify-banks.js) on every puzzle (one solution, the stored one) and on one rotation or mirror of each (still unique); its own transform code checks no puzzle turned or mirrored equals another; the counter is checked (12 3x3 and 576 4x4 Latin squares, a changed target gives 0); csp.js agrees on the first puzzle per band; the whole bank regenerated from the seed equals the file byte for byte. Shown to fail on a wrong grade, a mirrored duplicate, a puzzle with two solutions (an operation changed) and a wrong target.
- Relabelling: not a runtime transform for MathDoku (the arithmetic changes), so duplicates are checked under the 8 rotations and mirrors only (transposition is one of them).
- Size: data file 218,632 -> 228,577 bytes (+9,945, the bank file); index.html unchanged apart from the version.
- check.sh: build, contrast, hex guard, test.js 80 of 80, smoke OK; check OK on the first run.
- Docs: BRAIN_TEASERS 5.2 generator steps (repairs, grader, score, format, file names) and the section 6 file table; CONTRIBUTING section 4 (`build-banks.js [sudoku|mathdoku]`).
- Files: new `tools/puzzles/mathdoku.js`, `src/data/d-puz-mathdoku.js`; edited `tools/puzzles/build-banks.js`, `tools/puzzles/verify-banks.js`, `tools/test.js`, `tools/build.py` (VER v99, provisional), `docs/BRAIN_TEASERS.md`, `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`; built `index.html`, `sw.js`, `data.v99.js` (replaces `data.v97.js`).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files [x] VER v99 (provisional) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js (nothing user-visible changed, no smoke block) [x] no screenshots (no UI) [x] fixtures unchanged [x] size [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).

### A11 Data banks in a second precached file (v97, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- Why now (ROADMAP A11, risk R1): the built index.html was 1,376,935 bytes, and the chunks in flight (5th/6th grade math worlds, three Brain Teaser puzzle types, more banks) would push it past the 1.5 MB limit.
- Build (`tools/build.py`): two files. `index.html` = shell + CSS + fonts + `<script>` with `src/js` + `<script src="data.v97.js"></script>` + `<script>` with `src/boot.js`. `data.v97.js` = every `src/data/*.js` in filename order (d-comp, d-demo, d-gram, d-puz-sudoku, d-spell, d-vocab), byte for byte, between a header comment and the last line `var DATA_VER='v97';`. The build deletes every other `data.v*.js` at the repo root. `src/shell.html` got the external script tag and a `/*@@BOOT@@*/` marker.
- Load order: the three classic scripts run in document order, synchronously, and share one global scope, so it is the same order as the old single script (code, data, boot); data files keep calling `compAdd`, `GR`, `BANK.x = ...` at load time. A data script before the code would not work (the data files call code helpers). Plain `<script src>` works over `file://`, so smoke, shots and a parent opening the file directly are unchanged.
- Version pairing: both. The file name carries VER, so an index.html can only ask for its own release's data file, and no HTTP cache (GitHub Pages sends max-age=600; the service worker's fetch uses the HTTP cache) can hand it an older file under the same name. The stamp catches the rest: `src/boot.js` starts the app only if `dataReady()` (`src/js/90-app.js`: `DATA_VER` exists, so the file ran to its last line, and equals the page's VER). Otherwise `dataMissing()` shows "Star School needs to finish updating. Connect to the internet, then tap Try again." (Try again reloads) and logs one line in S.err (App log). No other save change; no new save fields.
- Service worker (`sw.js`, from build.py): cache `starschool-v97` precaches `./`, `index.html`, `data.v97.js`, manifest, icons; the activate step deletes other caches as before, so an update replaces both files together. Two small hardening changes: only `res.ok` responses are cached (a 404 or 500 page can no longer become the cached index.html or data file), and a script request that fails offline gets a network error instead of index.html as its body (which would have been a parse error).
- Sizes: index.html 1,376,935 -> 1,159,988 bytes (under the 1.2 MB warning again); data.v97.js 218,632; total 1,378,620. Of index.html, about 929 KB is code (src/js), 179 KB CSS, 52 KB fonts.
- Budgets (`tools/test.js`): index.html unchanged (warn 1.2 MB, fail 1.5 MB); data file warn 1 MB, fail 2 MB (mostly strings, which parse faster than code, and banks are where content grows); total warn 2 MB, fail 3 MB (what a tablet downloads once and parses at every start; P6-02's throttled start-up budget is the real gate). `tools/check.sh` prints all three.
- Tests: new check `data file split A11`: index.html has exactly three scripts (code, `data.v97.js`, boot) and no async/defer, boot checks `dataReady()`, no bank left in index.html, no `__VER__` left; the data file is all of src/data and ends with the stamp; the built code loads in Node without the data file (proves no code reads data while loading) and `dataReady()` is false; with the data file every expected global is there (BANK.gram/vocab/demo, PASS.comp/info, COMP, SPELL_WORDS, genSpell, PUZ.sudoku A-D) and `dataReady()` is true; with another release's stamp it is false; sw.js cache name and precache list; no stale data file at the root; the budgets. Shown to fail on a stale `data.v1.js` and on a precache list without the data file. The P3-02 sudoku check now looks for the bank in the data file.

### P3-08 Mazes (v94, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: new puzzle type `maze` in `src/js/75-bt-maze.js` (the `MZ` helpers and one `BT.reg`), CSS in `src/css/49-bt-maze.css`, a drawn `maze` icon in `15-icons.js`. Nothing in the framework changed. With a real type registered, the Brain Teasers card now shows on Home for every child (bands A-D all have mazes).
- Generation (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.4, runtime, seeded): recursive backtracker (iterative, with a chance to keep carving straight: A .7, B .35, C .2, D .12, a little less each level), so every maze is perfect; start and goal are the two farthest cells (two BFS passes), the start being the end nearer the top left. Sizes by level, four levels per step: A 5x5 (levels 1-6) then 6x6; B 8, 9, 10; C 12, 13, 14; D 16, 17, 18. Band C adds 3 stars in dead ends off the way (separate branches, deeper as the level rises); the flag's gate opens when all 3 are held. Band D adds 1 key and door (levels 1-6) or 2 (7-12): each door sits on a plain corridor cell of the way, its key at the end of a side branch that leaves the way before that door (key 2's branch between the doors). The par (shortest winning walk) comes from a BFS over (cell, items held); `solve()` returns that walk as cells. Puzzle JSON: `{b, w, h, o (hex digit of open sides per cell), s, g, stars | keys + doors, par}`, about 400 bytes for 18x18. `make()` takes about 0.3 ms.
- Play: drag from the bear; the trail follows cell to cell toward the finger and never through a wall, dragging back over the trail erases it, lifting the finger keeps the place. A tap on the trail goes back to that spot, a tap in a straight open line walks there. "Arrows" shows an up / left-down-right pad (remembered, see Ideas); keyboard arrows work. Picked-up stars and keys stay picked up when the trail is erased. A door without its key and the closed gate rattle and say what is missing. A mistake is reaching an empty dead end (marked amber, "Dead end. Go back!"); `allow` 2. Band D shows Steps and the Par. State saved on finger lift and after every tap or arrow (`{t trail, m items, dead, steps, mis}`); a saved state that does not fit the maze is ignored. Band A speaks the instruction on start.
- Hints (5.4): 1) a flashing arrow beside the bear toward the next thing to reach (the flag, or the next star or key on the route) and a spoken line ("The next star is up."). 2) dots on the next 5 cells of the route from where the bear is (going back first if it is in a wrong branch). 3) dots through the next junction on the route and one cell past it (at least 5). Hints never draw the route's last cell, so no tier shows the whole way.
- Look: one canvas. Static layer (floor, walls merged into long runs, drawn in three passes: shade lower right, base, highlight upper left) is painted once per size; a move redraws the dynamic layer only (start pad, amber dead ends, doors with a keyhole the shape of their key's bow, keys, stars, flag with a gate, an orange trail tube with a highlight, hint dots, the bear cub). Walls green in A and B, blue in C, purple in D; all colours are `--ill-*` and state tokens read at mount. Portrait: board on top, status pill, message, Arrows below; landscape: board left, panel right. 18x18 full redraw 3-14 ms at 6x CPU throttle (smoke, varies with machine load); the stage holds about 43 elements.
- Tests: named check `mazes P3-08` (4,032 mazes: 84 per level per band): deterministic, size per band and level, perfect maze (connected, N-1 openings, both sides agree), start/goal a farthest pair, band C stars in dead ends off the way, band D keys at dead ends off the way, reachable holding only earlier keys, doors on the way in order, goal unreachable without picking up keys; solvable by the test's own state BFS, `par` equals it, `solve()` is a legal shortest win (no wall, no door without its key, no flag before the stars); hints at the start and at three points along the walk follow the route, tier 2 at most 5 cells, tier 3 at least as many, neither reaches the last cell; tier 1 has a target; mean shortest win grows A 24, B 55, C 135, D 186 and dead ends 2/7/16/29; later levels of a band are longer than the first; saved-state fit; make speed. Smoke block `// P3-08` (380x780 by touch through CDP touch events, band B; 1024x600 by mouse, band C, then band D 18x18): Home card, hub, level strip, drag 6 cells, a wall stops the bear, drag back erases, hint tiers 1-3, back and Keep going resume, tap on the trail, arrow pad (and the choice saved), a dead end counts one mistake, the rest of the route wins (1 star with 3 hints, saved); band D: a door stops the bear without its key, the full 213-cell route wins 3 stars, board draw timed at 6x throttle; 48 px targets, no sideways scroll, no emoji chrome.
- Test expectation changed: the P3-01 smoke block expected exactly 1 type card on the hub (only the fake type existed). It now expects as many cards as `BT.offered()` (the fake type plus mazes), because a real type now ships. Two older smoke steps also failed on the base commit (669471c) before any maze code, because P2-09 and P2-10 were merged with each one's "my world is last" expectation: P2-09 expected Factor Factory to be the last math world (Decimal Desert now follows it), and P2-10 expected a new 4th grader's map to open on the desert (it opens on Factor Factory, the first grade 4 world). P2-09 now accepts last-or-just-before-the-desert; P2-10 accepts either world as the current one and taps the desert chip before checking it. Both blocks were run alone, failing on the base build and passing after.
- Shots `bt-maze-hub`, `bt-maze-levels`, `bt-maze-a`, `bt-maze-b` (with hint 2), `bt-maze-c`, `bt-maze-d`, `bt-maze-hint`, `bt-maze-pad`, `bt-maze-win`: looked at A, B, C, D, the pad and the win card at 380x780, C and D at 600x960 and 1024x600, the hub and level strip at 380x780. The tier-1 arrow first sat on top of the bear and stayed up under the tier-2 dots; it now sits beside the bear and hides on the next move or hint. The first pad (3x3) shrank the board on phones; it is now an inverted T beside the Arrows button.
- Not done: no zoomed board for small cells (see Ideas); no "steps over par" effect on the stars (par is shown only; stars use hints and dead ends like other types).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files [x] VER v94 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v95 -> v96 at merge.

### P3-10 Number puzzles: pyramids, balance, make the number (v96, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: three Brain Teaser types in a new `src/js/77-bt-number.js` (+ `src/css/49-bt-number.css`), registered separately as BRAIN_TEASERS section 4 lists them: `pyramid` (bands B C D, shown as "Pyramids"), `balance` (A B C D, "Balance"), `target` (B C D, "Target"; the catalog's "Make the Number"). The shorter names fit the play top bar at 380 px next to the timer chip (see Ideas). Icons: `blocks`, `scale`, `target` (no new icons). The pure rules (make, hint, solve, the target search, the balance tip) are in `BTN.PYR`, `BTN.BAL`, `BTN.TGT` so tools/test.js runs them without a page. Entry is the question screen's number pad (`padHTML` from 49-kinds.js) laid out in two rows of six (1-6, then 7 8 9 delete 0 check), plus hardware digits, Backspace and Enter.
- What each band gets (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.6 and 7; levels 1-12, then the framework's Endless):
  | Band | Pyramids | Balance | Target |
  |------|----------|---------|--------|
  | A (PK-K) | - | one picture, values 1-3 rising to 1-5, totals up to 10: apple + k (levels 1-3), apple + apple (4-8), three apples or two apples and a weight (9-12); from level 6 the picture may stand on the right tray | - |
  | B (1-2) | 3 rows, bottom 1-5 rising to 1-9 | two pictures, triangular (the second scale adds one new picture), values 1-5 rising to 1-10; aa, then a + k, then aaa / aa + k; b = a + k from level 9; scales may come in either order from level 9 | two of five tiles (1-10, from level 7 1-15) with + or −, target 5-20; addition-only targets on levels 1-4 |
  | C (3-4) | 4 rows, bottom 1-6 rising to 1-12 | three pictures, triangular, with repeated pictures (2 or 3 apples), and a last question that is an expression ("present + banana × 2 = ?"), values 1-6 rising to 1-12; scales shuffled from level 7 | all three tiles 1-10, + − × (+ − only for the target on levels 1-3), target 5-50 |
  | D (5-6) | 5 rows, bottom 1-10 rising to 1-20; levels 10-12 are "tricky" (no brick follows from two others; tagged) | not triangular: sum and difference (levels 1-4), 2a + b and a + b or a + 2b and 2a + b (5-8), three in three from level 9 (pair sums a + b, b + c, a + c; or a + b + c with two differences); values 1-8 rising to 1-20; determinant checked | all four tiles 1-13, + − × ÷, target 24 (levels 1-6) or 10-100 from a random expression (7-12) |
  - Pyramids: givens = bottom-row length + 2 (levels 1-3, the whole bottom row given: only adding), + 1 (4-7), + 0 (8-12), so givens go from most to fewest (section 7). Accepted only at full rank (Gaussian elimination on the bottom-row coefficients) and, in B and C and D levels 1-9, when "two of a triple known gives the third" fills it.
  - Target: six candidates per level, each counted by an exhaustive search (whole, non-negative steps; a + b and b + a, and the order of a chain of + or ×, count once); level 1 takes the one with the most solutions, level 12 the fewest. Any correct way wins. C and D must use every tile ("That is 24, but use every number!"). `make()` is at most about 5 ms unthrottled (target D), so under 50 ms at 6x.
- Play: Pyramids and Balance: tap a missing brick or a box, type, tick. A right number locks green (star-yellow when a hint filled it); a wrong one counts a mistake and shakes. On a balance the wrong number is tried on a scale where it is the only unknown: the arm turns 6° about the pin, the trays move with it, the picture wears the guess in an amber tag and the line says "too heavy" or "too light"; the next tap levels it. Solved pictures wear their weight on every scale. Target: tap a number, a sign, a number: the two merge into a result tile; a step that would go below zero or not share out evenly is refused kindly (no mistake); using up the tiles without the target counts a mistake and the tiles come back; Undo goes back one step. Band A speaks the question on mount; every screen has a speak button for the current line.
- Hints (3 tiers; a 4th press repeats tier 3, so it fills the next one): Pyramids: 1 glows a brick that can be found now, 2 says how ("Add the two bricks under it: 7 + 5." / "The brick above is 19. Take away the brick next to it, 9."), 3 fills it; tricky pyramids point at the brick that unlocks the most and explain guess-and-check. Balance: 1 glows the scale with one unknown left, 2 says the step (take k away from both sides / share W into n equal parts / add up the other side; band D: compare the scales, with the trick for its form), 3 fills that picture; C's last question: 1 points, 2 lists the weights to add, 3 fills. Target: 1 glows a tile to start with, 2 glows both tiles and names the sign only when it is not the last step ("Try 6 × 4 first." / "Use 9 and 8. Which sign makes 17?"), 3 does the step (from a dead end tiers 1-2 point at Undo and 3 starts over).
- State (`api.saveState` after every move, resumes exactly): pyramid `{done, hinted, sel, miss}`, balance `{done, hinted, sel, miss}` (slots are picture indexes and `'q'`), target `{tiles: [{id, v}], next, hist, miss}`. Puzzles: pyramid `{n, v (bricks bottom row first), g (1 = given), tricky}`, balance `{f (art names), v, eqs: [{l, lc, r, rc}], q, form}`, target `{nums, target, ops, use (2 = two tiles, 0 = all), sols}`. Pictures are the drawn ART apple, banana, cookie, cake, ball, gift (said "present").
- Tests: named check `number puzzles P3-10` (2,400 puzzles: every band, levels 1-12, 20 days): `make()` deterministic; pyramids unique by an independent search over the bottom row in whole numbers (forced bricks, bounded by the covering givens; a bottom brick under no given counts as infinite), bottom numbers in the band's range, every brick the sum below, givens per level, propagation where required and the tricky flag; balances unique by a brute force over a box (all pictures but one tried, the last forced) plus a cofactor determinant, values in range, trays not overfull, triangular in B and C, not in D (no scale with one unknown), C's last question, all five D forms seen, a wrong weight tips toward the heavier side; targets by the set of values each subset of tiles can make, tile and target ranges, signs per band, level 1 has more solutions than level 12 on average; `solve()` replayed with independent arithmetic; at every step tiers 1-2 never fill or join and never say the answer (a number the child cannot already see), tier 3 always finishes; make() under 8 ms average. Mutation-checked: removing the rank and propagation checks makes it fail with "Infinity solutions" / "2 solutions".
- Smoke block `// P3-10`: at 380x780 grade 3 (band C) and 1024x600 grade 5 (band D) Home -> Brain Teasers -> each type -> level 1 by real taps (`tools/lib/bt-num.js`): a wrong entry (counted; the balance tips and shows the guess), hint tiers 1 and 2 (something glows and is said, nothing filled), a right move, back to the levels and the hub, Keep going resumes the same puzzle and state, the rest tapped in, win card, stars and level 2 saved; plus band A (grade 0) balance at 380x780 (and pyramid and target hidden there). 48 px targets, no sideways scroll, no emoji chrome on every screen.
- Test expectation changed: the P3-01 smoke hub check expected exactly one type card (`hub.cards !== 1`) because no real type existed; it now expects the fake type's card and one card per type offered in the band (`BT.offered().length`). Two older smoke expectations failed on the untouched base commit (669471c, v91) as well, so check.sh could not pass: the `// P2-09` block wanted Factor Factory to be the last math world (Decimal Desert, P2-10, now follows it) and the `// P2-10` block wanted a fresh 4th grader's map to open on the desert (it opens on Factor Factory, the first grade-4 world; then a 30 s `waitForSelector` timeout). Now: Factor Factory is the last world or the one right before Decimal Desert; the desert block expects the map on Factor Factory when it exists, taps the open desert chip, and plays on as before. Nothing else changed.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-num-hub bt-num-pyr-b bt-num-pyr-c bt-num-pyr-d bt-num-bal-a bt-num-bal-b bt-num-bal-c bt-num-bal-d bt-num-tgt-b bt-num-tgt-c bt-num-tgt-d bt-num-win`, three sizes): looked at pyr-d and bal-c at 380x780 (bal-c with a tipped scale), bal-c, bal-a, pyr-c, tgt-c, tgt-d at 1024x600, bal-d at 600x960, tgt-b, tgt-d, bal-b, the win card and the hub at 380x780. Fixed from them: names cut in the top bar, scales too small and the pad too tall for three scales on a phone, the talk line stretched in landscape, scale highlights overlapping.
- Not done: the Magic Square (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.6 lists it under the pyramid chunk for bands C-D); the three types are what this chunk was given. Drag is not used anywhere, so no tap-only alternative was needed; Undo exists where a step can be undone (Target) — pyramid and balance never keep a wrong entry.
- Merge: built on branch P3-10-numpuz from v91 in parallel with P3-08 (mazes) and P3-09 (patterns). Overlaps: appended blocks in test.js, smoke.js, shots.js; the P3-01 smoke hub-count line and the P2-09 / P2-10 smoke world checks above (the other chunks may make the same changes: keep one); `Chunk status` and this log. VER is provisional.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v94 -> v95 at merge.

### P3-09 Patterns, matrices and odd one out (v95, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.5 and 7): one new puzzle type `pattern` ("Patterns", icon `skill-pattern`, bands ABCD, 12 levels, 1 mistake still earns 3 stars) in a new file `src/js/76-bt-pattern.js` (object `PAT`), with its look in `src/css/49-bt-pattern.css`. A puzzle is a short set of items (band A 3, B-D 4), one tap answers an item; a progress row of dots shows where the child is. Item kinds: `seq` "What comes next?" (pictures, 4 choices), `num` (numbers, 4 choices), `odd` "Which one does not belong?" (4 pictures, one attribute splits them 3-1), `ana` 2x2 "A is to B as C is to ?" (4 choices), `mat` 3x3 with the bottom-right cell missing (6 choices: 4 differ from the answer in one attribute, 1 in two).
- What each band gets (levels 1-4 / 5-8 / 9-12; the rule count grows over the levels, section 7):
  - A (PK-K, 3 items, the question is spoken by itself): next in AB / ABC / AAB, ABB, AABB cycles of shape; odd one out by shape, then by size or count, then with a distracting 2-2 split; a second sequence (size or count AB, then AAB/ABB/size ABC, then +1 count or two attributes moving together).
  - B (1-2, 4 items in an order that changes with the level): numbers +n then -n and bigger steps (up to 100); shape sequences with cycles, +1 / -1 count, arrows turning 45 or 90 degrees, up to three attributes; 2x2 analogies with one, then two changes (size jumps small/big so it reads next to a count); odd one out with one, then two distracting 2-2 splits.
  - C (3-4): two 3x3 matrices with 1, then 2 changing attributes (row rules: constant in the row, each value once, steady growth; shapes, sizes, counts, arrow turns); numbers x2, x3, alternating +a/+b, squares, big +n; sequences with arrows turning and two or three attributes.
  - D (5-6): two matrices with 2, then 3 changing attributes (the last tier without "constant in the row"); numbers x a + b, Fibonacci-style, triangular, squares plus a constant, two interleaved sequences; sequences where two or three attributes change with different cycle lengths.
- Uniqueness: a picture tile is shape (6 + arrow), colour (6), size (3), count (1-5), turn (8 steps, arrows only). `make()` builds each item from its rule, then keeps it only if every changing attribute of the shown part allows exactly one next value under the whole rule library (a cycle of 1-4 seen at least once, steady growth; for numbers also x, x a + b, second differences, growing differences, Fibonacci, alternating, interleaved, each needing one shown term more than it has parameters) and exactly one choice fits, with and without colour. Matrices are read by rows or by columns, attribute by attribute (the spec's "same holds reading by columns").
- Colour is never the only cue: colour is not an attribute of its own; inside an item it follows shape, size or count (or stays one colour), so two tiles of different colours always differ in something else too, and no two choices differ only in colour. Right and wrong use the `.choice` badges (tick, amber cross), not colour alone.
- Hints (per item; the frame counts tiers): 1 names the attributes to watch ("Look at the shapes and the sizes."), 2 states the rule for a row ("In each row there is one star, one square and one heart.", "Each number is double the one before.", "From the first picture to the second, it gets bigger."), 3 takes away two wrong choices (fewer if fewer are left; after that the item has no more hints and the button does nothing that counts). Words are shown in a yellow hint line and spoken.
- Drawing: tiles are inline SVG built per tile (no ids, defs, filters or gradients): a soft `--ledge-soft` shadow down-right, the shade tone, the base tone shrunk and moved toward the top-left light, a white gloss stroke; colours are `--ill-*` tokens through `.pt-c0`..`.pt-c5`. Boards are a `--bt-tint` tray with white ledged cells, the missing cell a dashed purple "?" box that fills with the answer (pop) when solved. Portrait: board on top, choices below; landscape tablets: matrix and 2x2 board left, choices right; long rows wrap into two rows on phones. Choices are at least 64 px.
- State (`api.saveState`): `{i, miss, h, x, g}` (item, mistakes, this item's hint tier, wrong taps, choices removed). Nothing new in `S` beyond the framework's `S.bt` records.
- Tests: test.js new named check "patterns P3-09": 1,152 puzzles (4 bands x 12 levels x 24 days, 4,320 items): `make()` deterministic, item and choice counts, exactly one choice fits under an independent rule checker written in the test (a little more generous than the app's: also third differences and rational x a + b), with colour and with colour ignored, and it is `solve()`'s answer; colour is a function of the other attributes; no two choices look the same without colour; distractors share most features (pictures differ in 1-2 attributes, matrices exactly 1,1,1,1,2; numbers within max(12, 60%) of the answer); tiles drawable and in range; hint words present and hint 2 never names a number answer; kinds and number rules per band; matrix changing attributes C 1-2, D 2-3; average rule count grows by band (1.07, 1.31, 1.71, 2.50) and over the tiers of every band; `make()` about 1 ms. Checked that the test catches a broken generator (a 3-1 noise split in odd-one-out made it fail).
- Smoke: new `// P3-09` block at the end of the main sequence: band A at 380x780 and band D at 1024x600, from Home -> Brain Teasers -> Patterns -> level 1: a wrong tap (amber, disabled, mistake saved), hint tiers 1, 2, 3 with the 10 s cooldown (words differ, two choices removed, the answer kept), every item answered by real taps (the "?" box fills), win card with 1 star (3 hints), stars and level 2 saved; buttons 48 px (choices 64), nothing outside, no sideways scroll on the page or in the puzzle, no emoji chrome.
- Test expectations changed: (1) P3-01 smoke "hub" expected exactly 1 type card (only the fake type existed); it now expects as many cards as `BT.offered()` (the fake type plus Patterns). (2) Two smoke steps already failing on the base commit 669471c (checked by opening the base build: a new 4th grader opens on world 10, Factor Factory): P2-09 required Factor Factory to be the last math world and P2-10 required a new 4th grader to open on Decimal Desert; since P2-09 and P2-10 merged, the factory sits before the desert. P2-09 now accepts the factory last or just before the desert; P2-10 expects the first grade-4 world and taps the desert's world chip before playing it. No test.js expectation changed.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-pat-levels bt-pat-a-seq bt-pat-a-odd bt-pat-a-grow bt-pat-b-num bt-pat-b-ana bt-pat-b-seq bt-pat-b-odd bt-pat-c-mat bt-pat-c-num bt-pat-d-mat bt-pat-d-seq bt-pat-d-num bt-pat-win`): looked at most of them at 380x780, several at 1024x600 and 600x960 (montages). Fixed from looking: matrix choices fell under the hint bar at 380x780 (smaller board on short phones), the 2x2 choices ran off the bottom at 1024x600, sizes were hard to tell apart next to counts (wider size steps, small/big jumps in analogies), the square's gloss looked like a corner bracket, six-picture rows wrapped needlessly.
- check.sh: check OK (test.js 73 of 73, smoke OK). index.html 1,371,539 bytes (+41.7 KB: 76-bt-pattern.js 36.3 KB, a third of it comments and hint words; 49-bt-pattern.css 5.3 KB).
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files only (new 76-bt-pattern.js and 49-bt-pattern.css; tests, smoke, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v95 (provisional, renumber at merge) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-09-patterns from 669471c (v91) in parallel with P3-08 (mazes) and P3-10 (number puzzles). Overlaps are appends: tools/test.js (one block before `// ---- report ----`), tools/smoke.js (one block after the P3-01 block, plus the P3-01 hub-count line and the P2-09/P2-10 lines above, which another chunk may fix the same way: keep one), tools/shots.js (one `Object.assign(SCREENS, ...)` block before `main`), built files (rebuild). Renumber VER at merge.

### P2-11 5th grade: world Volcano Lab (v90, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM M-5a and 2.1 row 13, ROADMAP P2-11): world `m_volcano` (Volcano Lab, grade 5, volcano icon) after `m_factory`, 9 stops in CURRICULUM order: pv5 Decimal Place Value, mbig5 3-Digit Times 2-Digit, dec3 Add/Subtract Decimals, pv6 Powers of Ten, dbig4 Divide by 2-Digit, dec4 Multiply Decimals, round4 Round Decimals, ooo1 Order of Operations, dec5 Divide Decimals (titles 22 characters or less), then the boss.
- Skills: new `dec` (Decimals, max 5, levels 3-5 only) and `ooo` (Order of Operations, max 1); extended `pv` 4 -> 6, `mbig` 4 -> 5, `dbig` 3 -> 4, `round` 3 -> 4. Every extension is a level dispatcher in `src/js/38-skills.js` that leaves old levels on their old generators (the new test proves the output is identical for 60 seeds per old level). All generators are one block `// ---- P2-11 Volcano Lab ----` at the end of `src/js/35-gen-math-upper.js` (names `vl*`).
- Levels: pv5 value of an underlined digit (4 choices) / expanded form / words (spoken, with "and" at the point) to a decimal, thousandths; pv6 x and / by 10, 100, 1,000 (whole and decimal), `10^n`, `4.2 x 10^2`; mbig5 3 x 2 digits with the standard algorithm (`stepsMul(a, b, 'std')`), max 6; dbig4 divisors 11-50, 3- and 4-digit dividends, half with a remainder (`sep:'R'`, `stepsDiv`); dec3 add and subtract with ragged places and whole numbers mixed in, vertical (`stepsAdd`, `stepsSub`); dec4 decimal x whole, then tenths x tenths and tenths x hundredths; dec5 decimal / whole, whole / 2, 4, 5, 8 with a decimal quotient, then / tenths (some with a hundredths dividend); round4 whole number, tenth, hundredth (round half up, never an answer ending in 0); ooo1 half "evaluate" (13 expression shapes with parentheses, 2 or 3 operations, every step a whole number, results 1-200) and half "write the expression" (four choices, one matches the sentence).
- Decimal answers: pad `dec`, `eq:'value'` (so 0.20 and 6.0 are also right), canonical `ans` with no trailing zero ("0.2", "6"); `q.max` is set from the digit count whenever it is over 4. Say text speaks symbols as words ("open parenthesis").
- New step builders in `src/js/39b-steps.js` (block `// ---- P2-11 decimal multiply and divide`): `stepsDecMul(a, b)` (a "Places" step that counts the places of the answer, then the whole-number steps of `stepsMul` on the digits; the last step asks and says where the point goes) and `stepsDecDiv(a, b)` (for a divisor in tenths, a "multiply both by 10" step; zeros are added to the dividend until it divides evenly, then the `stepsDiv` steps; the last step says where the point goes). Neither shows the answer. New step records: `dp`, `ten`, `dd`.
- dec 1-2 (Decimal Desert, P2-10) are not here. `dec` has `max: 5`; `vlDecLv` in 35-gen-math-upper.js sends levels 1-2 to the level 3 generator so a call never throws. `skLvEmpty` (37-comp.js) says `dec` levels below 3 are empty (clause marked P2-11), so every test loop skips them, and `tools/test.js` lists `dec:[1,2]` in `RESERVED` (every-level-is-reachable check). When P2-10 lands: delete the `sk=='dec'&&lv<3` clause of `skLvEmpty`, delete `dec:[1,2]` from RESERVED, make `vlDecLv` (and the `dec:` line in 38-skills.js) dispatch levels 1-2 to the P2-10 generators, and keep the three Volcano stops.
- Tests changed (old -> new) and why: P2-07 "stops in CURRICULUM order" `SK.round.max` 3 -> 4 and P2-09 "stops in CURRICULUM order" `pv` 4 -> 6, `mbig` 4 -> 5, `dbig` 3 -> 4, `round` 3 -> 4 (those skills grew; their own levels are checked unchanged). P2-09 fixtures check: it removes and re-adds Factor Factory to compare maps; it now also removes the worlds after it (`m_desert`, `m_volcano`) first, because a world that opens behind Factor Factory must lock when Factor Factory is added (nothing a child had changes: the new world has no stars). `tools/emoji-allow.txt` got the abacus (Unicode 11, not risky). Smoke P2-09: the check "Factor Factory is the last world" became "has a world before it" because Volcano Lab now follows it.
- Tests added: "Volcano Lab P2-11: stops ... decimal step builders": stop order, world grade/name/position, 10 stops, names; old levels unchanged; 3,000 seeded questions for each of the 9 levels with the maths recomputed on BigInt (decimal answers compared exactly as normalised strings; sums, differences, products, quotients checked by cross-multiplying; rounding half up; an own order-of-operations parser checks results, whole intermediate values and that exactly one "write" choice matches its sentence), `kindProblems` zero errors, say/label (under 60)/hint/q.max, hints never show the answer (checked on decimals and 3+ digit answers), reproducible, shares of each form; plus 3,000 problems each for `stepsDecMul` and `stepsDecDiv` in the style of the P2-08 check (every number of a step on show, last step asks, whole board rows, no symbols in the spoken text, the steps equal `stepsMul`/`stepsDiv` of the whole numbers, N / B read with p places is exactly a / b). "Volcano Lab P2-11: a world added after Factor Factory ... (fixtures)": 60 saves with and without the world (6 fixtures x 9 grades plus a half played and a finished Factor Factory and a Volcano Lab save): no star moves, nothing re-locks, the map opens where it did.
- Smoke `// P2-11` (last block of the main sequence, midway fixture, grade 5, 380x780): the world is open with 9 stops; every stop's first question fits (no sideways scroll, keys incl. the decimal point and choices 48 px or more); eight stops played with real taps (pv5, mbig5, dec3, dbig4, dec4, round4, ooo1, dec5), with decimal answers typed on the pad and stars saved; Help tapped through every step on dec4, dec5, mbig5 (button reads Next step, last step asks).
- Screenshots (`node tools/shots.js vl-map vl-pv5-value vl-pv5-words vl-pv6 vl-mbig5 vl-dec3 vl-dec4 vl-dec5 vl-dbig4 vl-round4 vl-ooo-eval vl-ooo-write vl-steps-dec4 vl-steps-dec5 vl-steps-mbig5`) looked at at 380x780.
- Merge: built on branch P2-11-volcano from b6dd0b7 in parallel with P2-10 (Decimal Desert). Conflicts to expect: `src/js/38-skills.js` (`dec:` line, `pv`, `mbig`, `dbig`, `round` dispatchers: keep every level of both chunks), `42-paths.js` (m_desert goes between m_factory and m_volcano), `37-comp.js` (`skLvEmpty`), the `RESERVED` line and the `mx` tables in test.js, 35-gen-math-upper.js (both blocks appended). ### P3-02 Dev-time solver core and sudoku banks (v92, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1 and 6): new dev-time folder `tools/puzzles/` (Node, never shipped) with a reusable solver core, the sudoku generator and grader, and the bank tools; generated bank `src/data/d-puz-sudoku.js` (240 puzzles). No UI: the bank is data only and nothing in the app reads it yet (P3-03 does).
- Core API (header comment of each file): `csp.js` takes a plain problem `{n, size, groups, cons, givens, dom}`: `groups` are all-different cell lists (rows, columns, boxes, jigsaw regions; a group of `size` cells is a house and also gets hidden-single propagation), `cons` are custom constraints `{cells, test(vals)}` (MathDoku cages, sums) pruned by enumerating the cells' candidates. Calls: `countSolutions(p, limit = 2, {stats})`, `solve(p, {rng})` (random full grid with a seeded rng), `solutions(p, limit)`, `stats.nodes` as a propagation-only difficulty score (what P3-04 asks for), helpers `sudokuGroups(N, bw, bh)`, `latinGroups(N)`. `human.js` `grade({size, houses, givens})` solves step by step with the easiest technique that works (1 last cell, 2 naked single, 3 hidden single, 4 locked candidates for any two overlapping houses, 5 naked pair) and returns `{solved, grade, grid, used, steps}`; grade 99 means it would need guessing. `rng.js` `rng(seedString)` with `int`, `shuffle`, `pick`. `sudoku.js` `makePuzzle`, `genSlot`, `buildBank`, `encode`/`decode`, `canon`/`bankKey`, `fileText`. `build-banks.js` holds a `TYPES` registry (file, seed, count) that P3-04 and P3-06 extend; `verify-banks.js` re-checks a bank with its own counter.
- Generator (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1): randomized backtracking fills a grid, givens are removed in random order (180-degree symmetric pairs for 9x9) and put back whenever the solution count is not 1, stop at the tier's givens, then grade and reject anything the band does not allow. Tiers (givens, allowed grades): A 4x4 10/9/8, grade 1 only; B 4x4 8/6/5, grades 1-3; C 6x6 22/18/14, grades 1-3; D 9x9 42/36/31, grades 1-3 for easy and medium, 4-5 for hard (hard must need locked candidates or a naked pair). Duplicates are rejected; for 4x4 and 6x6 "duplicate" means equal under the runtime transforms (symbol relabel, row and band swaps, column and stack swaps, transpose), so no two bank puzzles can look the same to a child.
- Bank format: `PUZ.sudoku[band] = {n, bw, bh, easy: [...], medium: [...], hard: [...]}`, each puzzle `'givens|solution|grade'` (`.` for an empty cell, cells in reading order; boxes are bw columns x bh rows). The spec's `givens|solution` plus a one-digit grade, so the UI and the test both know the technique (BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1 step 5 updated). Counts: 20 per band and tier, 240 in all. Grades in the bank: A all 1; B easy 8 x 1 and 12 x 2, B medium and hard all 2; C easy and medium all 2, C hard 19 x 2 and 1 x 3; D easy 19 x 2 and 1 x 3, D medium 17 x 2 and 3 x 3, D hard all 4.
- Determinism: each slot (band, tier, index) has its own rng seeded `star-school-sudoku-1|band|tier|index` and avoids the puzzles generated before it. `node tools/puzzles/build-banks.js sudoku` writes the file in about 4 s; `node tools/puzzles/build-banks.js --check` regenerates in memory and reported `same` (byte for byte) on this branch.
- Tests (test.js, named `sudoku banks P3-02`, before the report): loads the bank on its own and with all of src/js and src/data (same object), checks it is in the built index.html, then for every puzzle (verify-banks.js): format, 20 per tier, givens count for the tier, the solution is a valid grid, givens agree with it and do not clash, 9x9 givens symmetric, exactly one solution by an independent plain backtracking counter (`countSimple`, no code shared with csp.js), the human grader finishes it on the stored solution with the stored grade, grade inside the tier's range, no duplicates. The counter is itself checked (288 full 4x4 grids, an empty 9x9 is ambiguous, a changed given gives 0), csp.js agrees on the first puzzle of every tier, and the first and last slot of every band and tier (24) are regenerated from the seed and compared with the stored string. Shown to fail on a wrong grade, a puzzle with too few givens and two solutions, and a duplicate.
- check.sh: build, contrast, hex guard, test.js 69 of 69, smoke OK (no console errors, no network); check OK on the third run. The first two runs failed only the existing `P2-21 grammar` smoke step (a random level 8 question with a cut-off choice, which also happens on the unchanged v88 build; see Ideas and issues).
- Size: index.html +22,245 bytes (the data file; 1,242,592 -> 1,264,837), under the 1,500,000 fail limit (it was already over the 1,200,000 warning).
- Docs: CONTRIBUTING section 4 lists `build-banks.js`, `--check` and `verify-banks.js`; BRAIN_TEASERS 5.1 step 5 (format with grade) and the section 6 file table (`rng.js`).
- Files: new `tools/puzzles/csp.js`, `human.js`, `rng.js`, `sudoku.js`, `build-banks.js`, `verify-banks.js`, `src/data/d-puz-sudoku.js`; edited `tools/test.js`, `tools/build.py` (VER v92, provisional), `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/BRAIN_TEASERS.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`, built output.
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files [x] VER v92 (provisional) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js (nothing user-visible changed, no new smoke block) [x] no screenshots needed (no UI) [x] fixtures unchanged [x] size [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Merge: built on branch P3-02-solver from v88; renumber VER at merge.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v91 -> v92 at merge.

### P3-01 Brain Teasers hub and puzzle framework (v92, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: new `src/js/74-teasers.js` (the `BT` object: registry, saving, rewards, hub, level strip, play frame, win card), `src/css/48-teasers.css`, a Brain Teasers card on Home between the learning paths and the Game Room (`#btcard`, `46-home.js`), and one line in `view()` (`40-view.js`) that calls `BT.stop()` so a puzzle's timer and instance are cleaned up when the screen changes. No puzzle type ships in this chunk and no demo type was added (the spec does not ask for one): the hub shows "Puzzles are on their way" until a type registers. The framework is tested and screenshotted with a fake type (`tools/lib/bt-fake.js`).
- Framework API (the same text is the header comment of 74-teasers.js). A type file `75-bt-<id>.js` calls `BT.reg({id, name, icon, bands, levels?, allow?, rules?, make, mount, solve})` at load time:
  - `id` lowercase letters and digits, unique, not `cur`, `daily`, `badges`, `earn`, `rnd`; `bands` letters of ABCD; `icon` a name in ICONS (unknown falls back to `puzzle` until P4 draws the 19 type icons); `levels(band)` defaults to 12; `allow` mistakes that still earn 3 stars (default 2); `rules` one line with a speak button on the level strip. `BT.reg` throws on a bad definition, so a typo fails the test, not the tablet.
  - `make(band, level, rnd)` returns a JSON-safe puzzle (round-tripped through JSON by `BT.make`). `rnd()` is seeded by `type|band|level|day`, so level N is the same puzzle for the day and a different one tomorrow; the globals `rnd`, `R`, `pick`, `shuffle` are the seeded stream while `make` runs (restored in a `finally`). Endless uses level 9-12 (the last four levels) with the count of Endless solves as extra seed.
  - `mount(el, puzzle, api)` draws into `el` and returns `{hint(tier), destroy()}`. `api.win({mistakes, hints, sec})` (once), `api.sfx`, `api.say(text)`, `api.saveState(obj)` after every move, `api.loadState()` on mount (null when new), `api.band`, `api.level`. `hint(tier)` gets 1, 2, 3 (a fourth press repeats 3) and returns `false` when it gave nothing (then nothing is counted). `destroy()` must stop the puzzle's own timers. The frame owns: top bar with back, timer chip (bands C and D only; seconds are counted in every band), the Hint button (3 pips, 10 s cooldown), Restart (asks first), the win card, stars, game time, unlocking and saving. A puzzle whose `make` or `mount` throws goes back to the level strip with a toast and an entry in the error log.
  - `solve(puzzle)` is only for tests and the smoke bot. Registration order does not matter; the hub sorts by the catalog order of BRAIN_TEASERS section 4 (`BT.ORDER`).
  - Useful for later chunks: `BT.make(id, band, level, {day, salt})` (property tests), `BT.rate(hints, mistakes, allow)`, `BT.finish(run, {mistakes, hints, sec})` (pays and saves; what the win card calls), `BT.band()`, `BT.bandOf(grade)`, `BT.unreg(id)` (tests).
- Save (no migration, `LATEST` stays 6; every field read with a default, a damaged `S.bt` is repaired): `S.bt[type][band] = {lvl, stars: [0-3 per level], endless}` with `lvl` the highest unlocked level (12 solved = 13, Endless open); `S.bt.cur = {id, band, level (number or 'E'), puzzle, state, hints, sec, day}` (one unfinished puzzle, stored whole so it resumes exactly even on another day); `S.bt.daily = {day, done: [ids], pick: {id, band, level}}` (`pick` is an addition to the spec so the pick stays put all day); `S.bt.earn = {day, sec}` (game time puzzles earned today, for the cap); `S.bt.rnd = {day, n}` (solves today). `S.bt.badges` is reserved for P3-17. The roadmap table line for `S.bt` (`{type: {lvl, stars, seen, best}}`) is older than BRAIN_TEASERS section 3; the spec shape was followed.
- Rewards (section 9): 3 stars = no hint and mistakes within `allow`, 2 = at most one hint, 1 = finished. First solve of a level: its stars added to `S.stars` and game time 45/60/90/120 s (bands A-D) x stars/3 with a minimum of half. A replay or an Endless solve: 15 s, and only stars above the level's best are paid (the difference). The Puzzle of the Day (the pick for the child's band, at their next level, stable all day) pays 5 coins and 60 s once a day when its level is solved. Game time from puzzles is capped at half of the grown-up's daily cap (`ptCfg().max`, no cap when 0): once the cap is reached the win card says so and pays 0. Every third solve of the day counts as one round the way a learning round does (`day().r`, `streakTick()`, `actRound()`, the daily mission with its +5 coins and sticker). Solved time goes to the activity log under `bt`. Hint use lowers stars only.
- Bands: from `S.grade` (PK and K = A, 1-2 = B, 3-4 = C, 5-6 = D). A type is hidden in the hub when it does not offer the child's band; the level strip offers the child's band, the one below (always) and the one above after 10 stars in the child's band, as chips PK-K / 1-2 / 3-4 / 5-6.
- Tests: named check `teaser framework P3-01` (registration and its refusals, catalog order, bands, seeded puzzles incl. the global RNG restored after a throw, stars and game time tables, first solve / replay / improvement / worse replay, unlocking, Endless, the cap and its daily reset, three solves = one round, the Puzzle of the Day paid once, every fixture loads with no `S.bt` and nothing else changed by reading, damaged `S.bt` values, a save round trip). Smoke block `// P3-01` at 380x780 and 1024x600 plays the fake type through the real UI: Home card, hub, level strip with locked levels, play, hint tiers with cooldown, state saved, back, Keep going resumes exactly, restart sheet, win card (stars, game time), Next level, back to Home. No existing test expectation changed.
- Shots (`node tools/shots.js bt-hub-empty bt-hub bt-levels bt-play bt-win`): looked at the hub, level strip, play and win card at 380x780 and the hub at 1024x600. They use the fake type.
- Not done, left on purpose: the small Puzzle of the Day card on Home and the badges (P3-17); the "fail or quit the same level 3 times, offer the previous band" rule (BRAIN_TEASERS section 7); an arrow-pad setting, undo and speak buttons are each puzzle's job. The Home card uses the generic `puzzle` icon, not a drawn scene like the path cards.
- Merge: built on a parallel branch, renumbered v90 -> v91 at merge.

### P2-10 4th grade: world Decimal Desert (v90, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM M-4b and 2.1 row 12, ROADMAP P2-10): new world `m_desert` "Decimal Desert" (grade 4, after `m_frac`; P2-09 inserts Factor Factory between them at merge) with the 14 stops in CURRICULUM order: frac 6, frac 7, fop 1, frac 8, fop 2, dec 1, dec 2, ang 1, ang 2, geo 3, shape 6, meas 5, pat 4, story 7 (titles 22 letters or less) and its boss. New skills `fop` (2 levels), `dec` (2), `ang` (2); extended `geo` (3), `shape` (6), `meas` (5), `pat` (4), `story` (7) with dispatchers in `src/js/38-skills.js` (existing levels untouched). `frac` 6-8 were already written by P2-06; this chunk only gave them stops (`RESERVED` in test.js is now empty).
- Generators: new file `src/js/35-gen-math-upper.js`, one block `// ---- P2-10 Decimal Desert ----` (prefix `ds`, so P2-09 appends its own block). `fop 1` like-denominator add and subtract (answer as a fraction, may exceed 1; the last third uses mixed numbers with regrouping, answer in the three-slot mixed pad), `fop 2` whole x fraction with a repeated-addition bar picture (`dsRep`), `dec 1` fraction <-> decimal for tenths and hundredths, 10x10 grid and 1x10 strip, number lines to tenths and to hundredths, `dec 2` compare decimals (hundredths, thousandths in half the items, lengths always differ, 14% the same number written two ways), `ang 1` acute/right/obtuse/straight (a picture, or pick the picture), `ang 2` protractor (multiples of 5) and missing angle (right, straight, a given whole; any degree in half of them), `geo 3` missing side from area or perimeter, L-shape area (`dsLShape`), `shape 6` lines of symmetry (count 0-6 or "is this dotted line one?"), parallel/perpendicular/neither, triangle by angles or by sides, `meas 5` ten larger-to-smaller conversions (table in the hint only), `pat 4` in/out tables (fill the last box, or pick the rule), `story 7` division stories where the remainder is read (round up, drop, or it is the answer; a third have a multiply first).
- Step hints: `frac 6` (common-bottom branch) now uses `stepsCommonDen(.., 'cmp')`; `dec 2`, the `fop` levels, `ang 2`, `geo 3`, `meas 5`, `pat 4` use text-only steps (`dsHint`, built on `ST.step`); `story 7` uses `stepsStory`. Every step hint ends in a "?" and never states the answer.
- Look: `src/css/44-desert.css` (3 lines: `.choices.dsw`, one column for the long words Perpendicular / Equilateral). Pictures reuse VIZ (`fracBar`, `areaGrid`, `numLine`, `angle` with and without `proto`, `rectSides`, `shape`, `tableInOut`) plus four small inline SVGs (repeated bars, L-shape, a shape with a dotted line, two lines) using the existing viz classes.
- Tests (test.js 65 of 65): "Decimal Desert P2-10" 3,000 seeded questions per new level (11 levels) with the maths recomputed from the label and the picture (angles looked up from the drawn svg, the L-shape area by shoelace on its path, symmetry counted by reflecting the drawn corners, parallel/perpendicular measured from the drawn lines, in/out rules re-applied to every row, no second rule fits), kindProblems zero, no hint states the answer, reproducible per seed, each variant a fair share; stops read from CURRICULUM.md row 12; and a save check (fixtures x 9 grades + 3 synthetic saves, against a path without the desert): no star moves, nothing re-locks, the map opens where it did.
- Test expectations changed (each is a legitimate growth, nothing weakened): P2-06 "frac stops use levels" now `1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8`, and frac 6 hints are step hints (the old text-regex check became a check of the step record: both fractions renamed to one bottom that fits, tops agree with the answer); P2-07 `SK` maxima `geo 3, shape 6, meas 5, story 7`; the P2-07 fixture check cuts the math path after the forest while it runs (otherwise the map of a finished child legitimately opens on the desert). smoke.js: the Pre-K to grade 3 Grown-ups step checked that the LAST math world is open for a 3rd grader; the desert (grade 4) is now last, so it checks the last world of grade 3 or lower. The one allowed map move in the new save check: a child with every earlier stop passed, or a 4th grader and up whose map would otherwise end on the last world, now opens on the desert (same rule as Treasure Bay in P2-04).
- Smoke (`// P2-10` block, last in the main sequence): a 4th grader opens the math map on the desert without any unlock, plays Unlike Fractions (with a step hint) and one angle and one decimal round by taps, measures 13 stops at 380x780 (48 px keys and choices, no sideways scroll, pictures where they should be), stars saved. Shots `dd-*` (21 screens; looked at them at 380x780 in montages).
- Found and not changed: protractor reading is multiples of 5 only (CURRICULUM says "later any degree"; the scale has marks every 10, so any degree would not be readable; "any degree" is used in the missing-angle items instead). `shape 6` symmetry covers regular and common shapes only (no letters, no circle). The placement quiz passes one world per testlet, so one more world on the math path pushes an all-right child one world further from the end (the 5-testlet cap, see P2-03).
- Flake seen (not this chunk): the P2-21 grammar smoke step (`gram 8` stop, `cut` = a choice wider than its button) fails when the random level 8 question has a long choice; the same check on the P2-08 build gives 5 of 40 questions cut off, on this build 8 of 40. It failed in 2 of 3 full runs here and passed in the third.

### P2-28 5th grade passages (v86, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed: `COMPSPEC[5]` and 10 original grade 5 passages appended at the end of `src/data/d-comp.js` (never reordered): stories g5f01-g5f05 (Sam's Soccer Switch, The Quilt for Grandpa, Hiro and the Missing Key, Marcus and the Solo, The Borrowed Telescope) and nonfiction g5n01-g5n05 (Built for the Desert, Two Ways to Cross a River, Reading the Rings of a Tree, From Sand to Glass, Gift of the Nile); topics and titles avoid the ones other grades already use. Each has 5 questions with 4 choices: inference with evidence, compare two things, point of view, vocabulary in context, and theme (stories) or author's purpose (nonfiction). 50 questions.
- Spec: 180-230 words (passages are 181-209), at most 17 words per sentence on average and 26 in any sentence, Flesch-Kincaid at most 8.5 (they measure 3.6-5.9). Right answer is the longest choice in 20 of 50 questions (limit 50%); no choice is longer than 60 characters (bank limit); the first evidence sentence of every inference and vocabulary question is short enough for the hint to quote it.
- Stops: `comp 6` ("Read a Story 4") and `info 4` ("Learn About It 4") at the end of `r_island` (Author Island), after `spell 6`. `SK.comp.max` is 6 and `SK.info.max` is 4 in `38-skills.js`.
- Save safety: stars are keyed by stop id, no migration. The new stops are appended to an existing world: a child who finished Author Island sees both as NEW, open, and never as the current stop; no star moves and nothing re-locks (checked against a map without the two stops over 6 fixtures x 6 grades plus finished-world cases).
- Tests: `tools/test.js` new check "passages grade 5 P2-28" (validator, counts, theme or purpose question, 3,000 seeded questions each for comp 6 and info 4 with zero kindProblems, hint, say and label checks, hints quote evidence, a round of 10 per skill, save safety; stops are found by filtering to comp/info stops so other chunks' stops do not matter). The P2-25 data check loops over every grade, so grade 5 is covered there too.
- Expectations changed in existing tests: SK.comp.max is now 6 and SK.info.max 4, but comp 4-5 and info 2-3 (grades 3-4) have no passages until P2-26 and P2-27 merge. Five loops in test.js that walk levels 1..max (generator fuzz, every-level-has-a-node, per-level variety, 300-seed all-skills scan, review rebuild and the review model) now skip a comp/info level with no passages via `__hasLv` (set in the first eval; it is true for every level that has passages, so after P2-26/27 merge nothing is skipped and the helper can stay). Nothing else changed.

Stop titles are 22 characters or fewer.
- Skills: new `dbig` (Long Division, 3 levels) and `fact` (Factors & Primes, 2 levels); extended `mfact` 4 -> 5, `pv` 3 -> 4, `mbig` 1 -> 4, `big` 5 -> 7, `sub` 7 -> 8, `round` 2 -> 3. Each existing skill got a level dispatcher in `src/js/38-skills.js` (old levels call the old generator, so their output is byte-identical). P2-10 appends its own block to the same file.
- Levels: mfact 5 facts with 11 and 12 (half) then all facts to 12 x 12, 30% missing factor (hint: split 12 into 10 and 2, or the inverse division); pv 4 four forms (value of an underlined digit among 4 values, expanded form -> number, words -> digits with the words visible and read aloud, compare with < = >); mbig 2 (2-digit x 1), 3 (3- and 4-digit x 1, `max` 5), 4 (2-digit x 2-digit) vertical with `stepsMul` partial products; big 6 (two 4-digit, at least one carry, `max` 5), big 7 (5- and 6-digit, a third with three addends, `max` 7) with `stepsAdd`; sub 8 (4- to 6-digit, a regroup in every item, zeros in the top number in about 55%, `max` 6) with `stepsSub`; dbig 1 (2-digit / 1-digit, quotient 11-49, ldiv layout or equation), 2 (remainder, `sep:'R'`, `ST.ldiv` with `SLOT_R`), 3 (3- and 4-digit, half with a remainder; no remainder uses a plain box) with `stepsDiv`; fact 1 (which is a factor of N, tap ALL factors of N from six numbers via the `multi` kind, which is a multiple of B), fact 2 (prime or composite for 2-100; which of four is prime, three tricky composites); round 3 (any place from tens to hundred thousands, numbers 4 to 6 digits, answers to 999,999) with a three-step `stepsStory` hint that ends on the decision, not the number.

P2-07 fixture check: a forest finished with everything else passed used to open the map on the forest boss; it now opens on `mfact5`, the first stop of the world after it (the map opens on the first open, unpassed stop, and Factor Factory is open once the forest is finished). smoke.js "grade 3 from Grown-ups": it asked whether the last math world is open for a 3rd grader; Factor Factory (grade 4) is now last, so it asks about Fraction Forest by id (the last grade-3 world). ### P2-08 Step-by-step hint engine (v76, 2026-11-11, Claude)
- What changed (CURRICULUM M-hints, ROADMAP P2-08): a generic `hint.k='steps'` renderer (`hintSteps` in `src/js/39-hints.js`) and step builders in the new `src/js/39b-steps.js`: `stepsAdd` (column addition with carries, 2+ addends, decimals lined up on the point), `stepsSub` (regrouping marks: crossed digit with the new value above, across zeros too; decimals), `stepsMul` ('pp' partial products, default up to 3x2 digits; 'std' standard algorithm with carries, placeholder zeros, then "add the rows"), `stepsDiv` (long division: divide, then multiply and subtract, bring down; remainder in the last step), `stepsEquivFrac`, `stepsCommonDen` ('+', '-', 'cmp'), `stepsStory` (text-only steps). A step is `{html, say, c}` (c records the computation for the tests). The last step never shows the answer: it asks the last small computation with a "?" ("Hundreds: 1 + 2 = ?", "25 − 24 = ? That is the remainder").
- Look (`src/css/39-steps.css`, tokens only, transform/opacity motion): inside the amber hint panel, a row of pips (done, current as a bar, still to come), a white work board with an amber ledge, then the caption with its place in bold. Digits used in this step sit in amber rings, new digits pop in brand blue, carries and regroup marks are small `--read` digits, crossed digits get a slash that draws in, the "?" is a dashed amber box, lines draw from the right. Long division has the divisor, a drawn bracket and the line over the dividend; fractions are stacked with "x3 ->" labels between them. A narrow board sits beside its caption; a wide one goes above it.
- Behaviour: one step per tap. The round's Help button reads "Next step" (arrow) while steps remain, then "Help" again on the last step; each tap shows the next step in place (no panel re-pop) and speaks it. The step reached is kept per question (`HSTEP`, a WeakMap), so a wrong answer or the mastery "hint before the first try" shows the same step. Fitting: when the card still overflows after a step is shown (phones, tall boards) the question folds for the rest of that question (`.qarea.hs-fold`): a stacked prompt goes onto one line ("159 + 206 = [ ]", the board shows the columns), rows tighten, older long-division rounds and (from the 5th part) a and b in partial products hide behind a thin line, the common-bottom board drops its first line. Measured: every step of 16 sample problems (up to 6-digit sums, 3 addends, 4-digit / 2-digit division, 3x2 partial products) fits the card at 380x780, 600x960 and 1024x600.
- Prompt layouts for P2-09..14: `ST.vert(nums, op, slot)` (stacked, decimal points lined up with hidden zeros, a hidden sign on middle rows for the folded line) and `ST.ldiv(a, b, slot)` (answer box on the bracket; pass `SLOT_R` with `sep:'R'`).
- Converted: Treasure Bay `big 5` (was a 3-item list with every step visible) now uses `stepsAdd(a, b)`. Nothing else rewritten.
- Hooks elsewhere: `src/js/50-round.js` Help button calls `hintStep(q)` first (one call); `src/js/31-gen-math-early.js` genBayBig5 hint. Docs: CURRICULUM M-hints (API as shipped), DESIGN_SYSTEM 4 hint row, CONTRIBUTING 3.2 file row.
- Tests: test.js 52 of 52; new check "step hints (P2-08)": 3,000 random problems each for addition (2 addends, 2-4 addends, decimals), subtraction (with zeros, decimals), partial products, standard multiplication, long division (1- and 2-digit divisors, to 4 digits), equivalent fractions (both directions), common bottoms: every column's digits and carry, every regroup (only when needed, from the right place), every product, quotient digit, product and remainder recomputed independently; the last step plus what is brought down gives the answer; every number of a step is on show; boards are whole grid rows; spoken text has no symbols or markup; panel markup, pips and step clamping; 39-steps.css tokens only, keyframes k-* on transform/opacity, no filter. Proved to fail on 5 injected bugs (no carry, wrong quotient digit, regroup +9, ceil carry, wrong renamed numerator). Treasure Bay check now accepts `k:'steps'`; big5 gives 3 steps for 200 seeds. Smoke: new `// P2-08` block (big5 in a real round plus 11 sample hints at all three sizes): every step one per tap, "Next step" then "Help", the last step asks, no card overflow, no sideways scroll, 48 px targets, a wrong answer keeps the step, a tap after the last step stays there. Smoke fix (test only): the P4-05 "tap the home pet -> cheer face" step now taps and reads in one evaluate; the face lasts 550 ms of app time (about 70 ms real at speed 8), and the old three round trips missed it on a cold page (measured: v75 1 in 24, this build 9 in 30 before the fix).
- Screenshots (`node tools/shots.js steps-add steps-add-end steps-dec steps-sub steps-sub2 steps-mul-pp steps-mul-pp-end steps-mul-std steps-mul-1 steps-div steps-div-end steps-div2 steps-equiv steps-cd steps-story`; group P2-08 in shots.js, helpers `stepShot`, `stepQ`); before: `tools/out/p208-before/bay-big5-*`. Looked at all 15 at 380x780 (montages) and steps-mul-pp-end, steps-div-end at 600x960 and 1024x600.
- Checklist 7.1: [x] chunk files (plus the two one-line hooks, tests, shots, docs, built output) [x] VER v76 (provisional) [x] build [x] test.js [x] smoke.js [x] screenshots at three sizes [x] midway fixture (smoke) [x] size (index.html about 1,000 KB, +39 KB) [x] no network [x] no new emoji or hex [x] PROGRESS entry [x] one commit (not pushed).
- Should switch to step hints next (not done here, per scope): Treasure Bay `sub 5` (`stepsSub`), `story 4` and `pv 2` (`BAY.steps` lists -> `stepsStory`, or make `BAY.steps` return `stepsStory(list)`); old `big 3/4` and `sub 4` hints once the "old levels unchanged" test allows hint-only changes; P2-07 Fraction Forest equivalence items (`stepsEquivFrac`); spelling hint 2 (show the word for 2 s) is not a step list and needs its own timed hint kind. P2-09..14: use the builders for every multi-digit skill (mbig 2-5, big 6-7, sub 8, dbig 1-4, dec 3, fop 1-4).
- Merge: built on branch P2-08-steps from v75 in parallel with P2-07, P2-20/22/24/25 and P4-18. Overlaps are small: 31-gen-math-early.js (genBayBig5 only), 50-round.js (one call in the Help handler), test.js/smoke.js/shots.js (appended blocks; the Treasure Bay `common` hint-kind line).
